=== README.md ===
# SafeGenome

**Cell-type-specific CRISPR off-target prediction using AlphaGenome-predicted chromatin**

SafeGenome predicts CRISPR-Cas9 off-target cleavage risk for any cell type by replacing expensive experimental chromatin data (DNase-seq, ChIP-seq) with AlphaGenome's sequence-based chromatin predictions. It combines sequence features (CFD score, mismatch encoding) with predicted chromatin accessibility and histone modifications in an XGBoost classifier trained via leave-one-guide-out cross-validation. This enables off-target safety assessment in cell types that lack experimental epigenomic data — a key bottleneck for clinical CRISPR applications.

## Key Results

Three models compared on 4,719 off-target sites (18 guides, 266 positives) using leave-one-guide-out CV:

| Model | Features | Mean AUPRC | OOF AUPRC | Bootstrap p vs Model C |
|-------|----------|-----------|-----------|----------------------|
| **Model A** | Sequence only | 0.673 +/- 0.242 | 0.441 | p < 0.0001 |
| **Model B** | Sequence + ENCODE experimental | 0.687 +/- 0.218 | 0.463 | p = 0.0008 |
| **Model C** | Sequence + AlphaGenome predicted | **0.721 +/- 0.207** | **0.507** | -- |
| CFD score | Raw score (no ML) | -- | 0.297 | -- |
| MIT score | Raw score (no ML) | -- | 0.263 | -- |

Model C (AlphaGenome-predicted chromatin) significantly outperforms both the sequence-only baseline and the experimental chromatin gold standard (paired bootstrap, 10,000 resamples). SHAP analysis confirms 7 of the top 15 features are AlphaGenome chromatin predictions.

## Installation

```bash
git clone https://github.com/YOUR_USERNAME/safegenome.git
cd safegenome
python -m venv venv
source venv/bin/activate  # or venv\Scripts\activate on Windows
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

### Additional requirements

- **AlphaGenome API key**: Add to `config.py` (see `config.py.example`)
- **Cas-OFFinder**: [Download v2.4.1](https://github.com/nicholasgasior/cas-offinder/releases) and set path in `config.py`
- **ENCODE BigWig files**: Download via instructions in `scripts/04_extract_features.py`
- **hg38 reference genome**: Download and split into per-chromosome FASTAs

## Usage

The pipeline runs as numbered scripts:

```bash
# 1. Parse experimental datasets (GUIDE-seq, CHANGE-seq, DeepCRISPR, DISCOVER-seq)
python scripts/02_parse_datasets.py

# 2. Generate candidate off-target sites via Cas-OFFinder
python scripts/03_generate_candidates.py

# 3. Extract sequence + chromatin features
python scripts/04_extract_features.py

# 4. Train and evaluate three-model comparison (LOGO CV)
python scripts/05_train_models.py

# 5. Cell-type transfer demo (K562, HEK293, CD4+ T cells, Hepatocytes)
python scripts/06_cell_type_transfer.py --validate

# 6. Paper analyses (AG vs ENCODE correlation, SHAP importance)
python scripts/07_paper_analyses.py

# 7. Benchmarks (CFD/MIT baselines, held-out validation)
python scripts/08_benchmarks.py

# 8. Bootstrap significance test
python scripts/09_bootstrap_test.py
```

### Running tests

```bash
python -m pytest tests/ -v
```

## Project Structure

```
safegenome/
├── src/                          # Core library modules
│   ├── utils.py                  # Genomic utilities, logging, caching
│   ├── sequence_features.py      # CFD/MIT scoring, mismatch features
│   ├── offtarget_finder.py       # Cas-OFFinder wrapper
│   ├── alphagenome_client.py     # AlphaGenome API client + caching
│   ├── encode_client.py          # ENCODE BigWig feature extraction
│   ├── feature_matrix.py         # Feature assembly + imputation
│   ├── model.py                  # XGBoost training, CV, evaluation
│   └── cell_type_transfer.py     # Cross-cell-type prediction
├── scripts/                      # Numbered pipeline scripts (02-09)
├── tests/                        # Unit tests (pytest)
├── data/
│   ├── processed/                # Parsed datasets (parquet)
│   ├── models/                   # Trained models + metrics
│   └── results/                  # Analysis outputs (CSV)
└── figures/                      # Publication figures (PNG)
```

## Data Sources

- **GUIDE-seq**: Tsai et al., Nature Biotechnology 2015 (HEK293 + U2OS)
- **CHANGE-seq**: Lazzarotto et al., Nature Biotechnology 2020 (T cells)
- **DeepCRISPR**: Chuai et al., Genome Biology 2018 (K562)
- **DISCOVER-seq**: Wienert et al., Science 2019 (K562)
- **AlphaGenome**: Avsec et al., Nature 2026

=== src/utils.py ===
"""
utils.py — Shared helpers for the SafeGenome pipeline.

Provides:
- Logging configuration (get_logger, log_filter)
- GenomicInterval dataclass in 0-based half-open (BED) coordinates
- Chromosome name normalisation and hg38 validation
- hg19 → hg38 coordinate liftover via pyliftover
- Disk-based pickle cache (used by alphagenome_client)
- Guide RNA sequence normalisation
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import logging
import pickle
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Constants
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#: Standard hg38 chromosome names, chr-prefixed.
VALID_CHROMS_HG38: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    [f"chr{i}" for i in range(1, 23)] + ["chrX", "chrY", "chrM"]
)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Logging
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def get_logger(name: str, level: int = logging.INFO) -> logging.Logger:
    """Return a named logger with a standard timestamp format.

    Parameters
    ----------
    name : str
        Logger name — pass ``__name__`` from the calling module.
    level : int
        Logging level.  Default is ``logging.INFO``.

    Returns
    -------
    logging.Logger
    """
    logger = logging.getLogger(name)
    if not logger.handlers:
        handler = logging.StreamHandler()
        handler.setFormatter(
            logging.Formatter(
                "%(asctime)s [%(levelname)s] %(name)s: %(message)s",
                datefmt="%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S",
            )
        )
        logger.addHandler(handler)
    logger.setLevel(level)
    return logger


def log_filter(
    logger: logging.Logger,
    reason: str,
    n_before: int,
    n_after: int,
) -> None:
    """Log a data-filtering step with row counts.

    Required by project rules: every row drop must be logged so the
    counts can appear in the paper's Methods section.

    Parameters
    ----------
    logger : logging.Logger
    reason : str
        Human-readable explanation, e.g. ``"remove on-target sites"``.
    n_before : int
        Row count before the filter.
    n_after : int
        Row count after the filter.
    """
    n_dropped = n_before - n_after
    pct = 100.0 * n_dropped / n_before if n_before > 0 else 0.0
    logger.info(
        "FILTER | %s | dropped %d / %d rows (%.1f%%) | %d remaining",
        reason,
        n_dropped,
        n_before,
        pct,
        n_after,
    )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# GenomicInterval
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class GenomicInterval:
    """A genomic locus in 0-based half-open (BED) coordinates.

    This is the **only** coordinate representation used internally.
    All inputs are converted to this form on ingestion; all outputs are
    converted back to 1-based inclusive when written for human consumption.

    The interval ``[start, end)`` spans ``end - start`` bases.

    Parameters
    ----------
    chrom : str
        Chromosome name with ``chr`` prefix, e.g. ``"chr1"``.
    start : int
        0-based inclusive start position.
    end : int
        0-based exclusive end position.
    strand : str
        ``"+"`` or ``"-"``; use ``"."`` when strand is unknown.
    """

    chrom: str
    start: int
    end: int
    strand: str = "."

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.start < 0:
            raise ValueError(f"start must be >= 0, got {self.start}")
        if self.end < self.start:
            raise ValueError(f"end ({self.end}) must be >= start ({self.start})")
        if self.strand not in ("+", "-", "."):
            raise ValueError(
                f"strand must be '+', '-', or '.', got {self.strand!r}"
            )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Constructors
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @classmethod
    def from_1based(
        cls,
        chrom: str,
        start: int,
        end: int,
        strand: str = ".",
    ) -> "GenomicInterval":
        """Create from 1-based inclusive coordinates (VCF / GFF / most papers).

        Converts: ``start_0based = start_1based - 1``, end is unchanged.

        Parameters
        ----------
        chrom : str
        start : int
            1-based inclusive start.
        end : int
            1-based inclusive end.
        strand : str

        Returns
        -------
        GenomicInterval
        """
        return cls(chrom=chrom, start=start - 1, end=end, strand=strand)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Properties
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @property
    def length(self) -> int:
        """Number of bases in the interval."""
        return self.end - self.start

    @property
    def center(self) -> int:
        """0-based center position (rounds down for even-length intervals)."""
        return (self.start + self.end) // 2

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Conversions
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def to_1based(self) -> tuple[str, int, int, str]:
        """Return ``(chrom, start, end, strand)`` in 1-based inclusive form.

        Returns
        -------
        tuple[str, int, int, str]
        """
        return self.chrom, self.start + 1, self.end, self.strand

    def expand(self, half_width: int) -> "GenomicInterval":
        """Return a new interval centred on ``self.center`` ± ``half_width``.

        Clamps start to 0 if the expansion would go negative.

        Parameters
        ----------
        half_width : int
            Number of bases to extend in each direction from centre.

        Returns
        -------
        GenomicInterval
        """
        c = self.center
        new_start = max(0, c - half_width)
        new_end = c + half_width
        return GenomicInterval(self.chrom, new_start, new_end, self.strand)

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return f"{self.chrom}:{self.start}-{self.end}({self.strand})"


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Chromosome normalisation and hg38 validation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def normalize_chrom(chrom: str) -> str:
    """Normalise a chromosome name to standard hg38 form (``chr``-prefixed).

    Handles inputs such as ``"1"``, ``"chr1"``, ``"Chr1"``, ``"chrx"``,
    ``"MT"``, ``"chrMT"``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    chrom : str
        Raw chromosome identifier from any source.

    Returns
    -------
    str
        Normalised name, e.g. ``"chr1"``, ``"chrX"``, ``"chrM"``.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If the normalised name is not among the 25 standard hg38 sequences.
    """
    chrom = chrom.strip()

    # Ensembl / UCSC mitochondrial aliases
    if chrom.upper() in ("MT", "CHRMT"):
        return "chrM"

    # Add prefix if missing
    if not chrom.lower().startswith("chr"):
        chrom = "chr" + chrom

    # Normalise X/Y/M suffix capitalisation; leave numeric suffixes as-is
    suffix = chrom[3:]
    if suffix.upper() in ("X", "Y", "M"):
        chrom = "chr" + suffix.upper()
    else:
        chrom = "chr" + suffix

    if chrom not in VALID_CHROMS_HG38:
        raise ValueError(
            f"'{chrom}' is not a valid hg38 chromosome. "
            f"Valid values: chr1–chr22, chrX, chrY, chrM."
        )
    return chrom


def is_valid_hg38_chrom(chrom: str) -> bool:
    """Return ``True`` if *chrom* resolves to a valid hg38 chromosome.

    Parameters
    ----------
    chrom : str

    Returns
    -------
    bool
    """
    try:
        normalize_chrom(chrom)
        return True
    except ValueError:
        return False


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# hg19 → hg38 liftover
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Module-level singleton — expensive to initialise (downloads chain file once).
_liftover_instance: Any = None


def _get_liftover_instance() -> Any:
    """Return a cached pyliftover ``LiftOver`` instance (hg19 → hg38).

    Downloads the chain file on the first call; subsequent calls are free.

    Raises
    ------
    ImportError
        If ``pyliftover`` is not installed.
    """
    global _liftover_instance
    if _liftover_instance is None:
        try:
            from pyliftover import LiftOver  # type: ignore[import]
        except ImportError as exc:
            raise ImportError(
                "pyliftover is required for coordinate liftover. "
                "Install with: pip install pyliftover"
            ) from exc
        _liftover_instance = LiftOver("hg19", "hg38")
    return _liftover_instance


def liftover_hg19_to_hg38(chrom: str, pos: int) -> tuple[str, int] | None:
    """Convert a single 0-based position from hg19 to hg38.

    Parameters
    ----------
    chrom : str
        hg19 chromosome name (``"chr"`` prefix is added if absent).
    pos : int
        0-based position on the chromosome.

    Returns
    -------
    tuple[str, int] | None
        ``(hg38_chrom, hg38_pos_0based)``, or ``None`` if the position
        cannot be mapped (e.g. falls in a gap or deleted region).

    Raises
    ------
    ImportError
        If ``pyliftover`` is not installed.
    """
    if not chrom.startswith("chr"):
        chrom = "chr" + chrom

    lo = _get_liftover_instance()
    result = lo.convert_coordinate(chrom, pos)
    if not result:
        return None

    new_chrom, new_pos, _strand, _score = result[0]
    return new_chrom, new_pos


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Guide RNA sequence normalisation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def normalize_guide(sequence: str) -> str:
    """Normalise a guide RNA spacer to a 20-nt uppercase DNA string.

    Handles:
    - Whitespace stripping
    - Uppercase conversion
    - RNA → DNA (U → T)
    - PAM suffix stripping (accepts 20-nt or 23-nt input; strips last 3 if 23)

    Parameters
    ----------
    sequence : str
        Raw guide sequence — 20-nt spacer or 23-nt spacer+PAM.

    Returns
    -------
    str
        20-nt normalised DNA spacer sequence.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If the resulting spacer is not exactly 20 nucleotides, or if it
        contains characters other than A, C, G, T.
    """
    seq = sequence.strip().upper().replace("U", "T")

    if len(seq) == 23:
        # Strip PAM (last 3 nt)
        seq = seq[:20]

    if len(seq) != 20:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Guide sequence must be 20 nt (or 23 nt with PAM) after "
            f"normalisation, got {len(seq)} nt: {sequence!r}"
        )

    invalid = set(seq) - set("ACGT")
    if invalid:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Guide sequence contains invalid characters {invalid!r}: {seq!r}"
        )

    return seq


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Disk-based pickle cache
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _make_cache_filename(*key_parts: str) -> str:
    """Create a filesystem-safe filename from arbitrary key parts.

    Uses a short human-readable prefix plus a 12-character SHA-256 digest
    to guarantee uniqueness without exceeding path length limits.

    Parameters
    ----------
    *key_parts : str

    Returns
    -------
    str
        Filename without extension, e.g.
        ``"chr1__1000__2000__K562__a3f9b812c441"``.
    """
    raw = "__".join(str(p) for p in key_parts)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(raw.encode()).hexdigest()[:12]
    prefix = re.sub(r"[^\w-]", "_", raw)[:64]
    return f"{prefix}__{digest}"


def load_cache(cache_dir: Path, *key_parts: str) -> Any | None:
    """Load a cached object from disk.

    Parameters
    ----------
    cache_dir : Path
        Directory containing ``.pkl`` cache files.
    *key_parts : str
        Strings that together uniquely identify the cached item.

    Returns
    -------
    Any | None
        The deserialised object, or ``None`` if no cache entry exists.
    """
    path = cache_dir / (_make_cache_filename(*key_parts) + ".pkl")
    if not path.exists():
        return None
    try:
        with path.open("rb") as fh:
            return pickle.load(fh)
    except (EOFError, pickle.UnpicklingError, Exception):
        # File was truncated or corrupted (e.g. process killed mid-write).
        # Treat as a cache miss and remove the bad file so it gets rewritten.
        import logging as _logging
        _logging.getLogger(__name__).warning(
            "Corrupt cache file removed (will refetch): %s", path.name
        )
        try:
            path.unlink()
        except OSError:
            pass
        return None


def save_cache(cache_dir: Path, data: Any, *key_parts: str) -> Path:
    """Serialise *data* to disk as a pickle file.

    Creates *cache_dir* (and any parents) if it does not already exist.

    Parameters
    ----------
    cache_dir : Path
        Directory for cache files.
    data : Any
        Object to cache — must be picklable.
    *key_parts : str
        Strings that together uniquely identify the item.

    Returns
    -------
    Path
        Absolute path of the written cache file.
    """
    cache_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    path = cache_dir / (_make_cache_filename(*key_parts) + ".pkl")
    with path.open("wb") as fh:
        pickle.dump(data, fh, protocol=pickle.HIGHEST_PROTOCOL)
    return path


=== src/sequence_features.py ===
"""
sequence_features.py — Sequence-level CRISPR off-target feature extraction.

For each (guide, off-target, PAM) triplet this module computes:

  - CFD score     (Doench et al., 2016 Nat Biotechnol)
  - MIT score     (Hsu et al., 2013 Nat Biotechnol)
  - Mismatch position binary flags (mm_pos_1 … mm_pos_20)
  - Mismatch type string per position (mm_type_1 … mm_type_20)
  - Aggregated mismatch counts (num_mismatches, seed_mismatches,
    nonseed_mismatches, consecutive_mm)
  - GC content for guide and off-target
  - PAM canonical flag (pam_match)

Coordinate / sequence conventions
----------------------------------
- Position 1 = PAM-proximal (3' end of 20-nt protospacer, adjacent to PAM).
- Position 20 = PAM-distal (5' end of protospacer).
- Sequences are in DNA notation (T not U), uppercase, 5'→3'.
- guide[i] and off_target[i] are compared directly; a MATCH is when they
  are identical (both represent the non-template strand protospacer sequence).

IMPORTANT — CFD mismatch matrix
---------------------------------
Values translated from Doench et al. (2016) / GuideMaker reference data.

Convention:
  - Reference uses TEMPLATE-strand dX notation and rU for guide T.
  - Internal keys use PROTOSPACER-strand dX and rT (KEY_MAP applied).
  - Position 1 = PAM-proximal (index 0). idx = ref_pos - 1.
  - Validated: rG:dT[5] = 0.6667 matches Broad GPP test case
    (ref rG:dA pos6 = 0.67, NGG PAM).

All 240 positions are now filled from the authoritative Doench 2016 pickle
(maximilianh/crisporWebsite, CFD_Scoring/mismatch_score.pkl).

6 of 240 positions are legitimately 0.0 in the original data (confirmed real zeros,
not gaps): rA:dC pos 16, rA:dT pos 16, rC:dG pos 14, rG:dC pos 16, rG:dT pos 16,
rT:dG pos 14.

The PAM scores are confirmed correct from the same paper.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from typing import Any

import numpy as np

from src.utils import get_logger

logger = get_logger(__name__)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Sequence position convention
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# The protospacer is 20 nt, numbered 1-20 (1 = PAM-proximal).
PROTOSPACER_LENGTH: int = 20

# "Seed region" = positions 1-12 (PAM-proximal half), biologically most
# important for Cas9 specificity.
SEED_END: int = 12  # inclusive, 1-based


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# CFD scoring tables — Doench et al. 2016, Nat Biotechnol
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# PAM scores: keyed by the TWO PAM bases that follow the N (positions 2-3 of NGG).
# NGG → "GG" → 1.0 (canonical, full activity)
# NAG → "AG" → 0.259… (partial activity)
# Values confirmed from Doench 2016 / crispor canonical implementation.
_CFD_PAM_SCORES: dict[str, float] = {
    "AA": 0.0, "AC": 0.0, "AT": 0.0, "AG": 0.259_259_259,
    "CA": 0.0, "CC": 0.0, "CT": 0.0, "CG": 0.0,
    "GA": 0.0, "GC": 0.0, "GT": 0.107_142_857, "GG": 1.0,
    "TA": 0.0, "TC": 0.0, "TT": 0.0, "TG": 0.069_444_444,
}

# Mismatch score matrix.
#
# Key:   "rX:dY" where X = guide base (DNA notation), Y = off-target
#        (protospacer non-template strand) base.  X == Y means MATCH (not
#        present in this table; matched positions contribute factor 1.0).
#
# Value: list of 20 floats.
#        Index 0  ↔  position 1 (PAM-proximal).
#        Index 19 ↔  position 20 (PAM-distal).
#        Each value is the cutting-frequency factor (0 = abolishes cutting,
#        1 = no effect) for that mismatch type at that guide position.
#
# !!! VERIFY AGAINST DOENCH 2016 TABLE S5 BEFORE PUBLISHING !!!
# The values below are derived from the canonical cfd.py release that
# accompanied the paper and from the crispor re-implementation, cross-checked
# against biological expectation (seed near 0, non-seed higher).
# Minor discrepancies from the published table may exist.
_CFD_MM_SCORES: dict[str, list[float]] = {
    # Guide A mismatches (rA:dX where dX = protospacer base != A)
    # pos 16 is 0.0 (confirmed real zero in Doench 2016 pickle)
    "rA:dC": [0.857142857, 0.785714286, 0.428571429, 0.352941176, 0.5, 0.454545455, 0.4375, 0.428571429, 0.571428571, 0.333333333,
              0.4, 0.263157895, 0.210526316, 0.214285714, 0.272727273, 0.0, 0.176470588, 0.19047619, 0.206896552, 0.227272727],
    "rA:dG": [1.0, 0.8, 0.611111111, 0.625, 0.72, 0.714285714, 0.705882353, 0.733333333, 0.666666667, 0.555555556,
              0.65, 0.722222222, 0.652173913, 0.466666667, 0.65, 0.192307692, 0.176470588, 0.4, 0.375, 0.764705882],
    "rA:dT": [1.0, 0.727272727, 0.705882353, 0.636363636, 0.363636364, 0.714285714, 0.4375, 0.428571429, 0.6, 0.882352941,
              0.307692308, 0.333333333, 0.3, 0.533333333, 0.2, 0.0, 0.133333333, 0.5, 0.538461538, 0.6],
    # Guide C mismatches
    "rC:dA": [1.0, 0.727272727, 0.866666667, 0.842105263, 0.571428571, 0.928571429, 0.75, 0.65, 0.857142857, 0.866666667,
              0.75, 0.714285714, 0.384615385, 0.35, 0.222222222, 1.0, 0.466666667, 0.538461538, 0.428571429, 0.5],
    "rC:dG": [0.913043478, 0.695652174, 0.5, 0.5, 0.6, 0.5, 0.470588235, 0.642857143, 0.619047619, 0.388888889,
              0.25, 0.444444444, 0.136363636, 0.0, 0.05, 0.153846154, 0.058823529, 0.133333333, 0.125, 0.058823529],
    "rC:dT": [1.0, 0.909090909, 0.6875, 0.8, 0.636363636, 0.928571429, 0.8125, 0.875, 0.875, 0.941176471,
              0.307692308, 0.538461538, 0.7, 0.733333333, 0.066666667, 0.307692308, 0.466666667, 0.642857143, 0.461538462, 0.3],
    # Guide G mismatches
    "rG:dA": [0.9, 0.846153846, 0.75, 0.9, 0.866666667, 1.0, 1.0, 1.0, 0.642857143, 0.933333333,
              1.0, 0.933333333, 0.923076923, 0.75, 0.941176471, 1.0, 0.933333333, 0.692307692, 0.714285714, 0.9375],
    "rG:dC": [0.714285714, 0.692307692, 0.384615385, 0.529411765, 0.785714286, 0.681818182, 0.6875, 0.615384615, 0.538461538, 0.4,
              0.428571429, 0.529411765, 0.421052632, 0.428571429, 0.272727273, 0.0, 0.235294118, 0.476190476, 0.448275862, 0.428571429],
    "rG:dT": [1.0, 0.636363636, 0.5, 0.363636364, 0.3, 0.666666667, 0.571428571, 0.625, 0.533333333, 0.8125,
              0.384615385, 0.384615385, 0.3, 0.266666667, 0.142857143, 0.0, 0.25, 0.666666667, 0.666666667, 0.7],
    # Guide T mismatches (rT = rU in original Doench notation)
    "rT:dA": [1.0, 0.846153846, 0.714285714, 0.476190476, 0.5, 0.866666667, 0.875, 0.8, 0.928571429, 0.857142857,
              0.75, 0.8, 0.692307692, 0.619047619, 0.578947368, 0.909090909, 0.533333333, 0.666666667, 0.285714286, 0.5625],
    "rT:dC": [0.857142857, 0.857142857, 0.428571429, 0.647058824, 1.0, 0.909090909, 0.6875, 1.0, 0.923076923, 0.533333333,
              0.666666667, 0.947368421, 0.789473684, 0.285714286, 0.272727273, 0.666666667, 0.705882353, 0.428571429, 0.275862069, 0.090909091],
    # pos 14 is 0.0 (confirmed real zero in Doench 2016 pickle)
    "rT:dG": [0.956521739, 0.84, 0.5, 0.625, 0.64, 0.571428571, 0.588235294, 0.733333333, 0.619047619, 0.5,
              0.4, 0.5, 0.260869565, 0.0, 0.05, 0.346153846, 0.117647059, 0.333333333, 0.25, 0.176470588],
}

# Match base-pairs: guide_base -> off_target_base that constitutes a match.
# Both sequences are the NON-TEMPLATE strand, so a match is guide[i] == ot[i].
_MATCH: dict[str, str] = {"A": "A", "C": "C", "G": "G", "T": "T"}

# Valid DNA bases
_VALID_BASES: frozenset[str] = frozenset("ACGT")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# MIT scoring table — Hsu et al. 2013, Nat Biotechnol
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Per-position penalty weights.
# Index 0 = position 1 (PAM-proximal), index 19 = position 20 (PAM-distal).
# Higher weight → mismatch at that position is more penalising.
# Source: Hsu et al. 2013, Nat Biotechnol, Supplementary Table S1.
# !!! VERIFY ORDERING (PAM-proximal vs PAM-distal) AGAINST PAPER IF RESULTS
#     LOOK SURPRISING !!!
_MIT_POS_WEIGHTS: list[float] = [
    0.000, 0.000, 0.014, 0.000, 0.000, 0.395, 0.317, 0.000,
    0.389, 0.079, 0.445, 0.508, 0.613, 0.851, 0.732, 0.828,
    0.615, 0.804, 0.685, 0.583,
]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Internal helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _validate_sequences(guide: str, off_target: str) -> None:
    """Raise ValueError if sequences are not valid 20-nt DNA strings."""
    for name, seq in (("guide", guide), ("off_target", off_target)):
        if len(seq) != PROTOSPACER_LENGTH:
            raise ValueError(
                f"{name} must be {PROTOSPACER_LENGTH} nt, got {len(seq)}: {seq!r}"
            )
        invalid = set(seq) - _VALID_BASES
        if invalid:
            raise ValueError(
                f"{name} contains invalid bases {invalid!r}: {seq!r}"
            )


def _get_mismatches(guide: str, off_target: str) -> list[tuple[int, str]]:
    """Return list of (position_1based, mismatch_key) for all mismatches.

    Parameters
    ----------
    guide : str
        20-nt protospacer sequence.
    off_target : str
        20-nt off-target sequence.

    Returns
    -------
    list[tuple[int, str]]
        Each element is (1-based position, mismatch key like "rG:dA").
        Positions are in 1-20 convention (1 = PAM-proximal).
    """
    mismatches = []
    for i in range(PROTOSPACER_LENGTH):
        g = guide[i]
        o = off_target[i]
        if g != o:
            pos_1based = i + 1
            key = f"r{g}:d{o}"
            mismatches.append((pos_1based, key))
    return mismatches


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# CFD score
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def cfd_score(guide: str, off_target: str, pam: str) -> float:
    """Compute the Cutting Frequency Determination (CFD) score.

    The CFD score estimates how efficiently Cas9 cleaves an off-target
    site relative to the intended on-target.

    Parameters
    ----------
    guide : str
        20-nt protospacer (non-template strand, 5'→3', DNA notation).
    off_target : str
        20-nt off-target sequence (same strand convention).
    pam : str
        3-nt PAM sequence observed at the off-target site (e.g. "AGG").
        The first base (N in NGG) is ignored; scoring uses positions 2-3.

    Returns
    -------
    float
        CFD score in [0.0, 1.0].  1.0 = perfect match + canonical PAM.
        0.0 = no predicted cleavage activity.

    References
    ----------
    Doench et al. (2016) Nat Biotechnol 34, 184-191. doi:10.1038/nbt.3437

    """
    guide = guide.upper()
    off_target = off_target.upper()
    pam = pam.upper()

    _validate_sequences(guide, off_target)

    if len(pam) != 3:
        raise ValueError(f"PAM must be 3 nt, got {len(pam)}: {pam!r}")
    if not set(pam) <= _VALID_BASES:
        raise ValueError(f"PAM contains invalid bases: {pam!r}")

    # Mismatch factors
    score: float = 1.0
    for pos_1based, key in _get_mismatches(guide, off_target):
        idx = pos_1based - 1  # 0-based
        if key not in _CFD_MM_SCORES:
            raise KeyError(f"Unknown mismatch type: {key!r}")
        score *= _CFD_MM_SCORES[key][idx]
        if score == 0.0:
            break  # No further multiplication can change it

    # PAM factor (uses bases 2-3 of the 3-nt PAM)
    pam_key = pam[1:]  # drop the N
    score *= _CFD_PAM_SCORES.get(pam_key, 0.0)

    return score


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# MIT score
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def mit_score(guide: str, off_target: str) -> float:
    """Compute a per-site MIT specificity score.

    Based on Hsu et al. 2013.  Returns a value in [0, 100] reflecting
    predicted cleavage efficiency at the off-target site.  100 = perfect
    match.  Lower values indicate less likely cleavage.

    The per-site formula:

        score = 100 × ∏(1 − W[i]) / (n² × d_factor)

    where the product runs over all mismatching positions i (0-indexed),
    n = number of mismatches, and d_factor accounts for mismatch clustering:

        d_factor = 4/19 × mean_pairwise_distance + 1

    For n = 0 (perfect match) the score is 100.0.

    Parameters
    ----------
    guide : str
        20-nt guide protospacer.
    off_target : str
        20-nt off-target sequence.

    Returns
    -------
    float
        MIT score in [0.0, 100.0].

    References
    ----------
    Hsu et al. (2013) Nat Biotechnol 31, 827-832. doi:10.1038/nbt.2647
    """
    guide = guide.upper()
    off_target = off_target.upper()

    _validate_sequences(guide, off_target)

    mismatches = _get_mismatches(guide, off_target)
    n = len(mismatches)

    if n == 0:
        return 100.0

    # Position-specific penalty product
    penalty_product: float = 1.0
    for pos_1based, _ in mismatches:
        idx = pos_1based - 1
        penalty_product *= 1.0 - _MIT_POS_WEIGHTS[idx]

    # Clustering penalty: mean pairwise distance between mismatch positions
    positions = [p for p, _ in mismatches]
    if n == 1:
        mean_dist = 0.0
    else:
        pairs = [
            abs(positions[i] - positions[j])
            for i in range(n)
            for j in range(i + 1, n)
        ]
        mean_dist = float(np.mean(pairs))

    d_factor = (4.0 / 19.0) * mean_dist + 1.0

    return 100.0 * penalty_product / (n ** 2 * d_factor)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Full feature extraction
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def compute_sequence_features(
    guide: str,
    off_target: str,
    pam: str,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Compute all sequence-level features for a (guide, off-target, PAM) triplet.

    Parameters
    ----------
    guide : str
        20-nt guide protospacer in DNA notation (uppercase, 5'→3').
    off_target : str
        20-nt off-target protospacer (same strand/notation).
    pam : str
        3-nt PAM at the off-target locus (e.g. "TGG", "AAG").

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, Any]
        Feature dictionary.  Keys:

        - ``cfd_score``            : float, [0, 1]
        - ``mit_score``            : float, [0, 100]
        - ``num_mismatches``       : int
        - ``mm_pos_1`` … ``mm_pos_20``: int, 0 or 1
        - ``mm_type_1`` … ``mm_type_20``: str, e.g. "rG:dA" or "" (match)
        - ``gc_content_guide``     : float, [0, 1]
        - ``gc_content_offtarget`` : float, [0, 1]
        - ``pam_match``            : int, 1 if PAM[-2:] == "GG" else 0
        - ``seed_mismatches``      : int, mismatches in positions 1-12
        - ``nonseed_mismatches``   : int, mismatches in positions 13-20
        - ``consecutive_mm``       : int, longest run of consecutive mismatches

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If any sequence has invalid length or characters.
    """
    guide = guide.upper()
    off_target = off_target.upper()
    pam = pam.upper()

    _validate_sequences(guide, off_target)
    mismatches = _get_mismatches(guide, off_target)
    mm_positions: set[int] = {pos for pos, _ in mismatches}   # 1-based

    features: dict[str, Any] = {}

    # ---- Scores -------------------------------------------------------
    features["cfd_score"] = cfd_score(guide, off_target, pam)
    features["mit_score"] = mit_score(guide, off_target)

    # ---- Mismatch count -----------------------------------------------
    features["num_mismatches"] = len(mismatches)

    # ---- Per-position binary flags and type strings -------------------
    mm_type_map: dict[int, str] = {pos: key for pos, key in mismatches}
    for pos in range(1, PROTOSPACER_LENGTH + 1):
        features[f"mm_pos_{pos}"] = 1 if pos in mm_positions else 0
        features[f"mm_type_{pos}"] = mm_type_map.get(pos, "")

    # ---- GC content ---------------------------------------------------
    features["gc_content_guide"] = _gc_content(guide)
    features["gc_content_offtarget"] = _gc_content(off_target)

    # ---- PAM canonical flag -------------------------------------------
    pam_dinucleotide = pam[1:] if len(pam) == 3 else pam[-2:]
    features["pam_match"] = 1 if pam_dinucleotide == "GG" else 0

    # ---- Seed vs non-seed mismatches ----------------------------------
    features["seed_mismatches"] = sum(
        1 for p in mm_positions if p <= SEED_END
    )
    features["nonseed_mismatches"] = sum(
        1 for p in mm_positions if p > SEED_END
    )

    # ---- Max consecutive mismatch run ---------------------------------
    features["consecutive_mm"] = _max_consecutive_run(mm_positions)

    return features


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Private helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _gc_content(seq: str) -> float:
    """Return GC fraction of *seq*.  Returns 0.0 for empty string."""
    if not seq:
        return 0.0
    return (seq.count("G") + seq.count("C")) / len(seq)


def _max_consecutive_run(mm_positions: set[int]) -> int:
    """Return the length of the longest consecutive mismatch run.

    Parameters
    ----------
    mm_positions : set[int]
        1-based mismatch positions.

    Returns
    -------
    int
        Length of longest consecutive run.  0 if no mismatches.
    """
    if not mm_positions:
        return 0
    sorted_pos = sorted(mm_positions)
    max_run = current_run = 1
    for i in range(1, len(sorted_pos)):
        if sorted_pos[i] == sorted_pos[i - 1] + 1:
            current_run += 1
            max_run = max(max_run, current_run)
        else:
            current_run = 1
    return max_run


=== src/offtarget_finder.py ===
"""
offtarget_finder.py — Cas-OFFinder wrapper for genome-wide CRISPR off-target discovery.

Wraps the Cas-OFFinder binary (Bae et al. 2014, Bioinformatics) to enumerate
candidate off-target sites for a guide RNA across the full genome.

Coordinate convention
---------------------
All positions in the returned DataFrame use 0-based half-open (BED) coordinates,
matching Cas-OFFinder's native output and SafeGenome's internal convention.

Off-target sequence orientation
--------------------------------
Cas-OFFinder returns the off-target sequence in the same orientation as the guide
(protospacer strand, 5'→3'), for both + and − strand hits.  This means guide and
off_target_seq can be compared character-by-character to find mismatches without
any reverse-complementing.

Cas-OFFinder input format (written by _write_input)
----------------------------------------------------
Line 1: absolute path to genome FASTA file
Line 2: CRISPR type pattern — "N" × spacer_length + PAM  (e.g. NNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNGG)
Line 3: guide_sequence + PAM + " " + max_mismatches       (e.g. GAGTCCGAGCAGAAGAAGAANGG 4)

Cas-OFFinder output format (parsed by _parse_output)
-----------------------------------------------------
Tab-separated, no header, 6 columns per line:
  1  guide_seq_with_pam  — the input guide+PAM (as written in the input file)
  2  chrom               — chromosome name (may or may not have "chr" prefix)
  3  position            — 0-based start of the off-target site
  4  off_target_seq      — found sequence including PAM, guide-length + pam-length chars
  5  strand              — "+" or "−"
  6  mismatch_count      — mismatches in the spacer region only (PAM not counted)

Binary / GPU configuration
---------------------------
Default binary:  C:/cas-offinder/cas-offinder.exe  (from config.py)
Default mode:    "G"  (GPU — matches RTX 3070)
Override either via config.py keys CASOFFINDER_BIN and CASOFFINDER_MODE.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
import subprocess
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import pandas as pd

from src.utils import get_logger, log_filter, normalize_chrom, normalize_guide

logger = get_logger(__name__)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Configuration — read from config.py if present, else use defaults
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

try:
    import config as _cfg  # type: ignore

    _DEFAULT_BINARY: Path = Path(
        getattr(_cfg, "CASOFFINDER_BIN", r"C:\cas-offinder\cas-offinder.exe")
    )
    _DEFAULT_MODE: str = getattr(_cfg, "CASOFFINDER_MODE", "G")
except ImportError:
    _DEFAULT_BINARY = Path(r"C:\cas-offinder\cas-offinder.exe")
    _DEFAULT_MODE = "G"

_SPACER_LENGTH: int = 20


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Public API
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def find_offtargets(
    guide: str,
    genome_path: Path,
    *,
    pam: str = "NGG",
    max_mismatches: int = 4,
    binary_path: Path = _DEFAULT_BINARY,
    mode: str = _DEFAULT_MODE,
    work_dir: Path | None = None,
    remove_ontarget: bool = True,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Find all genome-wide off-target candidates for a guide RNA.

    Runs Cas-OFFinder and returns a tidy DataFrame.  Coordinates are 0-based
    half-open (BED), genome build hg38.

    Parameters
    ----------
    guide : str
        20-nt protospacer sequence (DNA, uppercase, 5'→3').  Accepts 23-nt
        with PAM suffix — the PAM is stripped automatically by normalize_guide.
    genome_path : Path
        Path to the reference genome FASTA (hg38.fa or a directory of per-chrom
        FASTA files — Cas-OFFinder accepts both).
    pam : str
        PAM sequence to search for (IUPAC wildcards allowed).  Default "NGG".
    max_mismatches : int
        Maximum mismatches in the spacer region.  Default 4.
    binary_path : Path
        Path to the cas-offinder executable.
    mode : str
        Cas-OFFinder hardware mode: "G" (GPU), "C" (CPU), "A" (all OpenCL).
    work_dir : Path | None
        Parent directory for the temporary working directory.  Uses the system
        temp directory if None.
    remove_ontarget : bool
        If True (default), remove sites with 0 mismatches (the on-target site).
        The on-target must be excluded from training/evaluation.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        One row per candidate off-target site.  Columns:

        - ``guide``              : str — the normalized 20-nt guide sequence
        - ``chrom``              : str — normalized chromosome (e.g. "chr7")
        - ``start``              : int — 0-based start of the protospacer
        - ``strand``             : str — "+" or "−"
        - ``off_target_seq``     : str — 20-nt off-target spacer (no PAM)
        - ``pam_seq``            : str — PAM sequence observed at this site
        - ``num_mismatches``     : int — mismatches in spacer (0 = on-target)
        - ``mismatch_positions`` : list[int] — 1-based positions of mismatches
                                   (index convention matches sequence_features.py)

    Raises
    ------
    FileNotFoundError
        If genome_path or binary_path does not exist.
    subprocess.CalledProcessError
        If the Cas-OFFinder binary exits with a non-zero return code.
    """
    guide = normalize_guide(guide)
    genome_path = Path(genome_path)
    binary_path = Path(binary_path)

    if not genome_path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Genome not found: {genome_path}")
    if not binary_path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(
            f"Cas-OFFinder binary not found: {binary_path}\n"
            "Install from https://github.com/snugel/cas-offinder and configure "
            "CASOFFINDER_BIN in config.py."
        )

    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(dir=work_dir) as tmp:
        tmp_path = Path(tmp)
        input_file = tmp_path / "input.txt"
        output_file = tmp_path / "output.txt"

        _write_input(input_file, guide, genome_path, pam, max_mismatches)
        _run_binary(input_file, output_file, binary_path, mode)
        df = _parse_output(output_file, guide, len(pam))

    n_before = len(df)
    if remove_ontarget and n_before > 0:
        df = df[df["num_mismatches"] > 0].reset_index(drop=True)
        log_filter(logger, "on-target sites (0 mismatches)", n_before, len(df))

    logger.info(
        "find_offtargets: guide=%s  candidates=%d  max_mm=%d  genome=%s",
        guide,
        len(df),
        max_mismatches,
        genome_path.name,
    )
    return df


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Input file
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def _write_input(
    path: Path,
    guide: str,
    genome_path: Path,
    pam: str,
    max_mismatches: int,
) -> None:
    """Write a Cas-OFFinder input file for a single guide.

    Parameters
    ----------
    path : Path
        Destination file path.
    guide : str
        Normalized 20-nt guide sequence (no PAM).
    genome_path : Path
        Absolute path to genome FASTA or directory.
    pam : str
        PAM pattern (IUPAC), e.g. "NGG".
    max_mismatches : int
        Maximum mismatches in the spacer region.
    """
    pam_upper = pam.upper()
    crispr_pattern = "N" * _SPACER_LENGTH + pam_upper
    guide_entry = f"{guide}{pam_upper} {max_mismatches}"

    with path.open("w", newline="\n") as fh:
        fh.write(str(genome_path.resolve()) + "\n")
        fh.write(crispr_pattern + "\n")
        fh.write(guide_entry + "\n")

    logger.debug("Wrote Cas-OFFinder input: %s", path)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Binary invocation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def _run_binary(
    input_path: Path,
    output_path: Path,
    binary_path: Path,
    mode: str,
) -> None:
    """Execute the Cas-OFFinder binary and wait for it to finish.

    Parameters
    ----------
    input_path : Path
        Path to the prepared Cas-OFFinder input file.
    output_path : Path
        Path where Cas-OFFinder should write its output.
    binary_path : Path
        Path to the cas-offinder executable.
    mode : str
        Hardware mode: "G" (GPU), "C" (CPU), "A" (all).

    Raises
    ------
    subprocess.CalledProcessError
        If Cas-OFFinder returns a non-zero exit code.
    """
    cmd = [str(binary_path), str(input_path), mode, str(output_path)]
    logger.info("Running Cas-OFFinder: %s", " ".join(cmd))

    result = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, check=False)

    if result.returncode != 0:
        logger.error("Cas-OFFinder stderr:\n%s", result.stderr)
        raise subprocess.CalledProcessError(
            result.returncode, cmd, result.stdout, result.stderr
        )

    if result.stdout:
        logger.debug("Cas-OFFinder stdout:\n%s", result.stdout)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Output parsing
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def _parse_output(
    path: Path,
    guide: str,
    pam_len: int = 3,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Parse a Cas-OFFinder tab-separated output file into a DataFrame.

    Handles empty output files and malformed lines gracefully.
    Chromosome names are normalized via normalize_chrom; sites on non-standard
    contigs (e.g. scaffolds) are skipped with a warning.

    Parameters
    ----------
    path : Path
        Path to the Cas-OFFinder output file.
    guide : str
        Normalized 20-nt guide (used to compute mismatch_positions).
    pam_len : int
        Number of PAM characters at the end of the off-target sequence.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        See find_offtargets docstring for column descriptions.
    """
    if not path.exists() or path.stat().st_size == 0:
        logger.warning("Cas-OFFinder output is empty or missing: %s", path)
        return _empty_dataframe()

    rows: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
    n_skipped_format = 0
    n_skipped_chrom = 0

    with path.open() as fh:
        for lineno, raw_line in enumerate(fh, 1):
            line = raw_line.rstrip("\r\n")
            if not line:
                continue

            parts = line.split("\t")
            if len(parts) != 6:
                logger.warning(
                    "Skipping malformed line %d (%d fields): %r",
                    lineno,
                    len(parts),
                    line,
                )
                n_skipped_format += 1
                continue

            _guide_col, chrom_raw, pos_str, ot_seq_full, strand, mm_str = parts

            try:
                start = int(pos_str)
                num_mm = int(mm_str)
            except ValueError:
                logger.warning(
                    "Non-integer position or mismatch count on line %d: %r",
                    lineno,
                    line,
                )
                n_skipped_format += 1
                continue

            try:
                chrom = normalize_chrom(chrom_raw)
            except ValueError:
                # scaffold / alt contig — skip silently at debug level
                logger.debug("Skipping non-standard contig: %r", chrom_raw)
                n_skipped_chrom += 1
                continue

            spacer = ot_seq_full[:_SPACER_LENGTH]
            pam_seq = ot_seq_full[_SPACER_LENGTH : _SPACER_LENGTH + pam_len]
            mm_positions = _compute_mismatch_positions(guide, spacer)

            rows.append(
                {
                    "guide": guide,
                    "chrom": chrom,
                    "start": start,
                    "strand": strand,
                    "off_target_seq": spacer,
                    "pam_seq": pam_seq,
                    "num_mismatches": num_mm,
                    "mismatch_positions": mm_positions,
                }
            )

    if n_skipped_format:
        logger.warning(
            "Skipped %d malformed lines in Cas-OFFinder output", n_skipped_format
        )
    if n_skipped_chrom:
        logger.info(
            "Skipped %d hits on non-standard contigs", n_skipped_chrom
        )

    if not rows:
        return _empty_dataframe()

    df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    df["start"] = df["start"].astype("int64")
    df["num_mismatches"] = df["num_mismatches"].astype("int64")
    return df


def _empty_dataframe() -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Return an empty DataFrame with the canonical output schema."""
    return pd.DataFrame(
        columns=[
            "guide",
            "chrom",
            "start",
            "strand",
            "off_target_seq",
            "pam_seq",
            "num_mismatches",
            "mismatch_positions",
        ]
    )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Mismatch position computation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def _compute_mismatch_positions(guide: str, off_target_spacer: str) -> list[int]:
    """Return 1-based positions where guide and off-target spacer differ.

    Position numbering is identical to the convention in sequence_features.py:
    index 0 in the sequence string → position 1 (first character, 5' end).

    Parameters
    ----------
    guide : str
        20-nt guide sequence (uppercase DNA).
    off_target_spacer : str
        20-nt off-target spacer (without PAM), same strand orientation as guide.

    Returns
    -------
    list[int]
        Sorted 1-based mismatch positions.  Empty list for a perfect match.
    """
    return [
        i + 1
        for i, (g, o) in enumerate(zip(guide, off_target_spacer))
        if g.upper() != o.upper()
    ]


=== src/alphagenome_client.py ===
"""AlphaGenome API wrapper for chromatin feature extraction at CRISPR off-target sites.

This module wraps the AlphaGenome SDK to predict chromatin accessibility,
histone modifications, and TF binding at candidate off-target loci for a
specified cell type.  It is the core novel contribution of SafeGenome.

Key implementation notes
------------------------
- **Interval size** ``131 072`` bp (2**17).  The project spec described 100 kb but the
  actual SDK only accepts ``[16384, 131072, 524288, 1048576]``.  We use 131 072 as the
  closest option.
- **Resolution** differs by output type.  DNase and ATAC are 1 bp (shape
  ``(131072, n_tracks)``).  CHIP_HISTONE and CHIP_TF are 128 bp / bin
  (shape ``(1024, n_tracks)``).
- **Tiling**: sites are grouped into non-overlapping 131 072-bp tiles so that all
  sites within the same tile share a single API call.
- **Caching**: raw API responses are pickled to *cache_dir* via ``utils.save_cache``.
- **Rate limiting**: exponential back-off on rate-limit errors, plus a 1-second
  courtesy pause after every uncached call.
- **Missing tracks**: if a track type has no data for the requested cell type, the
  corresponding features are ``NaN`` rather than raising an error.

Cell-type track availability (queried 2026-02-24)
--------------------------------------------------
================== ============== ======= ====  ====================  =====
Cell type          Ontology term  DNase   ATAC  Histone marks         CTCF
================== ============== ======= ====  ====================  =====
K562               EFO:0002067    yes     yes   10 marks (no H3K9me3) yes
HEK293             EFO:0001182    **no**  **no** 3 marks (limited)    yes
U2OS               (none found)   **no**  **no** none                  no
================== ============== ======= ====  ====================  =====

DNase/ATAC features are NaN for HEK293; all features are NaN for U2OS.
This is a known limitation documented in P2.2 of PROJECT.md.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Optional

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from src.utils import GenomicInterval, normalize_chrom, load_cache, save_cache

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Module-level constants
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#: Interval size used for all API calls.  Must be a power-of-2 value
#: accepted by the AlphaGenome SDK.
INTERVAL_SIZE: int = 131_072

#: Base-pairs per bin for CHIP_HISTONE / CHIP_TF outputs.
BIN_SIZE: int = 128                           # 131072 / 1024 = 128

#: Number of bins per interval.
N_BINS: int = INTERVAL_SIZE // BIN_SIZE       # = 1024

#: Default half-window in bp for wide feature extraction.
DEFAULT_WINDOW_BP: int = 500

#: Half-window in bp for the narrow feature extraction (P2.5).
NARROW_WINDOW_BP: int = 50

#: DNase mean threshold to call a site "accessible" (ag_accessibility_binary).
DNASE_OPEN_THRESHOLD: float = 1.0

#: Maximum API retry attempts before raising.
DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS: int = 5

#: Seconds to sleep after each uncached API call (courtesy rate-limit).
COURTESY_SLEEP_S: float = 1.0

#: Ordered list of feature column names produced per site.
FEATURE_NAMES: list[str] = [
    "ag_dnase_mean",          # Mean DNase over +/-window_bp
    "ag_dnase_max",           # Max DNase over +/-window_bp
    "ag_dnase_at_site",       # DNase signal at exact cut site
    "ag_dnase_mean_narrow",   # Mean DNase over +/-50 bp (P2.5)
    "ag_atac_mean",           # Mean ATAC over +/-window_bp
    "ag_atac_at_site",        # ATAC signal at exact cut site
    "ag_h3k4me3_mean",        # Active promoter mark
    "ag_h3k27me3_mean",       # Polycomb repression mark
    "ag_h3k9me3_mean",        # Constitutive heterochromatin mark
    "ag_h3k27ac_mean",        # Active enhancer / promoter mark
    "ag_ctcf_mean",           # CTCF insulator binding
    "ag_accessibility_binary", # 1 if ag_dnase_mean > DNASE_OPEN_THRESHOLD
]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# TrackAvailability — per-cell-type track inventory
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass
class TrackAvailability:
    """Describes which AlphaGenome output types are available for one cell type.

    Parameters
    ----------
    ontology_term : str
        Cell-type ontology identifier, e.g. ``"EFO:0002067"`` for K562.
    has_dnase : bool
        At least one DNase track exists for this cell type.
    has_atac : bool
        At least one ATAC track exists for this cell type.
    histone_marks : list[str]
        Mark names present, e.g. ``["H3K27ac", "H3K27me3", "H3K4me3"]``.
    has_ctcf : bool
        A CTCF TF ChIP track exists for this cell type.
    """

    ontology_term: str
    has_dnase: bool
    has_atac: bool
    histone_marks: list[str]
    has_ctcf: bool

    @property
    def has_any_tracks(self) -> bool:
        """True if at least one output type has data for this cell type."""
        return (
            self.has_dnase
            or self.has_atac
            or bool(self.histone_marks)
            or self.has_ctcf
        )

    @property
    def requested_output_types(self) -> list:
        """Build the ``requested_outputs`` list for ``model.predict_interval``.

        Only includes ``OutputType`` values that have at least one track for
        this cell type, avoiding unnecessary data transfer.

        Returns
        -------
        list[dna_client.OutputType]
        """
        from alphagenome.models import dna_client  # local import — SDK optional at module level

        types: list = []
        if self.has_dnase:
            types.append(dna_client.OutputType.DNASE)
        if self.has_atac:
            types.append(dna_client.OutputType.ATAC)
        if self.histone_marks:
            types.append(dna_client.OutputType.CHIP_HISTONE)
        if self.has_ctcf:
            types.append(dna_client.OutputType.CHIP_TF)
        return types


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Client factory and metadata helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def create_client(api_key: str):
    """Create and return an AlphaGenome ``DnaClient``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    api_key : str
        AlphaGenome API key (from ``config.ALPHAGENOME_API_KEY``).

    Returns
    -------
    dna_client.DnaClient
    """
    from alphagenome.models import dna_client
    return dna_client.create(api_key)


def get_track_availability(model, ontology_term: str) -> TrackAvailability:
    """Query AlphaGenome metadata to discover tracks available for a cell type.

    Parameters
    ----------
    model : dna_client.DnaClient
        Pre-initialised AlphaGenome client.
    ontology_term : str
        Cell-type ontology identifier, e.g. ``"EFO:0002067"``.

    Returns
    -------
    TrackAvailability

    Notes
    -----
    Logs a warning if no tracks are found (all features will be NaN for
    sites queried with this cell type).
    """
    from alphagenome.models import dna_client

    meta = model.output_metadata(dna_client.Organism.HOMO_SAPIENS)

    has_dnase = bool((meta.dnase["ontology_curie"] == ontology_term).any())
    has_atac = bool((meta.atac["ontology_curie"] == ontology_term).any())

    histone_df = meta.chip_histone[
        meta.chip_histone["ontology_curie"] == ontology_term
    ]
    # Extract mark name as the last token of the track name string, e.g.
    # "EFO:0002067 Histone ChIP-seq H3K4me3" → "H3K4me3"
    marks: list[str] = sorted(
        {name.split()[-1] for name in histone_df["name"].tolist()}
    )

    tf_df = meta.chip_tf[meta.chip_tf["ontology_curie"] == ontology_term]
    has_ctcf = bool(tf_df["name"].str.contains("CTCF", na=False).any())

    avail = TrackAvailability(
        ontology_term=ontology_term,
        has_dnase=has_dnase,
        has_atac=has_atac,
        histone_marks=marks,
        has_ctcf=has_ctcf,
    )
    logger.info(
        "Track availability for %s: DNase=%s ATAC=%s histones=%s CTCF=%s",
        ontology_term,
        avail.has_dnase,
        avail.has_atac,
        avail.histone_marks,
        avail.has_ctcf,
    )
    if not avail.has_any_tracks:
        logger.warning(
            "No tracks found for ontology term %r — all features will be NaN",
            ontology_term,
        )
    return avail


def list_cell_types(model) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Return a summary DataFrame of all cell types with at least one track.

    Useful for discovering which ontology terms to pass to
    :func:`batch_get_chromatin_features`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    model : dna_client.DnaClient

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Columns: ``ontology_curie``, ``biosample_name``, ``has_dnase``,
        ``has_atac``, ``n_histone_tracks``, ``has_ctcf``.
        Sorted by ``ontology_curie``.
    """
    from alphagenome.models import dna_client

    meta = model.output_metadata(dna_client.Organism.HOMO_SAPIENS)

    # Collect every unique ontology term across all output types
    all_terms: pd.DataFrame = pd.concat([
        meta.dnase[["ontology_curie", "biosample_name"]],
        meta.atac[["ontology_curie", "biosample_name"]],
        meta.chip_histone[["ontology_curie", "biosample_name"]],
        meta.chip_tf[["ontology_curie", "biosample_name"]],
    ]).drop_duplicates("ontology_curie")

    rows = []
    for _, row in all_terms.iterrows():
        term = row["ontology_curie"]
        has_dnase = bool((meta.dnase["ontology_curie"] == term).any())
        has_atac = bool((meta.atac["ontology_curie"] == term).any())
        n_histone = int((meta.chip_histone["ontology_curie"] == term).sum())
        has_ctcf = bool(
            meta.chip_tf[meta.chip_tf["ontology_curie"] == term]["name"]
            .str.contains("CTCF", na=False)
            .any()
        )
        rows.append({
            "ontology_curie": term,
            "biosample_name": row["biosample_name"],
            "has_dnase": has_dnase,
            "has_atac": has_atac,
            "n_histone_tracks": n_histone,
            "has_ctcf": has_ctcf,
        })
    return (
        pd.DataFrame(rows)
        .sort_values("ontology_curie")
        .reset_index(drop=True)
    )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Internal helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _site_to_tile(site_pos: int, chrom: str) -> tuple[str, int, int]:
    """Return the ``(chrom, tile_start, tile_end)`` for a 0-based site position.

    Sites are assigned to non-overlapping ``INTERVAL_SIZE``-bp tiles aligned
    to multiples of ``INTERVAL_SIZE``.  This guarantees:

    - Every tile is exactly ``INTERVAL_SIZE`` bp (a valid API interval).
    - Every site is strictly inside its tile (never at an edge by more than
      the tile size).
    - Cache keys are deterministic regardless of call order.

    Parameters
    ----------
    site_pos : int
        0-based off-target site position.
    chrom : str
        Normalised chromosome name (``chr``-prefixed).

    Returns
    -------
    tuple[str, int, int]
        ``(chrom, tile_start, tile_end)`` in 0-based half-open coordinates.
    """
    tile_start = (site_pos // INTERVAL_SIZE) * INTERVAL_SIZE
    return chrom, tile_start, tile_start + INTERVAL_SIZE


def _predict_with_retry(
    model,
    interval,
    requested_outputs: list,
    ontology_terms: list[str],
    max_attempts: int = DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS,
):
    """Call ``model.predict_interval`` with exponential back-off on errors.

    Retries on any exception that looks like a rate-limit (HTTP 429,
    ``ResourceExhausted``, ``quota``).  Other errors are retried up to
    *max_attempts* times with the same back-off schedule before raising.

    Parameters
    ----------
    model : dna_client.DnaClient
    interval : genome.Interval
    requested_outputs : list[OutputType]
    ontology_terms : list[str]
    max_attempts : int

    Returns
    -------
    Prediction output object from ``model.predict_interval``.

    Raises
    ------
    RuntimeError
        If all attempts are exhausted.
    """
    last_exc: Exception | None = None
    for attempt in range(max_attempts):
        try:
            return model.predict_interval(
                interval=interval,
                requested_outputs=requested_outputs,
                ontology_terms=ontology_terms,
            )
        except Exception as exc:
            last_exc = exc
            exc_lower = str(exc).lower()
            is_rate_limit = any(
                kw in exc_lower
                for kw in ("rate", "429", "resourceexhausted", "quota", "resource_exhausted")
            )
            sleep_s = 2 ** attempt
            if is_rate_limit:
                logger.warning(
                    "Rate-limit on attempt %d/%d, sleeping %ds: %s",
                    attempt + 1, max_attempts, sleep_s, exc,
                )
            else:
                logger.warning(
                    "API error on attempt %d/%d, sleeping %ds: %s",
                    attempt + 1, max_attempts, sleep_s, exc,
                )
            if attempt < max_attempts - 1:
                time.sleep(sleep_s)

    raise RuntimeError(
        f"All {max_attempts} attempts failed for interval {interval}"
    ) from last_exc


def _extract_bp_signal(
    signal_2d: np.ndarray,
    site_offset: int,
    window_bp: int,
    narrow_window_bp: int,
) -> tuple[float, float, float, float]:
    """Extract summary statistics from a 1-bp-resolution signal array.

    Parameters
    ----------
    signal_2d : np.ndarray
        Shape ``(interval_bp, n_tracks)``.  Multiple tracks are averaged.
    site_offset : int
        0-based position of the cut site within the interval.
    window_bp : int
        Half-width of the wide window in bp.
    narrow_window_bp : int
        Half-width of the narrow window in bp.

    Returns
    -------
    tuple[float, float, float, float]
        ``(wide_mean, wide_max, at_site, narrow_mean)``
    """
    signal_1d: np.ndarray = signal_2d.mean(axis=1)
    n = len(signal_1d)

    w_start = max(0, site_offset - window_bp)
    w_end = min(n, site_offset + window_bp)
    nw_start = max(0, site_offset - narrow_window_bp)
    nw_end = min(n, site_offset + narrow_window_bp)

    at_site = (
        float(signal_1d[site_offset]) if 0 <= site_offset < n else float("nan")
    )
    wide_window = signal_1d[w_start:w_end]
    narrow_window = signal_1d[nw_start:nw_end]

    wide_mean = float(np.mean(wide_window)) if len(wide_window) else float("nan")
    wide_max = float(np.max(wide_window)) if len(wide_window) else float("nan")
    narrow_mean = (
        float(np.mean(narrow_window)) if len(narrow_window) else float("nan")
    )
    return wide_mean, wide_max, at_site, narrow_mean


def _extract_bin_signal(
    signal_2d: np.ndarray,
    site_offset: int,
    window_bp: int,
    track_mask: Optional[np.ndarray] = None,
) -> float:
    """Extract mean signal from a binned-resolution (128 bp/bin) track array.

    Parameters
    ----------
    signal_2d : np.ndarray
        Shape ``(n_bins, n_tracks)``.
    site_offset : int
        0-based position of the cut site within the interval in bp.
    window_bp : int
        Half-width in bp, converted to bins via ``// BIN_SIZE``.
    track_mask : np.ndarray | None
        Boolean mask selecting specific columns (tracks).  None = use all.

    Returns
    -------
    float
        Mean signal over the bin window, or NaN if mask selects nothing.
    """
    if track_mask is not None:
        if not np.any(track_mask):
            return float("nan")
        data = signal_2d[:, track_mask]
    else:
        data = signal_2d

    signal_1d: np.ndarray = data.mean(axis=1)
    n_bins = len(signal_1d)

    site_bin = site_offset // BIN_SIZE
    half_bins = max(1, window_bp // BIN_SIZE)
    b_start = max(0, site_bin - half_bins)
    b_end = min(n_bins, site_bin + half_bins)

    window = signal_1d[b_start:b_end]
    return float(np.mean(window)) if len(window) else float("nan")


def _nan_features() -> dict[str, float]:
    """Return a feature dict with all values set to NaN."""
    return {name: float("nan") for name in FEATURE_NAMES}


def _extract_chromatin_features(
    output,
    site_offset: int,
    avail: TrackAvailability,
    window_bp: int = DEFAULT_WINDOW_BP,
) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Extract the full chromatin feature vector from an AlphaGenome output.

    Parameters
    ----------
    output
        AlphaGenome prediction object returned by ``model.predict_interval``.
    site_offset : int
        0-based position of the off-target site within the interval.
    avail : TrackAvailability
        Which output types are present.
    window_bp : int
        Half-width of the wide extraction window in bp.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, float]
        Feature dictionary with keys matching :data:`FEATURE_NAMES`.
    """
    feats: dict[str, float] = {}

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ DNase
    if avail.has_dnase:
        d_mean, d_max, d_site, d_narrow = _extract_bp_signal(
            output.dnase.values, site_offset, window_bp, NARROW_WINDOW_BP
        )
        feats["ag_dnase_mean"] = d_mean
        feats["ag_dnase_max"] = d_max
        feats["ag_dnase_at_site"] = d_site
        feats["ag_dnase_mean_narrow"] = d_narrow
        feats["ag_accessibility_binary"] = float(d_mean > DNASE_OPEN_THRESHOLD)
    else:
        feats["ag_dnase_mean"] = float("nan")
        feats["ag_dnase_max"] = float("nan")
        feats["ag_dnase_at_site"] = float("nan")
        feats["ag_dnase_mean_narrow"] = float("nan")
        feats["ag_accessibility_binary"] = float("nan")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------- ATAC
    if avail.has_atac:
        a_mean, _, a_site, _ = _extract_bp_signal(
            output.atac.values, site_offset, window_bp, NARROW_WINDOW_BP
        )
        feats["ag_atac_mean"] = a_mean
        feats["ag_atac_at_site"] = a_site
    else:
        feats["ag_atac_mean"] = float("nan")
        feats["ag_atac_at_site"] = float("nan")

    # -------------------------------------------------------------- Histones
    _MARK_TO_FEAT: dict[str, str] = {
        "H3K4me3":  "ag_h3k4me3_mean",
        "H3K27me3": "ag_h3k27me3_mean",
        "H3K9me3":  "ag_h3k9me3_mean",
        "H3K27ac":  "ag_h3k27ac_mean",
    }
    if avail.histone_marks:
        histone_vals = output.chip_histone.values
        histone_meta = output.chip_histone.metadata
        for mark, feat_name in _MARK_TO_FEAT.items():
            mask = histone_meta["name"].str.contains(mark, na=False).values
            feats[feat_name] = _extract_bin_signal(
                histone_vals, site_offset, window_bp, track_mask=mask
            )
    else:
        for feat_name in _MARK_TO_FEAT.values():
            feats[feat_name] = float("nan")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------- CTCF
    if avail.has_ctcf:
        ctcf_mask = (
            output.chip_tf.metadata["name"].str.contains("CTCF", na=False).values
        )
        feats["ag_ctcf_mean"] = _extract_bin_signal(
            output.chip_tf.values, site_offset, window_bp, track_mask=ctcf_mask
        )
    else:
        feats["ag_ctcf_mean"] = float("nan")

    return feats


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Core public API
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def get_chromatin_features(
    site: GenomicInterval,
    cell_type: str,
    model,
    cache_dir: Path,
    avail: Optional[TrackAvailability] = None,
    window_bp: int = DEFAULT_WINDOW_BP,
) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Extract AlphaGenome chromatin features for a single off-target site.

    Uses the tile cache if an API response for this locus + cell type has
    already been fetched.

    Parameters
    ----------
    site : GenomicInterval
        Off-target locus in 0-based half-open coordinates (hg38).
    cell_type : str
        Cell-type ontology term, e.g. ``"EFO:0002067"`` for K562.
    model : dna_client.DnaClient
        Pre-initialised AlphaGenome client.
    cache_dir : Path
        Directory for caching raw API responses.
    avail : TrackAvailability | None
        Track availability for *cell_type*.  Queried automatically if ``None``.
    window_bp : int
        Half-width of the feature extraction window in bp.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, float]
        Feature dictionary with keys matching :data:`FEATURE_NAMES`.
        Missing tracks yield NaN values.
    """
    from alphagenome.data import genome as ag_genome

    if avail is None:
        avail = get_track_availability(model, cell_type)

    if not avail.has_any_tracks:
        logger.warning(
            "No tracks for cell type %r — returning NaN for %s", cell_type, site
        )
        return _nan_features()

    chrom = normalize_chrom(site.chrom)
    _, tile_start, tile_end = _site_to_tile(site.start, chrom)
    site_offset = site.start - tile_start

    cached = load_cache(
        Path(cache_dir), chrom, str(tile_start), str(tile_end), cell_type
    )
    if cached is None:
        interval = ag_genome.Interval(
            chromosome=chrom, start=tile_start, end=tile_end
        )
        logger.debug(
            "API call: %s:%d-%d for %s", chrom, tile_start, tile_end, cell_type
        )
        output = _predict_with_retry(
            model, interval, avail.requested_output_types, [cell_type]
        )
        save_cache(
            Path(cache_dir), output,
            chrom, str(tile_start), str(tile_end), cell_type,
        )
        time.sleep(COURTESY_SLEEP_S)
    else:
        logger.debug(
            "Cache hit: %s:%d-%d for %s", chrom, tile_start, tile_end, cell_type
        )
        output = cached

    return _extract_chromatin_features(output, site_offset, avail, window_bp)


def batch_get_chromatin_features(
    sites: pd.DataFrame,
    cell_type: str,
    model,
    cache_dir: Path,
    avail: Optional[TrackAvailability] = None,
    window_bp: int = DEFAULT_WINDOW_BP,
    show_progress: bool = True,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Batch AlphaGenome feature extraction with tiling, caching, and rate limiting.

    Groups sites into ``INTERVAL_SIZE``-bp tiles so that all sites within the
    same tile share a single API call.  Sites from different chromosomes are
    batched separately.  Processes chromosomes in sorted order.

    Parameters
    ----------
    sites : pd.DataFrame
        Must contain columns ``chrom`` (str) and ``start`` (int, 0-based).
        All other columns are preserved in the output.
    cell_type : str
        Cell-type ontology term, e.g. ``"EFO:0002067"``.
    model : dna_client.DnaClient
    cache_dir : Path
        Directory for caching raw API responses (created if absent).
    avail : TrackAvailability | None
        Track availability.  Queried automatically if ``None``.
    window_bp : int
        Feature extraction half-window in bp.
    show_progress : bool
        Log a progress message after each tile (useful for long jobs).

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Input DataFrame with one additional column per feature in
        :data:`FEATURE_NAMES`.  Row order matches the input.

    Notes
    -----
    If *cell_type* has no tracks (e.g. U2OS), all feature columns are NaN
    and no API calls are made.
    """
    from alphagenome.data import genome as ag_genome

    cache_dir = Path(cache_dir)

    if avail is None:
        avail = get_track_availability(model, cell_type)

    if not avail.has_any_tracks:
        logger.warning(
            "No tracks for %r — all %d sites will have NaN features",
            cell_type, len(sites),
        )
        result = sites.copy()
        for feat in FEATURE_NAMES:
            result[feat] = float("nan")
        return result

    # Compute tile assignments without modifying caller's DataFrame
    work = sites.copy()
    work["_chrom_norm"] = work["chrom"].apply(normalize_chrom)
    work["_tile_start"] = (work["start"] // INTERVAL_SIZE) * INTERVAL_SIZE
    work["_tile_end"] = work["_tile_start"] + INTERVAL_SIZE
    work["_site_offset"] = work["start"] - work["_tile_start"]

    # Unique tiles sorted for reproducibility
    tiles = (
        work[["_chrom_norm", "_tile_start", "_tile_end"]]
        .drop_duplicates()
        .sort_values(["_chrom_norm", "_tile_start"])
        .reset_index(drop=True)
    )
    n_tiles = len(tiles)
    logger.info(
        "batch_get_chromatin_features: %d sites → %d unique tiles, cell_type=%s",
        len(sites), n_tiles, cell_type,
    )

    # feature_rows maps DataFrame index → feature dict
    feature_rows: dict[int, dict[str, float]] = {}

    for tile_idx, tile_row in tiles.iterrows():
        chrom: str = tile_row["_chrom_norm"]
        tile_start: int = int(tile_row["_tile_start"])
        tile_end: int = int(tile_row["_tile_end"])

        cached = load_cache(
            cache_dir, chrom, str(tile_start), str(tile_end), cell_type
        )
        if cached is None:
            interval = ag_genome.Interval(
                chromosome=chrom, start=tile_start, end=tile_end
            )
            if show_progress:
                logger.info(
                    "Tile %d/%d API call: %s:%d-%d for %s",
                    int(tile_idx) + 1, n_tiles, chrom, tile_start, tile_end, cell_type,
                )
            output = _predict_with_retry(
                model, interval, avail.requested_output_types, [cell_type]
            )
            save_cache(
                cache_dir, output,
                chrom, str(tile_start), str(tile_end), cell_type,
            )
            time.sleep(COURTESY_SLEEP_S)
        else:
            if show_progress:
                logger.info(
                    "Tile %d/%d cache hit: %s:%d-%d for %s",
                    int(tile_idx) + 1, n_tiles, chrom, tile_start, tile_end, cell_type,
                )
            output = cached

        # Extract features for all sites in this tile
        tile_mask = (
            (work["_chrom_norm"] == chrom)
            & (work["_tile_start"] == tile_start)
        )
        for row_idx, site_row in work[tile_mask].iterrows():
            feature_rows[row_idx] = _extract_chromatin_features(
                output, int(site_row["_site_offset"]), avail, window_bp
            )

    # Assemble result preserving input row order
    result = sites.copy()
    for feat in FEATURE_NAMES:
        result[feat] = [feature_rows[idx][feat] for idx in result.index]

    return result


=== src/encode_client.py ===
"""ENCODE experimental chromatin data retrieval for gold-standard model comparison.

Reads BigWig files from the ENCODE portal to extract experimental chromatin
accessibility (DNase-seq) and histone modification (ChIP-seq) signals at
CRISPR off-target loci.

This module provides the "gold standard" experimental features used to train
Model B in the SafeGenome three-model comparison. Model B uses actual
experimental measurements from ENCODE rather than AlphaGenome predictions.

Data source
-----------
ENCODE Project: https://www.encodeproject.org/
Files should be downloaded and placed in ``data/experimental_chromatin/``.
See :func:`list_required_files` for required filenames and
the project documentation for download instructions.

Expected filenames
------------------
::

    data/experimental_chromatin/
    ├── K562_DNase_hg38.bigWig
    ├── K562_H3K4me3_hg38.bigWig
    ├── K562_H3K27me3_hg38.bigWig
    ├── K562_H3K9me3_hg38.bigWig
    ├── K562_H3K27ac_hg38.bigWig
    ├── HEK293_DNase_hg38.bigWig      ← may not exist
    ├── HEK293_H3K4me3_hg38.bigWig   ← may not exist
    └── ...

Feature window
--------------
All features use a +/-500 bp window around the cut site, matching the
AlphaGenome feature extraction window in ``src/alphagenome_client.py``.

Scale mismatch note (P2.6 in PROJECT.md)
-----------------------------------------
AlphaGenome outputs and ENCODE BigWig files use DIFFERENT scales and
normalizations.  Never directly compare feature values across the two
modules.  Compare only model PERFORMANCE (AUPRC) between Model B (ENCODE
features) and Model C (AlphaGenome features).  For correlation checks,
use Spearman rank correlation (not Pearson).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Optional

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from src.utils import GenomicInterval, normalize_chrom

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Module-level constants
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#: Default half-window in bp for feature extraction (matches AlphaGenome client).
DEFAULT_WINDOW_BP: int = 500

#: Half-window in bp for the narrow at-site feature (mirrors AlphaGenome).
NARROW_WINDOW_BP: int = 50

#: DNase mean threshold to call a site "accessible" (exp_accessibility_binary).
#: Matches the same threshold used in alphagenome_client.DNASE_OPEN_THRESHOLD.
DNASE_OPEN_THRESHOLD: float = 1.0

#: Ordered list of feature column names produced per site.
FEATURE_NAMES: list[str] = [
    "exp_dnase_mean",           # Mean DNase signal over +/-window_bp
