    "exp_dnase_max",            # Max DNase signal over +/-window_bp
    "exp_dnase_at_site",        # DNase signal at exact cut site (0-based start)
    "exp_h3k4me3_mean",         # Mean H3K4me3 signal (active promoter mark)
    "exp_h3k27me3_mean",        # Mean H3K27me3 signal (Polycomb repression)
    "exp_h3k9me3_mean",         # Mean H3K9me3 signal (constitutive heterochromatin)
    "exp_h3k27ac_mean",         # Mean H3K27ac signal (active enhancer/promoter)
    "exp_accessibility_binary", # 1 if exp_dnase_mean > DNASE_OPEN_THRESHOLD
]

#: Recognised assay names — determines which BigWig files are scanned.
ASSAY_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = ("DNase", "H3K4me3", "H3K27me3", "H3K9me3", "H3K27ac")

#: Filename template used when scanning the BigWig directory.
FILE_TEMPLATE: str = "{cell_type}_{assay}_hg38.bigWig"

#: Map from assay name to the single feature name it populates (histones only).
_HISTONE_MAP: dict[str, str] = {
    "H3K4me3":  "exp_h3k4me3_mean",
    "H3K27me3": "exp_h3k27me3_mean",
    "H3K9me3":  "exp_h3k9me3_mean",
    "H3K27ac":  "exp_h3k27ac_mean",
}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# BigWigManifest — file registry
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass
class BigWigManifest:
    """Registry mapping ``(cell_type, assay)`` pairs to BigWig file paths.

    Build this once at pipeline startup via :func:`build_manifest` and pass
    it to :func:`get_chromatin_features` / :func:`batch_get_chromatin_features`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    bigwig_dir : Path
        Root directory that was scanned to build the manifest.
    files : dict[tuple[str, str], Path]
        Mapping of ``(cell_type, assay)`` to absolute ``Path`` objects.
        Populated by :func:`build_manifest`.

    Notes
    -----
    Missing assays are represented by their absence from *files*, not by a
    sentinel value.  Missing assays yield NaN features — not an error.
    """

    bigwig_dir: Path
    files: dict[tuple[str, str], Path] = field(default_factory=dict)

    def get(self, cell_type: str, assay: str) -> Optional[Path]:
        """Return the Path for ``(cell_type, assay)``, or ``None`` if absent."""
        return self.files.get((cell_type, assay))

    def has_any(self, cell_type: str) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if at least one BigWig file exists for *cell_type*."""
        return any(ct == cell_type for ct, _ in self.files)

    def available_cell_types(self) -> list[str]:
        """Sorted list of cell types with at least one BigWig file."""
        return sorted({ct for ct, _ in self.files})

    def available_assays(self, cell_type: str) -> list[str]:
        """Sorted list of assays available for *cell_type*."""
        return sorted(assay for ct, assay in self.files if ct == cell_type)


def build_manifest(bigwig_dir: Path) -> BigWigManifest:
    """Scan *bigwig_dir* for BigWig files and build a :class:`BigWigManifest`.

    Recognises files matching ``{cell_type}_{assay}_hg38.bigWig`` for
    ``cell_type`` in ``("K562", "HEK293")`` and ``assay`` in
    :data:`ASSAY_NAMES`.  Unknown files are silently ignored.

    Parameters
    ----------
    bigwig_dir : Path
        Directory containing BigWig files (e.g. ``data/experimental_chromatin/``).

    Returns
    -------
    BigWigManifest
        Populated manifest.  If the directory does not exist or has no
        recognised files, an empty manifest is returned and a WARNING is logged.
    """
    bigwig_dir = Path(bigwig_dir)
    manifest = BigWigManifest(bigwig_dir=bigwig_dir)

    if not bigwig_dir.exists():
        logger.warning(
            "BigWig directory does not exist: %s — all features will be NaN",
            bigwig_dir,
        )
        return manifest

    found = 0
    for cell_type in ("K562", "HEK293"):
        for assay in ASSAY_NAMES:
            fname = FILE_TEMPLATE.format(cell_type=cell_type, assay=assay)
            path = bigwig_dir / fname
            if path.exists():
                manifest.files[(cell_type, assay)] = path
                logger.info(
                    "BigWig manifest: %s/%s → %s", cell_type, assay, fname
                )
                found += 1

    if found == 0:
        logger.warning(
            "No recognised BigWig files found in %s — all features will be NaN",
            bigwig_dir,
        )
    else:
        logger.info("BigWig manifest: %d file(s) indexed", found)

    return manifest


def list_required_files() -> list[str]:
    """Return expected filenames for all BigWig files.

    Use this to check which files still need to be downloaded.

    Returns
    -------
    list[str]
        Filenames (relative to ``data/experimental_chromatin/``), one per
        cell-type × assay combination.
    """
    return [
        FILE_TEMPLATE.format(cell_type=ct, assay=assay)
        for ct in ("K562", "HEK293")
        for assay in ASSAY_NAMES
    ]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Internal helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _open_bigwig(path: Path) -> Any:
    """Open a BigWig file with pybigtools.

    Parameters
    ----------
    path : Path
        Absolute path to the ``.bigWig`` file.

    Returns
    -------
    pybigtools.BBIRead
        Open file handle.  Caller is responsible for calling ``.close()``.

    Raises
    ------
    ImportError
        If ``pybigtools`` is not installed.
    FileNotFoundError
        If *path* does not exist.
    """
    try:
        import pybigtools  # type: ignore[import]
    except ImportError as exc:
        raise ImportError(
            "pybigtools is required for ENCODE feature extraction. "
            "Install with: pip install pybigtools"
        ) from exc

    if not path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"BigWig file not found: {path}")

    return pybigtools.open(str(path))


def _query_region(bw: Any, chrom: str, start: int, end: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Query a BigWig file over ``[start, end)``, returning NaN for gaps.

    pybigtools returns a ``numpy.ndarray`` where uncovered positions are filled
    with the ``missing`` value (set to NaN here).

    Chromosome boundary clamping is handled: if ``start < 0`` or
    ``end > chrom_length``, the out-of-bounds region is filled with NaN.

    Parameters
    ----------
    bw : pybigtools.BBIRead
        Open BigWig file handle.
    chrom : str
        Chromosome name (``chr``-prefixed, e.g. ``"chr7"``).
    start : int
        0-based inclusive start position.
    end : int
        0-based exclusive end position.

    Returns
    -------
    np.ndarray
        Float64 array of length ``end - start``, with NaN for uncovered
        positions.  Returns an all-NaN array if *chrom* is absent from
        the BigWig or if the requested range is entirely out of bounds.
    """
    n = end - start
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    chroms = bw.chroms()
    if chrom not in chroms:
        logger.debug("Chrom %s not in BigWig; returning NaN array", chrom)
        return np.full(n, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)

    chrom_len: int = chroms[chrom]
    actual_start = max(0, start)
    actual_end = min(chrom_len, end)

    if actual_start >= actual_end:
        return np.full(n, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)

    # pybigtools returns a numpy array directly; missing=nan fills uncovered positions
    arr = np.asarray(
        bw.values(chrom, actual_start, actual_end, missing=float("nan")),
        dtype=np.float64,
    )

    # Pad with NaN if the query was clamped at chromosome boundaries.
    if actual_start > start or actual_end < end:
        padded = np.full(n, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)
        pad_offset = actual_start - start
        padded[pad_offset : pad_offset + len(arr)] = arr
        return padded

    return arr


def _extract_signal_stats(
    arr: np.ndarray,
    site_offset: int,
    window_bp: int,
) -> tuple[float, float, float]:
    """Extract summary statistics from a signal array.

    Parameters
    ----------
    arr : np.ndarray
        1D float64 signal array (may contain NaN).
    site_offset : int
        0-based index of the cut site within *arr*.
    window_bp : int
        Half-width of the extraction window in base pairs.

    Returns
    -------
    tuple[float, float, float]
        ``(mean, max, at_site)`` over the window.  All NaN if *arr* is
        empty, the window is empty, or all values are NaN.
        ``at_site`` is NaN if *site_offset* is out of bounds.

    Notes
    -----
    Uses ``np.nanmean`` / ``np.nanmax`` so that sparse coverage (isolated
    None values from pyBigWig) does not dominate the statistics.
    """
    n = len(arr)
    if n == 0:
        return float("nan"), float("nan"), float("nan")

    w_start = max(0, site_offset - window_bp)
    w_end = min(n, site_offset + window_bp)
    window = arr[w_start:w_end]

    if len(window) == 0:
        return float("nan"), float("nan"), float("nan")

    import warnings
    with warnings.catch_warnings():
        warnings.simplefilter("ignore", RuntimeWarning)
        mean_v = float(np.nanmean(window))
        max_v = float(np.nanmax(window))

    at_site = float(arr[site_offset]) if 0 <= site_offset < n else float("nan")
    return mean_v, max_v, at_site


def _nan_features() -> dict[str, float]:
    """Return a feature dict with all values set to NaN."""
    return {name: float("nan") for name in FEATURE_NAMES}


def _extract_from_open_bws(
    open_bws: dict[str, Any],
    chrom: str,
    site_pos: int,
    window_bp: int,
) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Extract the full chromatin feature vector from already-open BigWig handles.

    This is the core extraction routine shared by both
    :func:`get_chromatin_features` (single-site) and
    :func:`batch_get_chromatin_features` (batch, files opened once).

    Parameters
    ----------
    open_bws : dict[str, Any]
        Mapping of assay name (e.g. ``"DNase"``) to open pyBigWig handle.
    chrom : str
        Normalised chromosome name (``chr``-prefixed).
    site_pos : int
        0-based position of the cut site on *chrom*.
    window_bp : int
        Half-width of the feature extraction window in bp.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, float]
        Feature dictionary with keys matching :data:`FEATURE_NAMES`.
        Assays absent from *open_bws* yield NaN values.
    """
    feats = _nan_features()

    # The query window starts at max(0, site_pos - window_bp).
    # site_offset is the index of the cut site within the returned array.
    q_start = max(0, site_pos - window_bp)
    q_end = site_pos + window_bp
    site_offset = site_pos - q_start  # always >= 0

    # ---- DNase: produces mean, max, at_site, and accessibility_binary
    if "DNase" in open_bws:
        arr = _query_region(open_bws["DNase"], chrom, q_start, q_end)
        mean_v, max_v, at_site = _extract_signal_stats(arr, site_offset, window_bp)
        feats["exp_dnase_mean"] = mean_v
        feats["exp_dnase_max"] = max_v
        feats["exp_dnase_at_site"] = at_site
        # Match alphagenome_client convention: nan > threshold = False → 0.0
        feats["exp_accessibility_binary"] = float(mean_v > DNASE_OPEN_THRESHOLD)

    # ---- Histone marks: each assay → single mean feature
    for assay, feat_name in _HISTONE_MAP.items():
        if assay in open_bws:
            arr = _query_region(open_bws[assay], chrom, q_start, q_end)
            mean_v, _, _ = _extract_signal_stats(arr, site_offset, window_bp)
            feats[feat_name] = mean_v

    return feats


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Public API
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def get_chromatin_features(
    site: GenomicInterval,
    cell_type: str,
    manifest: BigWigManifest,
    window_bp: int = DEFAULT_WINDOW_BP,
) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Extract ENCODE experimental chromatin features for a single off-target site.

    Opens each BigWig file, queries the region, and closes the file.  For
    processing many sites, prefer :func:`batch_get_chromatin_features` which
    keeps files open across all queries.

    Parameters
    ----------
    site : GenomicInterval
        Off-target locus in 0-based half-open coordinates (hg38).
        The cut site is taken as ``site.start``.
    cell_type : str
        Cell type name exactly as indexed in the manifest, e.g. ``"K562"``
        or ``"HEK293"``.
    manifest : BigWigManifest
        BigWig file manifest built by :func:`build_manifest`.
    window_bp : int
        Half-width of the feature extraction window in bp.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, float]
        Feature dictionary with keys matching :data:`FEATURE_NAMES`.
        Missing files or missing assays yield NaN values.

    Notes
    -----
    Logs a WARNING if no BigWig files are found for *cell_type*.
    Per-assay open errors are also warned and result in NaN for that assay.
    """
    chrom = normalize_chrom(site.chrom)

    if not manifest.has_any(cell_type):
        logger.warning(
            "No BigWig files for cell type %r — returning NaN for %s",
            cell_type,
            site,
        )
        return _nan_features()

    open_bws: dict[str, Any] = {}
    try:
        for assay in ASSAY_NAMES:
            path = manifest.get(cell_type, assay)
            if path is not None:
                try:
                    open_bws[assay] = _open_bigwig(path)
                except Exception as exc:
                    logger.warning(
                        "Could not open %s/%s BigWig: %s", cell_type, assay, exc
                    )

        return _extract_from_open_bws(open_bws, chrom, site.start, window_bp)

    finally:
        for bw in open_bws.values():
            try:
                bw.close()
            except Exception:
                pass


def batch_get_chromatin_features(
    sites: pd.DataFrame,
    cell_type: str,
    manifest: BigWigManifest,
    window_bp: int = DEFAULT_WINDOW_BP,
    show_progress: bool = True,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Batch ENCODE feature extraction for a DataFrame of off-target sites.

    Opens BigWig files **once** for the entire batch (unlike
    :func:`get_chromatin_features` which opens/closes per site).  This is
    the recommended function for processing more than a handful of sites.

    Parameters
    ----------
    sites : pd.DataFrame
        Must contain columns ``chrom`` (str) and ``start`` (int, 0-based).
        All other columns are preserved in the output.
    cell_type : str
        Cell type name, e.g. ``"K562"`` or ``"HEK293"``.
    manifest : BigWigManifest
        BigWig file manifest built by :func:`build_manifest`.
    window_bp : int
        Feature extraction half-window in bp.
    show_progress : bool
        Log a progress message every 1 000 sites.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Input DataFrame with one additional column per feature in
        :data:`FEATURE_NAMES`.  Row order matches the input.

    Notes
    -----
    If *cell_type* has no BigWig files, all feature columns are NaN and
    no files are opened.
    """
    n_sites = len(sites)
    logger.info(
        "batch_get_chromatin_features: %d sites, cell_type=%s", n_sites, cell_type
    )

    result = sites.copy()

    if not manifest.has_any(cell_type):
        logger.warning(
            "No BigWig files for %r — all %d sites will have NaN features",
            cell_type,
            n_sites,
        )
        for feat in FEATURE_NAMES:
            result[feat] = float("nan")
        return result

    # Open all available BigWig files once for the entire batch.
    open_bws: dict[str, Any] = {}
    try:
        for assay in ASSAY_NAMES:
            path = manifest.get(cell_type, assay)
            if path is not None:
                try:
                    open_bws[assay] = _open_bigwig(path)
                    logger.debug("Opened %s/%s BigWig", cell_type, assay)
                except Exception as exc:
                    logger.warning(
                        "Could not open %s/%s BigWig: %s", cell_type, assay, exc
                    )

        feature_rows: list[dict[str, float]] = []
        for i, (_, row) in enumerate(sites.iterrows()):
            if show_progress and (i % 1000 == 0 or i == n_sites - 1):
                logger.info(
                    "batch_get_chromatin_features: site %d/%d for %s",
                    i + 1,
                    n_sites,
                    cell_type,
                )
            chrom = normalize_chrom(str(row["chrom"]))
            site_pos = int(row["start"])
            feature_rows.append(
                _extract_from_open_bws(open_bws, chrom, site_pos, window_bp)
            )

    finally:
        for bw in open_bws.values():
            try:
                bw.close()
            except Exception:
                pass

    for feat in FEATURE_NAMES:
        result[feat] = [fr[feat] for fr in feature_rows]

    return result


=== src/feature_matrix.py ===
"""Feature matrix assembly for the SafeGenome three-model CRISPR off-target comparison.

Combines sequence features, AlphaGenome chromatin predictions, and ENCODE
experimental chromatin signals with ground-truth labels into a single
training-ready DataFrame.

Three-model feature sets
------------------------
- **Model A** (sequence-only baseline): sequence features derived from guide
  and off-target sequences — CFD score, MIT score, mismatch positions/types,
  GC content, PAM, seed statistics.
- **Model B** (gold standard): Model A features + ENCODE experimental
  chromatin signals.  Trained on K562 + HEK293 only.
- **Model C** (our method): Model A features + AlphaGenome predicted
  chromatin signals.  Trained on K562 + HEK293 only.

Critical rules enforced here
-----------------------------
1. **On-target removal**: candidates with ``num_mismatches == 0`` are removed
   before any downstream step.
2. **Label assignment via match window**: positives are matched to candidates
   by (guide_id, chrom, |start_cand − start_pos| ≤ match_window_bp).  All
   unmatched candidates are labeled negative (y = 0).
3. **NaN imputation**: chromatin features may be NaN (missing cell-type
   tracks, missing BigWig files, chromosome boundaries).  Each NaN column is
   filled with its within-dataset median and a binary missingness indicator
   column (``{feature}_missing``) is added.  See ``impute_chromatin_features``.
4. **All data drops are logged** via ``utils.log_filter`` for the paper's
   Methods section.

Cross-validation and model training are handled in ``src/model.py``, not here.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Optional

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

import src.alphagenome_client as _ag_mod
import src.encode_client as _enc_mod
from src.sequence_features import compute_sequence_features
from src.utils import log_filter, normalize_chrom

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Module-level constants
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#: Maps mismatch-type strings (CFD notation) to integer codes for XGBoost.
#: "" (match at a position) → 0.  Twelve canonical rX:dY mismatches → 1–12.
#: Any unrecognised value → -1 (logged as a warning).
MM_TYPE_TO_INT: dict[str, int] = {
    "":       0,
    "rA:dC":  1,  "rA:dG":  2,  "rA:dT":  3,
    "rC:dA":  4,  "rC:dG":  5,  "rC:dT":  6,
    "rG:dA":  7,  "rG:dC":  8,  "rG:dT":  9,
    "rT:dA": 10,  "rT:dC": 11,  "rT:dG": 12,
}

#: All sequence feature names (numeric-compatible, suitable for XGBoost).
#: mm_type columns are encoded as integers via MM_TYPE_TO_INT.
SEQUENCE_FEATURE_NAMES: list[str] = (
    ["cfd_score", "mit_score", "num_mismatches"]
    + [f"mm_pos_{i}" for i in range(1, 21)]
    + [f"mm_type_{i}" for i in range(1, 21)]
    + [
        "gc_content_guide", "gc_content_offtarget",
        "pam_match", "seed_mismatches", "nonseed_mismatches", "consecutive_mm",
    ]
)

#: AlphaGenome predicted chromatin feature names (from alphagenome_client).
AG_FEATURE_NAMES: list[str] = list(_ag_mod.FEATURE_NAMES)

#: ENCODE experimental chromatin feature names (from encode_client).
EXP_FEATURE_NAMES: list[str] = list(_enc_mod.FEATURE_NAMES)

#: Feature sets for each model.
MODEL_A_FEATURES: list[str] = list(SEQUENCE_FEATURE_NAMES)
MODEL_B_FEATURES: list[str] = list(SEQUENCE_FEATURE_NAMES) + list(EXP_FEATURE_NAMES)
MODEL_C_FEATURES: list[str] = list(SEQUENCE_FEATURE_NAMES) + list(AG_FEATURE_NAMES)

#: Output metadata column names (never used as model features).
METADATA_COLS: list[str] = [
    "guide_id", "chrom", "start", "strand",
    "guide_seq", "off_target_seq", "pam_seq",
    "cell_type", "dataset", "label",
]

#: Default position tolerance for matching candidates to experimental positives.
DEFAULT_MATCH_WINDOW_BP: int = 5

#: Default half-window for chromatin feature extraction (matches other modules).
DEFAULT_WINDOW_BP: int = 500

#: Suffix appended to imputed feature columns to flag missingness.
MISSING_INDICATOR_SUFFIX: str = "_missing"

#: Required columns in the candidates DataFrame.
_REQUIRED_CANDIDATE_COLS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "guide_id", "guide_seq", "chrom", "start",
    "strand", "off_target_seq", "pam_seq", "num_mismatches",
)

#: Required columns in the positives DataFrame.
_REQUIRED_POSITIVE_COLS: tuple[str, ...] = ("guide_id", "chrom", "start")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _require_columns(df: pd.DataFrame, cols: tuple[str, ...], name: str) -> None:
    """Raise ValueError if any of *cols* are absent from *df*."""
    missing = [c for c in cols if c not in df.columns]
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{name} is missing required columns: {missing}. "
            f"Present columns: {list(df.columns)}"
        )


def get_model_features(
    model_type: str,
    include_missingness_indicators: bool = False,
) -> list[str]:
    """Return the feature column names for a given model type.

    Parameters
    ----------
    model_type : str
        One of ``"A"``, ``"B"``, ``"C"`` (case-insensitive).
    include_missingness_indicators : bool
        If ``True``, append ``{feature}_missing`` indicator columns for all
        chromatin features (relevant for Models B and C).

    Returns
    -------
    list[str]
        Ordered list of feature column names.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If *model_type* is not ``"A"``, ``"B"``, or ``"C"``.
    """
    mapping: dict[str, list[str]] = {
        "a": MODEL_A_FEATURES,
        "b": MODEL_B_FEATURES,
        "c": MODEL_C_FEATURES,
    }
    key = model_type.strip().lower()
    if key not in mapping:
        raise ValueError(
            f"model_type must be 'A', 'B', or 'C', got {model_type!r}"
        )
    feats = list(mapping[key])

    if include_missingness_indicators:
        if key == "b":
            chromatin = EXP_FEATURE_NAMES
        elif key == "c":
            chromatin = AG_FEATURE_NAMES
        else:
            chromatin = []
        feats += [f + MISSING_INDICATOR_SUFFIX for f in chromatin]

    return feats


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Label assignment
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def label_candidates(
    candidates: pd.DataFrame,
    positives: pd.DataFrame,
    *,
    match_window_bp: int = DEFAULT_MATCH_WINDOW_BP,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Assign binary labels to off-target candidates by matching to experimental positives.

    A candidate is labeled positive (``label=1``) if a site in *positives*
    satisfies all three criteria:

    - Same ``guide_id``
    - Same chromosome (after normalisation via :func:`~src.utils.normalize_chrom`)
    - ``|candidate.start − positive.start| ≤ match_window_bp``

    All other candidates receive ``label=0`` (negative).

    Parameters
    ----------
    candidates : pd.DataFrame
        Must contain ``guide_id``, ``chrom``, ``start``.
    positives : pd.DataFrame
        Must contain ``guide_id``, ``chrom``, ``start``.
    match_window_bp : int
        Tolerance in base pairs for position matching.  Default 5 bp.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Copy of *candidates* with an additional ``label`` column (int, 0 or 1).
    """
    result = candidates.copy()

    if len(candidates) == 0:
        result["label"] = pd.array([], dtype=int)
        return result

    if len(positives) == 0:
        logger.warning(
            "label_candidates: positives DataFrame is empty — "
            "all %d candidates labeled negative", len(candidates)
        )
        result["label"] = 0
        return result

    # Normalise positives: deduplicate (guide_id, chrom, start) triples.
    pos = positives[["guide_id", "chrom", "start"]].copy()
    pos = pos.assign(chrom=pos["chrom"].apply(normalize_chrom))
    pos = pos.drop_duplicates(subset=["guide_id", "chrom", "start"])
    pos = pos.rename(columns={"start": "_pos_start"})

    # Build a working copy of candidates with normalised chrom and an
    # explicit column holding the original DataFrame index for later alignment.
    cands_work = candidates[["guide_id", "chrom", "start"]].copy()
    cands_work["_orig_idx"] = candidates.index
    cands_work = cands_work.assign(chrom=cands_work["chrom"].apply(normalize_chrom))

    # Left-merge: each candidate row joins all positives for same guide+chrom.
    merged = cands_work.merge(pos, on=["guide_id", "chrom"], how="left")
    merged["_within"] = (
        (merged["start"] - merged["_pos_start"]).abs() <= match_window_bp
    )
    # NaN distance (no positive for this guide+chrom) → no match.
    merged["_within"] = merged["_within"].fillna(False)

    # A candidate is positive if ANY of its joined rows matched.
    any_match = merged.groupby("_orig_idx")["_within"].any()

    # Align back to original index; unrepresented rows (impossible after
    # left-merge, but defensive) → False.
    result["label"] = (
        any_match.reindex(candidates.index, fill_value=False).astype(int).values
    )

    n_pos = int(result["label"].sum())
    n_total = len(result)
    logger.info(
        "label_candidates: %d / %d positive (%.1f%%), window=%d bp",
        n_pos, n_total,
        100.0 * n_pos / n_total if n_total else 0.0,
        match_window_bp,
    )
    return result


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Sequence feature extraction
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def extract_sequence_features_batch(candidates: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Compute sequence features for every candidate site.

    Calls :func:`~src.sequence_features.compute_sequence_features` row by row
    and encodes ``mm_type_*`` string columns to integers via
    :data:`MM_TYPE_TO_INT`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    candidates : pd.DataFrame
        Must contain ``guide_seq``, ``off_target_seq``, ``pam_seq``.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Input DataFrame with all :data:`SEQUENCE_FEATURE_NAMES` columns
        appended.  Rows with invalid sequences get NaN for all feature
        columns.
    """
    result = candidates.copy()
    feature_dicts: list[dict[str, Any]] = []

    for row_idx, row in candidates.iterrows():
        try:
            feats = compute_sequence_features(
                guide=str(row["guide_seq"]),
                off_target=str(row["off_target_seq"]),
                pam=str(row["pam_seq"]),
            )
            # Encode string mismatch types to integers in-place.
            for i in range(1, 21):
                key = f"mm_type_{i}"
                raw = feats.get(key, "")
                if isinstance(raw, str):
                    encoded = MM_TYPE_TO_INT.get(raw, -1)
                    if encoded == -1 and raw != "":
                        logger.warning(
                            "Unknown mm_type %r at row %s pos %d — encoding as -1",
                            raw, row_idx, i,
                        )
                    feats[key] = encoded
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning(
                "Sequence feature error at index %s (%s:%s): %s",
                row_idx, row.get("chrom"), row.get("start"), exc,
            )
            feats = {k: float("nan") for k in SEQUENCE_FEATURE_NAMES}

        feature_dicts.append(feats)

    feats_df = pd.DataFrame(feature_dicts, index=candidates.index)
    for col in feats_df.columns:
        result[col] = feats_df[col]

    return result


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# NaN imputation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def impute_chromatin_features(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    feature_cols: list[str],
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Median-impute NaN chromatin features and add binary missingness indicators.

    For each column in *feature_cols* that contains NaN values:

    1. Adds a new column ``{feature}_missing`` (1 where value was NaN, 0 otherwise).
    2. Fills NaN values with the column's within-DataFrame median.
       If the entire column is NaN (e.g., U2OS with no AlphaGenome tracks),
       fills with ``0.0`` and logs a WARNING.

    Non-NaN values are never modified.

    Parameters
    ----------
    df : pd.DataFrame
    feature_cols : list[str]
        Column names to impute.  Columns absent from *df* are skipped.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Copy of *df* with imputed values and added ``*_missing`` columns.

    Notes
    -----
    Do NOT apply imputation to sequence features — they are always available.
    Apply only to chromatin features (AlphaGenome and ENCODE columns).
    """
    result = df.copy()

    for col in feature_cols:
        if col not in result.columns:
            logger.debug("impute_chromatin_features: column %r not in DataFrame, skipping", col)
            continue

        is_nan = result[col].isna()
        result[col + MISSING_INDICATOR_SUFFIX] = is_nan.astype(int)

        if not is_nan.any():
            continue  # No NaN values — nothing to impute.

        median_val = result[col].median()  # pandas median() ignores NaN.

        if pd.isna(median_val):
            logger.warning(
                "Feature %r is entirely NaN (e.g., no tracks for this cell type); "
                "imputing with 0.0",
                col,
            )
            median_val = 0.0

        n_imputed = int(is_nan.sum())
        logger.info(
            "Imputing %d / %d NaN values in %r with median=%.4f",
            n_imputed, len(result), col, median_val,
        )
        result[col] = result[col].fillna(median_val)

    return result


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Master assembly function
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def build_feature_matrix(
    candidates: pd.DataFrame,
    positives: pd.DataFrame,
    *,
    cell_type: str,
    encode_manifest: Optional[Any] = None,
    ag_model: Optional[Any] = None,
    ag_ontology_term: Optional[str] = None,
    ag_cache_dir: Optional[Path] = None,
    window_bp: int = DEFAULT_WINDOW_BP,
    match_window_bp: int = DEFAULT_MATCH_WINDOW_BP,
    output_path: Optional[Path] = None,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Assemble the full feature matrix for model training and evaluation.

    Orchestrates label assignment, sequence feature extraction, AlphaGenome
    chromatin prediction, ENCODE experimental chromatin extraction, and NaN
    imputation into a single training-ready DataFrame.

    Parameters
    ----------
    candidates : pd.DataFrame
        All candidate off-target sites (both future positives and negatives).
        Required columns: ``guide_id``, ``guide_seq``, ``chrom``, ``start``
        (0-based), ``strand``, ``off_target_seq``, ``pam_seq``,
        ``num_mismatches``.
    positives : pd.DataFrame
        Confirmed positive off-target sites from GUIDE-seq / CHANGE-seq.
        Required columns: ``guide_id``, ``chrom``, ``start`` (0-based).
    cell_type : str
        Human-readable cell-type name, e.g. ``"K562"``.  Used for ENCODE
        BigWig lookup and stored in the output ``cell_type`` metadata column.
    encode_manifest : BigWigManifest | None
        ENCODE BigWig manifest from :func:`~src.encode_client.build_manifest`.
        Pass ``None`` to skip experimental chromatin features (Model A or C).
    ag_model : DnaClient | None
        Pre-initialised AlphaGenome client.  Pass ``None`` to skip AlphaGenome
        chromatin features (Model A or B).
    ag_ontology_term : str | None
        AlphaGenome cell-type ontology term, e.g. ``"EFO:0002067"`` for K562.
        Required when *ag_model* is provided.
    ag_cache_dir : Path | None
        Cache directory for AlphaGenome API responses.  Required when
        *ag_model* is provided.
    window_bp : int
        Half-width of chromatin feature extraction window in bp (default 500).
    match_window_bp : int
        Maximum distance (bp) for matching a candidate to an experimental
        positive.  Default 5 bp.
    output_path : Path | None
        If given, save the assembled DataFrame as a parquet file at this path
        (parent directories are created automatically).

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Feature matrix with columns:

        - Metadata: ``guide_id``, ``chrom``, ``start``, ``strand``,
          ``guide_seq``, ``off_target_seq``, ``pam_seq``, ``cell_type``,
          ``dataset``, ``label``
        - Sequence features: :data:`SEQUENCE_FEATURE_NAMES`
        - AlphaGenome features (if *ag_model* provided): :data:`AG_FEATURE_NAMES`
          + ``{feat}_missing`` indicator columns
        - ENCODE features (if *encode_manifest* provided): :data:`EXP_FEATURE_NAMES`
          + ``{feat}_missing`` indicator columns

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If required columns are absent from *candidates* or *positives*, or
        if *ag_model* is provided without *ag_ontology_term*.
    """
    # ---- Validate inputs --------------------------------------------------
    _require_columns(candidates, _REQUIRED_CANDIDATE_COLS, "candidates")
    _require_columns(positives, _REQUIRED_POSITIVE_COLS, "positives")

    if ag_model is not None and ag_ontology_term is None:
        raise ValueError(
            "ag_ontology_term is required when ag_model is provided. "
            "Example: ag_ontology_term='EFO:0002067' for K562."
        )
    if ag_model is not None and ag_cache_dir is None:
        raise ValueError("ag_cache_dir is required when ag_model is provided.")

    logger.info(
        "build_feature_matrix: %d candidates, %d positives, cell_type=%s",
        len(candidates), len(positives), cell_type,
    )

    # ---- Step 1: Remove on-target sites (num_mismatches == 0) ------------
    n_before = len(candidates)
    work = candidates[candidates["num_mismatches"] > 0].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "remove on-target sites (num_mismatches == 0)", n_before, len(work))

    if len(work) == 0:
        logger.error("No candidates remain after on-target removal — check input data.")
        return work

    # ---- Step 2: Label candidates -----------------------------------------
    work = label_candidates(work, positives, match_window_bp=match_window_bp)

    n_pos = int(work["label"].sum())
    n_neg = len(work) - n_pos
    if n_pos == 0:
        logger.warning(
            "Zero positive candidates after label assignment. "
            "Check coordinate systems and guide_id mapping."
        )
    elif n_pos / len(work) > 0.50:
        logger.warning(
            "Positive rate is %.1f%% (>50%%) — match window may be too large.",
            100.0 * n_pos / len(work),
        )
    logger.info(
        "Class balance: %d positive (%.1f%%), %d negative",
        n_pos, 100.0 * n_pos / len(work), n_neg,
    )

    # ---- Step 3: Sequence features ----------------------------------------
    logger.info("Extracting sequence features for %d candidates ...", len(work))
    work = extract_sequence_features_batch(work)

    # ---- Step 4: AlphaGenome chromatin features ----------------------------
    if ag_model is not None:
        logger.info(
            "Extracting AlphaGenome features for %d sites, ontology=%s ...",
            len(work), ag_ontology_term,
        )
        work = _ag_mod.batch_get_chromatin_features(
            work,
            cell_type=ag_ontology_term,
            model=ag_model,
            cache_dir=Path(ag_cache_dir),
            window_bp=window_bp,
        )
        ag_cols = [c for c in AG_FEATURE_NAMES if c in work.columns]
        work = impute_chromatin_features(work, ag_cols)

    # ---- Step 5: ENCODE experimental chromatin features -------------------
    if encode_manifest is not None:
        logger.info(
            "Extracting ENCODE features for %d sites, cell_type=%s ...",
            len(work), cell_type,
        )
        work = _enc_mod.batch_get_chromatin_features(
            work,
            cell_type=cell_type,
            manifest=encode_manifest,
            window_bp=window_bp,
        )
        exp_cols = [c for c in EXP_FEATURE_NAMES if c in work.columns]
        work = impute_chromatin_features(work, exp_cols)

    # ---- Step 6: Add metadata columns if not already present --------------
    if "cell_type" not in work.columns:
        work["cell_type"] = cell_type
    if "dataset" not in work.columns:
        work["dataset"] = "unknown"

    # ---- Step 7: Summary log ---------------------------------------------
    logger.info(
        "Feature matrix complete: %d rows × %d columns | "
        "%d positive (%.1f%%) | %d negative",
        len(work), len(work.columns),
        n_pos, 100.0 * n_pos / len(work) if len(work) else 0.0,
        n_neg,
    )

    # ---- Step 8: Optional save -------------------------------------------
    if output_path is not None:
        output_path = Path(output_path)
        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        work.to_parquet(output_path, index=False)
        logger.info("Saved feature matrix → %s", output_path)

    return work


=== src/model.py ===
"""Model training, cross-validation, and evaluation for SafeGenome.

Three XGBoost models are compared using leave-one-guide-out cross-validation:

- **Model A** (sequence-only baseline): trained on all datasets using only
  sequence-derived features (CFD score, mismatch positions/types, GC content,
  etc.).  Uses the full dataset for maximum baseline strength.
- **Model B** (sequence + experimental chromatin — gold standard): trained on
  K562 + HEK293 only, using sequence features plus ENCODE experimental
  chromatin signals (DNase-seq, ChIP-seq histone marks).
- **Model C** (sequence + AlphaGenome predicted chromatin — our method): same
  cell-type subset as Model B, using sequence features plus AlphaGenome-
  predicted chromatin features.

Critical protocol enforced here
---------------------------------
1. **Leave-one-guide-out CV** via ``StratifiedGroupKFold`` grouped by
   ``guide_id``.  Sites from the same guide must never split across train/test
   — they share sequence-level properties that would cause data leakage.
2. **Average Precision (AUPRC)** is the primary metric.  ROC-AUC is computed
   as a secondary metric but is misleadingly optimistic given the severe class
   imbalance (typically 1–5% positives).
3. **scale_pos_weight** is set to ``n_neg / n_pos`` in each XGBoost model to
   compensate for class imbalance.
4. **random_state=42** used everywhere for reproducibility.
5. **On-target sites must already be removed** from the feature matrix before
   calling any function here (enforced via a ValueError in
   ``prepare_model_data``).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Optional

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from scipy import stats
from sklearn.metrics import (
    average_precision_score,
    precision_recall_curve,
    roc_auc_score,
)
from sklearn.model_selection import StratifiedGroupKFold
import xgboost as xgb

from src.feature_matrix import get_model_features
from src.utils import log_filter

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Constants
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#: Default cell types for Models B and C (where chromatin data exists).
MODEL_BC_CELL_TYPES: list[str] = ["K562", "HEK293"]

#: Default number of CV folds.
DEFAULT_N_SPLITS: int = 5

#: Top fraction used for precision/recall/enrichment metrics.
TOP_FRACTION: float = 0.10

#: Default XGBoost parameters shared by all three models.
DEFAULT_XGB_PARAMS: dict = {
    "n_estimators": 200,
    "max_depth": 6,
    "learning_rate": 0.1,
    "subsample": 0.8,
    "colsample_bytree": 0.8,
    "eval_metric": "aucpr",
    "verbosity": 0,
    "random_state": 42,
}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Metric helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def compute_scale_pos_weight(y: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """Compute XGBoost ``scale_pos_weight`` as ``n_neg / n_pos``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    y : np.ndarray
        Binary label array (0 or 1).

    Returns
    -------
    float
        ``n_neg / n_pos``.  Returns 1.0 if there are no positives.
    """
    n_pos = int((y == 1).sum())
    n_neg = int((y == 0).sum())
    if n_pos == 0:
        logger.warning("compute_scale_pos_weight: no positives found; returning 1.0")
        return 1.0
    return float(n_neg) / float(n_pos)


def precision_at_k(y_true: np.ndarray, y_score: np.ndarray, k: int) -> float:
    """Precision among the top-k scored predictions.

    Parameters
    ----------
    y_true : np.ndarray
        Binary ground truth labels.
    y_score : np.ndarray
        Predicted scores (higher = more likely positive).
    k : int
        Number of top predictions to evaluate.

    Returns
    -------
    float
        Fraction of top-k predictions that are true positives.
    """
    k = min(k, len(y_true))
    if k == 0:
        return 0.0
    top_k_idx = np.argsort(y_score)[::-1][:k]
    return float(y_true[top_k_idx].sum()) / k


def recall_at_k(y_true: np.ndarray, y_score: np.ndarray, k: int) -> float:
    """Recall among the top-k scored predictions.

    Parameters
    ----------
    y_true : np.ndarray
        Binary ground truth labels.
    y_score : np.ndarray
        Predicted scores (higher = more likely positive).
    k : int
        Number of top predictions to evaluate.

    Returns
    -------
    float
        Fraction of all true positives recovered in the top-k predictions.
        Returns 0.0 if there are no positives.
    """
    n_pos = int(y_true.sum())
    if n_pos == 0:
        return 0.0
    k = min(k, len(y_true))
    top_k_idx = np.argsort(y_score)[::-1][:k]
    return float(y_true[top_k_idx].sum()) / n_pos


def enrichment_at_top_fraction(
    y_true: np.ndarray,
    y_score: np.ndarray,
    fraction: float = TOP_FRACTION,
) -> float:
    """Enrichment of true positives in the top-fraction of scored predictions.

    Enrichment = (observed positive rate in top fraction) / (overall positive
    rate).  A value of 1.0 indicates no enrichment (random).  2.0 means 2×
    enrichment.

    Parameters
    ----------
    y_true : np.ndarray
        Binary ground truth labels.
    y_score : np.ndarray
        Predicted scores (higher = more likely positive).
    fraction : float
        Top fraction to evaluate (default 0.10 = top 10%).

    Returns
    -------
    float
        Enrichment factor.  Returns 0.0 if overall positive rate is 0.
    """
    n_total = len(y_true)
    if n_total == 0:
        return 0.0
    overall_rate = float(y_true.mean())
    if overall_rate == 0.0:
        return 0.0
    k = max(1, int(np.round(fraction * n_total)))
    top_rate = precision_at_k(y_true, y_score, k)
    return top_rate / overall_rate


def compute_metrics(
    y_true: np.ndarray,
    y_score: np.ndarray,
    *,
    top_fraction: float = TOP_FRACTION,
) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Compute the full set of evaluation metrics for one fold.

    Parameters
    ----------
    y_true : np.ndarray
        Binary ground truth labels (0 or 1).
    y_score : np.ndarray
        Predicted scores (higher = more likely positive).
    top_fraction : float
        Fraction of top predictions for precision/recall/enrichment metrics.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, float]
        Keys: ``auprc``, ``auroc``, ``precision_at_top``, ``recall_at_top``,
        ``enrichment_at_top``.  Any metric that cannot be computed (e.g., only
        one class present) is returned as ``float("nan")``.
    """
    metrics: dict[str, float] = {}
    k = max(1, int(np.round(top_fraction * len(y_true))))

    n_pos = int(y_true.sum())
    if n_pos == 0 or n_pos == len(y_true):
        logger.warning(
            "compute_metrics: only one class present (n_pos=%d, n_total=%d); "
            "AUPRC and AUROC set to NaN",
            n_pos, len(y_true),
        )
        metrics["auprc"] = float("nan")
        metrics["auroc"] = float("nan")
    else:
        metrics["auprc"] = float(average_precision_score(y_true, y_score))
        metrics["auroc"] = float(roc_auc_score(y_true, y_score))

    metrics["precision_at_top"] = precision_at_k(y_true, y_score, k)
    metrics["recall_at_top"] = recall_at_k(y_true, y_score, k)
    metrics["enrichment_at_top"] = enrichment_at_top_fraction(y_true, y_score, top_fraction)
    return metrics


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Result dataclasses
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass
class FoldMetrics:
    """Metrics from a single cross-validation fold."""

    fold: int
    n_train: int
    n_test: int
    n_pos_test: int
    auprc: float
    auroc: float
    precision_at_top: float
    recall_at_top: float
    enrichment_at_top: float
    test_guide_ids: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)


@dataclass
class CVResult:
    """Results from leave-one-guide-out cross-validation for one model type.

    Attributes
    ----------
    model_type : str
        One of ``"A"``, ``"B"``, or ``"C"`` (or an ablation label).
    feature_cols : list[str]
        Feature columns used for this model.
    folds : list[FoldMetrics]
        Per-fold evaluation metrics.
    oof_y_true : np.ndarray
        Concatenated out-of-fold ground truth labels.
    oof_y_score : np.ndarray
        Concatenated out-of-fold predicted scores.
    final_model : object
        XGBClassifier trained on all data (for SHAP / cell-type transfer).
    mean_auprc : float
        Mean AUPRC across valid (non-NaN) folds.
    std_auprc : float
        Standard deviation of AUPRC across valid folds.
    mean_auroc : float
        Mean AUROC across valid folds.
    std_auroc : float
        Standard deviation of AUROC across valid folds.
    """

    model_type: str
    feature_cols: list[str]
    folds: list[FoldMetrics]
    oof_y_true: np.ndarray
    oof_y_score: np.ndarray
    final_model: object  # xgb.XGBClassifier
    mean_auprc: float
    std_auprc: float
    mean_auroc: float
    std_auroc: float


@dataclass
class ModelComparison:
    """Statistical comparison between two CVResult objects.

    Attributes
    ----------
    model_a_type : str
        Label for the reference model.
    model_b_type : str
        Label for the model being compared.
    delta_auprc : float
        ``mean_auprc(b) − mean_auprc(a)``; positive means model B is better.
    p_value : float
        Two-sided paired t-test p-value (backward-compat alias).
    significant : bool
        True if any two-sided p-value < 0.05.
    n_paired : int
        Number of valid (non-NaN) paired folds used in the tests.
    ttest_p_one_sided : float
        One-sided paired t-test p-value (H1: model_b > model_a).
    wilcoxon_p_two_sided : float
        Two-sided Wilcoxon signed-rank p-value.
    wilcoxon_p_one_sided : float
        One-sided Wilcoxon signed-rank p-value (H1: model_b > model_a).
    """

    model_a_type: str
    model_b_type: str
    delta_auprc: float
    p_value: float
    significant: bool
    n_paired: int = 0
    ttest_p_one_sided: float = float("nan")
    wilcoxon_p_two_sided: float = float("nan")
    wilcoxon_p_one_sided: float = float("nan")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Data preparation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def prepare_model_data(
    feature_matrix: pd.DataFrame,
    model_type: str,
    *,
    cell_types: Optional[list[str]] = None,
    exclude_datasets: Optional[list[str]] = None,
    include_missingness_indicators: bool = False,
) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray, list[str]]:
    """Filter the feature matrix and return arrays ready for model training.

    Parameters
    ----------
    feature_matrix : pd.DataFrame
        Full feature matrix from ``build_feature_matrix``.  Must contain
        ``guide_id``, ``label``, and all relevant feature columns.
    model_type : str
        One of ``"A"``, ``"B"``, ``"C"`` (case-insensitive).
    cell_types : list[str] | None
        If provided, keep only rows where ``cell_type`` is in this list.
        ``None`` keeps all rows.
    exclude_datasets : list[str] | None
        If provided, drop rows where ``dataset`` is in this list.  Useful for
        sensitivity analysis (e.g. ``exclude_datasets=["deepcrispr_k562"]``).
    include_missingness_indicators : bool
        If ``True``, append ``{feature}_missing`` indicator columns to the
        feature set for chromatin features in Models B and C.

    Returns
    -------
    X : np.ndarray of shape (n_samples, n_features)
        Feature matrix (float32).
    y : np.ndarray of shape (n_samples,)
        Binary labels (int32).
    groups : np.ndarray of shape (n_samples,)
        Guide IDs for ``StratifiedGroupKFold`` splitting.
    feature_cols : list[str]
        Feature column names in the same order as X columns.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If on-target sites (``num_mismatches == 0``) are detected, if
        required columns are missing, or if no rows remain after filtering.
    """
    mt = model_type.strip().upper()
    if mt not in {"A", "B", "C"}:
        raise ValueError(f"model_type must be 'A', 'B', or 'C', got {model_type!r}")

    # Guard: on-targets must have been removed upstream.
    if "num_mismatches" in feature_matrix.columns:
        n_ontarget = int((feature_matrix["num_mismatches"] == 0).sum())
        if n_ontarget > 0:
            raise ValueError(
                f"prepare_model_data: {n_ontarget} on-target site(s) "
                "(num_mismatches == 0) detected. Remove them with "
                "build_feature_matrix before calling this function."
            )

    for col in ("guide_id", "label"):
        if col not in feature_matrix.columns:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Feature matrix is missing required column {col!r}."
            )

    df = feature_matrix.copy()
    n_start = len(df)

    # --- Exclude specified datasets ---
    if exclude_datasets:
        if "dataset" in df.columns:
            mask = df["dataset"].isin(exclude_datasets)
            n_excluded = int(mask.sum())
            log_filter(
                logger,
                f"exclude_datasets={exclude_datasets}",
                n_start,
                n_start - n_excluded,
            )
            df = df[~mask]
        else:
            logger.warning(
                "exclude_datasets specified but 'dataset' column not present; ignoring"
            )

    # --- Filter by cell type ---
    if cell_types is not None:
        if "cell_type" in df.columns:
            n_before = len(df)
            mask = df["cell_type"].isin(cell_types)
            log_filter(
                logger,
                f"cell_types={cell_types}",
                n_before,
                int(mask.sum()),
            )
            df = df[mask]
        else:
            logger.warning(
                "cell_types specified but 'cell_type' column not present; ignoring"
            )

    if len(df) == 0:
        raise ValueError(
            f"prepare_model_data: no rows remain after filtering for model {mt}. "
            "Check cell_types and exclude_datasets arguments."
        )

    # --- Select feature columns ---
    feature_cols = get_model_features(
        mt, include_missingness_indicators=include_missingness_indicators
    )

    missing_cols = [c for c in feature_cols if c not in df.columns]
    if missing_cols:
        logger.warning(
            "Model %s: %d feature column(s) not found in matrix and will be "
            "excluded: %s%s",
            mt,
            len(missing_cols),
            missing_cols[:5],
            " ..." if len(missing_cols) > 5 else "",
        )
    feature_cols = [c for c in feature_cols if c in df.columns]

    if not feature_cols:
        raise ValueError(
            f"prepare_model_data: no feature columns found for model {mt}. "
            "Ensure the feature matrix contains the appropriate feature columns."
        )

    X = df[feature_cols].to_numpy(dtype=np.float32)
    y = df["label"].to_numpy(dtype=np.int32)
    groups = df["guide_id"].to_numpy()

    n_pos = int(y.sum())
    logger.info(
        "prepare_model_data (Model %s): %d rows, %d features, "
        "%d positives (%.1f%%), %d guides",
        mt, len(df), len(feature_cols),
        n_pos, 100.0 * n_pos / len(df),
        len(np.unique(groups)),
    )

    return X, y, groups, feature_cols


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# XGBoost helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def make_xgb_classifier(
    scale_pos_weight: float,
    *,
    n_estimators: int = 200,
    max_depth: int = 6,
    learning_rate: float = 0.1,
    subsample: float = 0.8,
    colsample_bytree: float = 0.8,
    random_state: int = 42,
    extra_params: Optional[dict] = None,
) -> xgb.XGBClassifier:
    """Construct an ``XGBClassifier`` with SafeGenome defaults.

    Parameters
    ----------
    scale_pos_weight : float
        Class imbalance weight (``n_neg / n_pos``).
    n_estimators : int
        Number of boosting rounds.
    max_depth : int
        Maximum tree depth.
    learning_rate : float
        Step-size shrinkage.
    subsample : float
        Subsample ratio of training instances per tree.
    colsample_bytree : float
        Subsample ratio of features per tree.
    random_state : int
        Random seed (``random_state=42`` everywhere for reproducibility).
    extra_params : dict | None
        Additional keyword arguments passed to ``XGBClassifier``, overriding
        any of the above defaults.

    Returns
    -------
    xgb.XGBClassifier
    """
    params: dict = {
        "n_estimators": n_estimators,
        "max_depth": max_depth,
        "learning_rate": learning_rate,
        "subsample": subsample,
        "colsample_bytree": colsample_bytree,
        "scale_pos_weight": scale_pos_weight,
        "eval_metric": "aucpr",
        "verbosity": 0,
        "random_state": random_state,
    }
    if extra_params:
        params.update(extra_params)
    return xgb.XGBClassifier(**params)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Cross-validation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def cross_validate_model(
    X: np.ndarray,
    y: np.ndarray,
    groups: np.ndarray,
    *,
    model_type: str,
    feature_cols: list[str],
    n_splits: int = DEFAULT_N_SPLITS,
    random_state: int = 42,
    xgb_extra_params: Optional[dict] = None,
    top_fraction: float = TOP_FRACTION,
) -> CVResult:
    """Run leave-one-guide-out cross-validation for one model type.

    Uses ``StratifiedGroupKFold`` to ensure:

    1. Sites from the same guide are never split across train/test.
    2. Label distribution is approximately preserved across folds.

    A final model is also trained on all data for SHAP analysis and cell-type
    transfer predictions.

    Parameters
    ----------
    X : np.ndarray
        Feature matrix of shape ``(n_samples, n_features)``.
    y : np.ndarray
        Binary labels of shape ``(n_samples,)``.
    groups : np.ndarray
        Guide IDs (same length as ``y``) for group-based splitting.
    model_type : str
        Label for this model (``"A"``, ``"B"``, or ``"C"``).
    feature_cols : list[str]
        Names of columns in ``X`` (same order).
    n_splits : int
        Number of CV folds.  Automatically reduced if fewer unique guides
        are present.
    random_state : int
        Random seed.
    xgb_extra_params : dict | None
        Additional parameters forwarded to ``make_xgb_classifier``.
    top_fraction : float
        Fraction of top-scored sites for precision/recall/enrichment.

    Returns
    -------
    CVResult
    """
    n_unique_groups = len(np.unique(groups))
    if n_splits > n_unique_groups:
        logger.warning(
            "cross_validate_model (Model %s): n_splits=%d > n_unique_guides=%d; "
            "reducing n_splits to %d",
            model_type, n_splits, n_unique_groups, n_unique_groups,
        )
        n_splits = n_unique_groups

    scale_pos_weight = compute_scale_pos_weight(y)
    n_pos = int(y.sum())
    n_neg = len(y) - n_pos
    logger.info(
        "cross_validate_model (Model %s): %d samples, %d features, "
        "%d positives, %d negatives, scale_pos_weight=%.2f, n_splits=%d",
        model_type, len(y), len(feature_cols),
        n_pos, n_neg, scale_pos_weight, n_splits,
    )

    cv = StratifiedGroupKFold(n_splits=n_splits, shuffle=True, random_state=random_state)
    fold_metrics_list: list[FoldMetrics] = []
    oof_y_true_parts: list[np.ndarray] = []
    oof_y_score_parts: list[np.ndarray] = []

    for fold_idx, (train_idx, test_idx) in enumerate(cv.split(X, y, groups=groups)):
        X_train, X_test = X[train_idx], X[test_idx]
        y_train, y_test = y[train_idx], y[test_idx]
        test_guide_ids_in_fold = sorted(set(str(g) for g in groups[test_idx]))

        fold_spw = compute_scale_pos_weight(y_train)
        clf = make_xgb_classifier(
            fold_spw,
            random_state=random_state,
            extra_params=xgb_extra_params,
        )
        clf.fit(X_train, y_train)
        y_score = clf.predict_proba(X_test)[:, 1]

        fold_m = compute_metrics(y_test, y_score, top_fraction=top_fraction)
        n_pos_test = int(y_test.sum())

        fold_metrics_list.append(FoldMetrics(
            fold=fold_idx,
            n_train=len(y_train),
            n_test=len(y_test),
            n_pos_test=n_pos_test,
            auprc=fold_m["auprc"],
            auroc=fold_m["auroc"],
            precision_at_top=fold_m["precision_at_top"],
            recall_at_top=fold_m["recall_at_top"],
            enrichment_at_top=fold_m["enrichment_at_top"],
            test_guide_ids=test_guide_ids_in_fold,
        ))
        oof_y_true_parts.append(y_test)
        oof_y_score_parts.append(y_score)

        logger.info(
            "  Fold %d [%s]: n_test=%d (%d pos), AUPRC=%.4f, AUROC=%.4f",
            fold_idx, ",".join(test_guide_ids_in_fold),
            len(y_test), n_pos_test,
            fold_m["auprc"], fold_m["auroc"],
        )

    # Aggregate across non-NaN folds.
    auprcs = [fm.auprc for fm in fold_metrics_list if not np.isnan(fm.auprc)]
    aurocs = [fm.auroc for fm in fold_metrics_list if not np.isnan(fm.auroc)]
    mean_auprc = float(np.mean(auprcs)) if auprcs else float("nan")
    std_auprc = float(np.std(auprcs)) if len(auprcs) > 1 else 0.0
    mean_auroc = float(np.mean(aurocs)) if aurocs else float("nan")
    std_auroc = float(np.std(aurocs)) if len(aurocs) > 1 else 0.0

    logger.info(
        "Model %s CV complete: mean_AUPRC=%.4f±%.4f, mean_AUROC=%.4f±%.4f",
        model_type, mean_auprc, std_auprc, mean_auroc, std_auroc,
    )

    # Train final model on all data for downstream use.
    final_clf = make_xgb_classifier(
        scale_pos_weight, random_state=random_state, extra_params=xgb_extra_params
    )
    final_clf.fit(X, y)

    return CVResult(
        model_type=model_type,
        feature_cols=list(feature_cols),
        folds=fold_metrics_list,
        oof_y_true=np.concatenate(oof_y_true_parts),
        oof_y_score=np.concatenate(oof_y_score_parts),
        final_model=final_clf,
        mean_auprc=mean_auprc,
        std_auprc=std_auprc,
        mean_auroc=mean_auroc,
        std_auroc=std_auroc,
    )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Statistical comparison
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def compare_models(result_a: CVResult, result_b: CVResult) -> ModelComparison:
    """Statistical comparison of per-fold AUPRC between two CV results.

    When both results have ``test_guide_ids`` populated (LOGO CV), folds are
    matched by guide ID so that models trained on different subsets can be
    compared on their overlapping guides.  Otherwise falls back to fold-index
    pairing.

    Computes paired t-test and Wilcoxon signed-rank test, each two-sided and
    one-sided (H1: result_b > result_a).

    Parameters
    ----------
    result_a : CVResult
        Reference model (typically Model A or Model B).
    result_b : CVResult
        Model to compare (typically Model C).

    Returns
    -------
    ModelComparison
    """
    # --- Build guide → auprc mapping when available ---
    a_has_guides = all(fm.test_guide_ids for fm in result_a.folds)
    b_has_guides = all(fm.test_guide_ids for fm in result_b.folds)

    if a_has_guides and b_has_guides:
        a_map: dict[str, float] = {}
        for fm in result_a.folds:
            for gid in fm.test_guide_ids:
                a_map[gid] = fm.auprc
        b_map: dict[str, float] = {}
        for fm in result_b.folds:
            for gid in fm.test_guide_ids:
                b_map[gid] = fm.auprc
        shared = sorted(set(a_map) & set(b_map))
        auprcs_a = np.array([a_map[g] for g in shared])
        auprcs_b = np.array([b_map[g] for g in shared])
    else:
        auprcs_a = np.array([fm.auprc for fm in result_a.folds])
        auprcs_b = np.array([fm.auprc for fm in result_b.folds])

    # Drop NaN pairs
    valid = ~(np.isnan(auprcs_a) | np.isnan(auprcs_b))
    a_valid = auprcs_a[valid]
    b_valid = auprcs_b[valid]
    n_paired = len(a_valid)

    delta_auprc = (
        float(np.mean(b_valid) - np.mean(a_valid)) if n_paired > 0 else float("nan")
    )

    # --- Paired t-test ---
    if n_paired < 2:
        logger.warning(
            "compare_models: fewer than 2 valid fold pairs (%d); tests unreliable",
            n_paired,
        )
        ttest_two = ttest_one = float("nan")
    else:
        _, ttest_two = stats.ttest_rel(a_valid, b_valid)
        _, ttest_one = stats.ttest_rel(a_valid, b_valid, alternative="less")
        ttest_two = float(ttest_two)
        ttest_one = float(ttest_one)

    # --- Wilcoxon signed-rank test ---
    diffs = b_valid - a_valid
    n_nonzero = int(np.count_nonzero(diffs))
    if n_nonzero < 6:
        logger.info(
            "compare_models (%s vs %s): only %d non-zero differences; "
            "Wilcoxon unreliable — reporting NaN",
            result_a.model_type, result_b.model_type, n_nonzero,
        )
        wilcoxon_two = wilcoxon_one = float("nan")
    else:
        _, wilcoxon_two = stats.wilcoxon(diffs)
        _, wilcoxon_one = stats.wilcoxon(diffs, alternative="greater")
        wilcoxon_two = float(wilcoxon_two)
        wilcoxon_one = float(wilcoxon_one)

    significant = any(
        (not np.isnan(p)) and p < 0.05
        for p in [ttest_two, wilcoxon_two]
    )

    logger.info(
        "compare_models (%s vs %s): delta=%.4f, n_paired=%d | "
        "ttest 2s=%.4f 1s=%.4f | wilcoxon 2s=%.4f 1s=%.4f | sig=%s",
        result_a.model_type, result_b.model_type,
        delta_auprc, n_paired,
        ttest_two, ttest_one,
        wilcoxon_two, wilcoxon_one,
        significant,
    )

    return ModelComparison(
        model_a_type=result_a.model_type,
        model_b_type=result_b.model_type,
        delta_auprc=delta_auprc,
        p_value=ttest_two,
        significant=significant,
        n_paired=n_paired,
        ttest_p_one_sided=ttest_one,
        wilcoxon_p_two_sided=wilcoxon_two,
        wilcoxon_p_one_sided=wilcoxon_one,
    )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# SHAP analysis
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def compute_shap_values(
    model: xgb.XGBClassifier,
    X: np.ndarray,
    feature_cols: list[str],
) -> tuple[np.ndarray, pd.DataFrame]:
    """Compute SHAP values for the trained model.

    Parameters
    ----------
    model : xgb.XGBClassifier
        Trained XGBoost classifier.
    X : np.ndarray
        Feature matrix of shape ``(n_samples, n_features)``.
    feature_cols : list[str]
        Feature column names (same order as X columns).

    Returns
    -------
    shap_values : np.ndarray of shape (n_samples, n_features)
        SHAP values for each sample and feature (positive-class).
    mean_abs_shap : pd.DataFrame
        DataFrame with columns ``feature`` and ``mean_abs_shap``, sorted
        descending by mean absolute SHAP value.

    Raises
    ------
    ImportError
        If the ``shap`` package is not installed.
    """
    try:
        import shap
    except ImportError:
        raise ImportError(
            "SHAP is required for compute_shap_values. "
            "Install with: pip install shap>=0.42"
        )

    explainer = shap.TreeExplainer(model)
    shap_values = explainer.shap_values(X)

    # Handle both legacy (list of arrays) and modern (single array) shap APIs.
    if isinstance(shap_values, list):
        shap_values = shap_values[1]  # positive-class SHAP values

    mean_abs = np.abs(shap_values).mean(axis=0)
    importance_df = (
        pd.DataFrame({"feature": feature_cols, "mean_abs_shap": mean_abs})
        .sort_values("mean_abs_shap", ascending=False)
        .reset_index(drop=True)
    )

    return shap_values, importance_df


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# PR curve plotting
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def plot_pr_curves(
    cv_results: dict[str, CVResult],
    *,
    output_path: Optional[Path] = None,
    title: str = "Precision-Recall Curves — SafeGenome Three-Model Comparison",
    figsize: tuple[float, float] = (7.0, 6.0),
):
    """Plot pooled out-of-fold PR curves for Models A, B, and C.

    Uses concatenated OOF predictions across all folds.  Also plots a dashed
    random-classifier baseline at the dataset's positive rate.

    Parameters
    ----------
    cv_results : dict[str, CVResult]
        Mapping from model label (``"A"``, ``"B"``, ``"C"``) to CVResult.
    output_path : Path | None
        If provided, save the figure here (PNG or SVG based on suffix).
    title : str
        Figure title.
    figsize : tuple[float, float]
        Figure size in inches (width, height).

    Returns
    -------
    matplotlib.figure.Figure

    Raises
    ------
    ImportError
        If matplotlib is not installed.
    """
    try:
        import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    except ImportError:
        raise ImportError(
            "matplotlib is required for plot_pr_curves. "
            "Install with: pip install matplotlib>=3.7"
        )

    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=figsize)

    _COLORS = {"A": "#1f77b4", "B": "#2ca02c", "C": "#ff7f0e"}
    _LINESTYLES = {"A": "--", "B": "-", "C": "-"}
    _LABELS = {
        "A": "Model A — Sequence only",
        "B": "Model B — Sequence + Experimental chromatin",
        "C": "Model C — Sequence + AlphaGenome (ours)",
    }

    for model_label, result in cv_results.items():
        y_true = result.oof_y_true
        y_score = result.oof_y_score
        if len(y_true) == 0 or int(y_true.sum()) == 0:
            logger.warning(
                "plot_pr_curves: Model %s has no positives in OOF — skipping", model_label
            )
            continue
        precision, recall, _ = precision_recall_curve(y_true, y_score)
        auprc = float(average_precision_score(y_true, y_score))
        color = _COLORS.get(model_label, "gray")
        ls = _LINESTYLES.get(model_label, "-")
        label = _LABELS.get(model_label, f"Model {model_label}")
        ax.plot(
            recall, precision,
            color=color, linestyle=ls, linewidth=2,
            label=f"{label} (AUPRC={auprc:.3f})",
        )

    # Random baseline.
    if cv_results:
        any_result = next(iter(cv_results.values()))
        baseline = float(any_result.oof_y_true.mean())
        ax.axhline(
            baseline, color="gray", linestyle=":", linewidth=1.0,
            label=f"Random (P={baseline:.3f})",
        )

    ax.set_xlabel("Recall", fontsize=12)
    ax.set_ylabel("Precision", fontsize=12)
    ax.set_title(title, fontsize=13)
    ax.legend(loc="upper right", fontsize=9)
    ax.set_xlim([0.0, 1.0])
    ax.set_ylim([0.0, 1.05])
    fig.tight_layout()

    if output_path is not None:
        output_path = Path(output_path)
        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        fig.savefig(output_path, dpi=150, bbox_inches="tight")
        logger.info("PR curve figure saved → %s", output_path)

    return fig


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Ablation analysis
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run_ablation(
    X: np.ndarray,
    y: np.ndarray,
    groups: np.ndarray,
    feature_cols: list[str],
    *,
    feature_subsets: dict[str, list[str]],
    n_splits: int = DEFAULT_N_SPLITS,
    random_state: int = 42,
    xgb_extra_params: Optional[dict] = None,
) -> dict[str, dict[str, float]]:
    """Run ablation analysis across a set of feature subsets.

    Trains a separate leave-one-guide-out CV for each feature subset and
    returns mean AUPRC and AUROC per subset.  Useful for understanding which
    AlphaGenome chromatin feature types drive performance (DNase vs histone
    marks vs CTCF etc.).

    Parameters
    ----------
    X : np.ndarray
        Full feature matrix ``(n_samples, n_features)``.
    y : np.ndarray
        Binary labels.
    groups : np.ndarray
        Guide IDs for GroupKFold.
    feature_cols : list[str]
        Full list of feature column names (same order as X columns).
    feature_subsets : dict[str, list[str]]
        Mapping from subset name to the feature names to include.  Names not
        present in *feature_cols* are silently ignored.
    n_splits : int
        Number of CV folds.
    random_state : int
        Random seed.
    xgb_extra_params : dict | None
        Extra XGBoost parameters.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, dict[str, float]]
        Mapping from subset name to
        ``{"mean_auprc", "std_auprc", "mean_auroc", "std_auroc"}``.
    """
    col_to_idx = {col: i for i, col in enumerate(feature_cols)}
    results: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}

    for subset_name, subset_cols in feature_subsets.items():
        valid_cols = [c for c in subset_cols if c in col_to_idx]
        if not valid_cols:
            logger.warning(
                "run_ablation: subset %r has no valid columns — skipping", subset_name
            )
            continue

        col_indices = [col_to_idx[c] for c in valid_cols]
        X_sub = X[:, col_indices]

        cv_result = cross_validate_model(
            X_sub, y, groups,
            model_type=f"ablation_{subset_name}",
            feature_cols=valid_cols,
            n_splits=n_splits,
            random_state=random_state,
            xgb_extra_params=xgb_extra_params,
        )

        results[subset_name] = {
            "mean_auprc": cv_result.mean_auprc,
            "std_auprc": cv_result.std_auprc,
            "mean_auroc": cv_result.mean_auroc,
            "std_auroc": cv_result.std_auroc,
        }
        logger.info(
            "Ablation [%s]: mean_AUPRC=%.4f±%.4f",
            subset_name, cv_result.mean_auprc, cv_result.std_auprc,
        )

    return results


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Three-model pipeline entry point
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run_three_model_comparison(
    feature_matrix: pd.DataFrame,
    *,
    n_splits: int = DEFAULT_N_SPLITS,
    exclude_datasets: Optional[list[str]] = None,
    model_bc_cell_types: Optional[list[str]] = None,
    include_missingness_indicators: bool = False,
    xgb_extra_params: Optional[dict] = None,
    random_state: int = 42,
    output_dir: Optional[Path] = None,
    run_shap: bool = False,
    run_ablation_flag: bool = False,
    top_fraction: float = TOP_FRACTION,
) -> dict[str, CVResult]:
    """Run the full three-model comparison pipeline.

    Trains and cross-validates Models A, B, and C, prints a statistical
    comparison, and optionally saves figures and ablation results.

    Parameters
    ----------
    feature_matrix : pd.DataFrame
        Full assembled feature matrix (from ``build_feature_matrix``).
        On-target sites (``num_mismatches == 0``) must already be removed.
    n_splits : int
        Number of leave-one-guide-out CV folds.
    exclude_datasets : list[str] | None
        Dataset names to exclude for sensitivity analysis
        (e.g. ``["deepcrispr_k562"]``).
    model_bc_cell_types : list[str] | None
        Cell types included in Models B and C.  Defaults to
        ``["K562", "HEK293"]``.
    include_missingness_indicators : bool
        Whether to include ``{feature}_missing`` indicator columns in
        Models B and C.
    xgb_extra_params : dict | None
        Extra XGBoost hyperparameters to override defaults.
    random_state : int
        Global random seed.
    output_dir : Path | None
        Directory for saving PR curve figure, SHAP CSVs, and ablation CSV.
    run_shap : bool
        If ``True``, compute SHAP feature importances for all three models
        and save to *output_dir* (requires the ``shap`` package).
    run_ablation_flag : bool
        If ``True``, run feature-subset ablation analysis for Model C and
        save results to *output_dir*.
    top_fraction : float
        Fraction of top predictions for precision/recall/enrichment metrics.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, CVResult]
        Keys ``"A"``, ``"B"``, ``"C"`` → their :class:`CVResult` objects.

    Notes
    -----
    **Model A** is trained on ALL rows not excluded by *exclude_datasets*
    (no cell-type restriction).  This maximises baseline strength.

    **Models B and C** are restricted to *model_bc_cell_types* (default
    K562 + HEK293) where both experimental and AlphaGenome chromatin features
    are available.
    """
    if model_bc_cell_types is None:
        model_bc_cell_types = list(MODEL_BC_CELL_TYPES)

    logger.info("=== SafeGenome Three-Model Comparison ===")
    logger.info(
        "  n_splits=%d, exclude_datasets=%s, model_bc_cell_types=%s",
        n_splits, exclude_datasets, model_bc_cell_types,
    )

    results: dict[str, CVResult] = {}

    # --- Model A: all data, sequence features only ---
    logger.info("--- Model A: sequence-only baseline (all cell types) ---")
    X_a, y_a, groups_a, feat_a = prepare_model_data(
        feature_matrix, "A",
        cell_types=None,
        exclude_datasets=exclude_datasets,
        include_missingness_indicators=False,
    )
    results["A"] = cross_validate_model(
        X_a, y_a, groups_a,
        model_type="A",
        feature_cols=feat_a,
        n_splits=n_splits,
        random_state=random_state,
        xgb_extra_params=xgb_extra_params,
        top_fraction=top_fraction,
    )

    # --- Model B: K562 + HEK293, sequence + experimental chromatin ---
    logger.info("--- Model B: sequence + experimental chromatin ---")
    X_b, y_b, groups_b, feat_b = prepare_model_data(
        feature_matrix, "B",
        cell_types=model_bc_cell_types,
        exclude_datasets=exclude_datasets,
        include_missingness_indicators=include_missingness_indicators,
    )
    results["B"] = cross_validate_model(
        X_b, y_b, groups_b,
        model_type="B",
        feature_cols=feat_b,
        n_splits=n_splits,
        random_state=random_state,
        xgb_extra_params=xgb_extra_params,
        top_fraction=top_fraction,
    )

    # --- Model C: K562 + HEK293, sequence + AlphaGenome chromatin ---
    logger.info("--- Model C: sequence + AlphaGenome predicted chromatin ---")
    X_c, y_c, groups_c, feat_c = prepare_model_data(
        feature_matrix, "C",
        cell_types=model_bc_cell_types,
        exclude_datasets=exclude_datasets,
        include_missingness_indicators=include_missingness_indicators,
    )
    results["C"] = cross_validate_model(
        X_c, y_c, groups_c,
        model_type="C",
        feature_cols=feat_c,
        n_splits=n_splits,
        random_state=random_state,
        xgb_extra_params=xgb_extra_params,
        top_fraction=top_fraction,
    )

    # --- Statistical comparisons ---
    comp_ac = compare_models(results["A"], results["C"])
    comp_bc = compare_models(results["B"], results["C"])
    comp_ab = compare_models(results["A"], results["B"])

    # --- PR curve figure ---
    if output_dir is not None:
        output_dir = Path(output_dir)
        output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        try:
            plot_pr_curves(results, output_path=output_dir / "fig2_pr_curves.png")
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning("PR curve plotting failed: %s", exc)

    # --- SHAP analysis ---
    if run_shap:
        _data_for_shap: dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, list[str]]] = {
            "A": (X_a, feat_a),
            "B": (X_b, feat_b),
            "C": (X_c, feat_c),
        }
        for model_label, (X_shap, feat_shap) in _data_for_shap.items():
            try:
                _, importance_df = compute_shap_values(
                    results[model_label].final_model, X_shap, feat_shap
                )
                if output_dir is not None:
                    shap_path = output_dir / f"shap_importance_model_{model_label}.csv"
                    importance_df.to_csv(shap_path, index=False)
                    logger.info("SHAP importance saved → %s", shap_path)
            except Exception as exc:
                logger.warning("SHAP analysis for Model %s failed: %s", model_label, exc)

    # --- Ablation for Model C ---
    if run_ablation_flag:
        from src.feature_matrix import AG_FEATURE_NAMES, SEQUENCE_FEATURE_NAMES as SEQ_FEATS

        ag_subsets: dict[str, list[str]] = {
            "seq_only": list(SEQ_FEATS),
            "seq_dnase": list(SEQ_FEATS) + [f for f in AG_FEATURE_NAMES if "dnase" in f],
            "seq_atac": list(SEQ_FEATS) + [f for f in AG_FEATURE_NAMES if "atac" in f],
            "seq_histone": list(SEQ_FEATS) + [
                f for f in AG_FEATURE_NAMES
