                if any(m in f for m in ("h3k4me3", "h3k27me3", "h3k9me3", "h3k27ac"))
            ],
            "seq_ctcf": list(SEQ_FEATS) + [f for f in AG_FEATURE_NAMES if "ctcf" in f],
            "seq_full_ag": list(feat_c),
        }

        ablation_results = run_ablation(
            X_c, y_c, groups_c, feat_c,
            feature_subsets=ag_subsets,
            n_splits=n_splits,
            random_state=random_state,
            xgb_extra_params=xgb_extra_params,
        )

        if output_dir is not None:
            ablation_df = pd.DataFrame(
                [{"subset": k, **v} for k, v in ablation_results.items()]
            )
            ablation_path = output_dir / "ablation_results.csv"
            ablation_df.to_csv(ablation_path, index=False)
            logger.info("Ablation results saved → %s", ablation_path)

    return results


=== src/cell_type_transfer.py ===
"""Cross-cell-type CRISPR off-target risk re-ranking using AlphaGenome.

This module implements the "killer feature" of SafeGenome: re-ranking the same
set of CRISPR off-target candidates across multiple cell types using only
AlphaGenome's predicted chromatin.  Traditional tools (Elevation, DeepCRISPR)
require experimental data per cell type; SafeGenome can score any cell type
from sequence alone.

Workflow
--------
1. Start with off-target candidates (from Cas-OFFinder) with sequence
   features pre-computed (or compute them here).
2. For each target cell type, query AlphaGenome for chromatin features.
3. Score candidates with the trained **Model C** (sequence + AlphaGenome
   features).
4. Aggregate into a site × cell-type risk-score matrix.
5. Visualise as a heatmap (Figure 3 in the paper) and compute rank-change
   statistics.

Critical note on imputation during inference
--------------------------------------------
During training, NaN chromatin features were imputed with within-dataset
medians (or 0.0 for all-NaN columns such as U2OS AlphaGenome features).
At inference time for a new cell type we apply the same strategy: NaN values
are imputed with the within-inference-batch median (or 0.0 for all-NaN
columns, consistent with the U2OS/HEK293 DNase handling during training).
This is appropriate for the visualisation.  For publication-quality metrics,
store training-set medians and apply them here.

Functions
---------
make_site_id                     → canonical site identifier string
score_candidates_for_cell_type   → score + rank sites for one cell type
transfer_across_cell_types       → long-format scores across all cell types
build_score_matrix               → reshape long → wide score matrix
compute_rank_correlation_matrix  → Spearman rank correlations between cell types
validate_transfer_rankings       → AUPRC vs. experimental labels for one cell type
compute_chromatin_correlation    → Spearman r (AlphaGenome vs ENCODE, Validation 1)
plot_transfer_heatmap            → Figure 3: site × cell-type risk heatmap
plot_rank_correlation_heatmap    → cell-type × cell-type Spearman correlation heatmap
run_cell_type_transfer           → main pipeline entry point
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Optional

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from scipy import stats
from scipy.stats import spearmanr, rankdata

import src.alphagenome_client as _ag_mod
from src.feature_matrix import (
    AG_FEATURE_NAMES,
    SEQUENCE_FEATURE_NAMES,
    extract_sequence_features_batch,
    impute_chromatin_features,
    label_candidates,
)
from src.model import compute_metrics

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Constants
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#: Default recommended cell types and their AlphaGenome ontology terms.
RECOMMENDED_CELL_TYPES: dict[str, str] = {
    "K562": "EFO:0002067",
    "HEK293": "EFO:0001182",
    "CD4_T_cell": "CL:0000624",
    "Hepatocyte": "CL:0000182",
}

#: Required site columns for AlphaGenome queries.
_REQUIRED_SITE_COLS: tuple[str, ...] = ("chrom", "start")

#: Column used to indicate computed sequence features are present.
_SEQ_FEATURE_SENTINEL: str = "cfd_score"

#: Column name for the canonical site identifier.
SITE_ID_COL: str = "site_id"


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Site ID helper
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def make_site_id(chrom: str, start: int, strand: str = ".") -> str:
    """Return a canonical site identifier string.

    Parameters
    ----------
    chrom : str
        Chromosome name, e.g. ``"chr7"``.
    start : int
        0-based start position.
    strand : str
        Strand (``"+"``, ``"-"``, or ``"."`` if unknown).

    Returns
    -------
    str
        Identifier of the form ``"chr7:100000:+"``.
    """
    return f"{chrom}:{start}:{strand}"


def _add_site_ids(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Add a ``site_id`` column if not already present."""
    if SITE_ID_COL not in df.columns:
        strand = df["strand"] if "strand" in df.columns else "."
        df = df.copy()
        df[SITE_ID_COL] = [
            make_site_id(str(c), int(s), str(st))
            for c, s, st in zip(df["chrom"], df["start"], strand)
        ]
    return df


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Core scoring
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def score_candidates_for_cell_type(
    candidates: pd.DataFrame,
    cell_type: str,
    ag_ontology_term: str,
    model,
    feature_cols: list[str],
    *,
    ag_client,
    ag_cache_dir: Path,
    window_bp: int = 500,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Score and rank off-target candidates for a single cell type.

    Sequence features are computed if absent.  AlphaGenome chromatin features
    are queried for *cell_type*, NaN values are imputed, then all features in
    *feature_cols* are assembled and scored with *model*.

    Parameters
    ----------
    candidates : pd.DataFrame
        Off-target candidate sites.  Required columns: ``chrom``, ``start``
        (0-based).  Sequence features (``cfd_score`` etc.) are computed
        automatically if not present.  If present they are reused.
    cell_type : str
        Human-readable cell-type label, e.g. ``"K562"`` (used for logging).
    ag_ontology_term : str
        AlphaGenome ontology term, e.g. ``"EFO:0002067"``.
    model : xgb.XGBClassifier
        Trained Model C (sequence + AlphaGenome features).
    feature_cols : list[str]
        Feature columns the model expects (in training order).  Typically
        ``MODEL_C_FEATURES`` from ``src.feature_matrix``.
    ag_client : DnaClient
        Initialised AlphaGenome client.
    ag_cache_dir : Path
        Cache directory for API responses.
    window_bp : int
        Feature extraction half-window around each site (default 500 bp).

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Copy of *candidates* with two additional columns:

        - ``risk_score`` (float in [0, 1]): probability of off-target cleavage
          in *cell_type*.
        - ``rank`` (int, 1-indexed): rank by descending risk score (1 = highest
          risk).
        - ``cell_type`` (str): *cell_type* label.
        - ``site_id`` (str): canonical ``"{chrom}:{start}:{strand}"``.
    """
    for col in _REQUIRED_SITE_COLS:
        if col not in candidates.columns:
            raise ValueError(
                f"score_candidates_for_cell_type: candidates is missing column "
                f"{col!r}. Required: {list(_REQUIRED_SITE_COLS)}"
            )

    work = candidates.copy()
    work = _add_site_ids(work)

    # --- Step 1: Sequence features (compute if absent) ---
    if _SEQ_FEATURE_SENTINEL not in work.columns:
        seq_cols_needed = [
            c for c in feature_cols if c in SEQUENCE_FEATURE_NAMES
        ]
        if seq_cols_needed:
            logger.info(
                "score_candidates_for_cell_type: sequence features missing — "
                "computing for %d candidates",
                len(work),
            )
            work = extract_sequence_features_batch(work)

    # --- Step 2: AlphaGenome chromatin features ---
    logger.info(
        "Querying AlphaGenome for %d sites, cell_type=%s (%s) ...",
        len(work), cell_type, ag_ontology_term,
    )
    work = _ag_mod.batch_get_chromatin_features(
        work,
        cell_type=ag_ontology_term,
        model=ag_client,
        cache_dir=Path(ag_cache_dir),
        window_bp=window_bp,
    )

    # --- Step 3: Impute NaN chromatin features ---
    ag_cols_present = [c for c in AG_FEATURE_NAMES if c in work.columns]
    work = impute_chromatin_features(work, ag_cols_present)

    # --- Step 4: Build X and score ---
    available_feat_cols = [c for c in feature_cols if c in work.columns]
    missing_feat_cols = [c for c in feature_cols if c not in work.columns]
    if missing_feat_cols:
        logger.warning(
            "score_candidates_for_cell_type: %d feature column(s) missing from "
            "candidates for cell_type=%s — filling with 0: %s%s",
            len(missing_feat_cols), cell_type,
            missing_feat_cols[:5],
            " ..." if len(missing_feat_cols) > 5 else "",
        )
        for col in missing_feat_cols:
            work[col] = 0.0
        available_feat_cols = feature_cols  # now all present (filled with 0)

    X = work[available_feat_cols].to_numpy(dtype=np.float32)
    risk_scores = model.predict_proba(X)[:, 1]

    # Rank: 1 = highest risk (largest risk_score).
    ranks = rankdata(-risk_scores, method="min").astype(int)

    result = candidates.copy()
    result = _add_site_ids(result)
    result["risk_score"] = risk_scores
    result["rank"] = ranks
    result["cell_type"] = cell_type

    n_pos = int(result["label"].sum()) if "label" in result.columns else None
    logger.info(
        "score_candidates_for_cell_type done: cell_type=%s, %d sites%s",
        cell_type, len(result),
        f", {n_pos} labeled positives" if n_pos is not None else "",
    )
    return result


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Multi-cell-type transfer
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def transfer_across_cell_types(
    candidates: pd.DataFrame,
    cell_type_map: dict[str, str],
    model,
    feature_cols: list[str],
    *,
    ag_client,
    ag_cache_dir: Path,
    window_bp: int = 500,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Score and rank candidates across multiple cell types.

    For each cell type in *cell_type_map*, calls
    :func:`score_candidates_for_cell_type` and concatenates results into a
    single long-format DataFrame.

    Parameters
    ----------
    candidates : pd.DataFrame
        Off-target candidates.  Required columns: ``chrom``, ``start``.
    cell_type_map : dict[str, str]
        Mapping from human-readable cell-type name to AlphaGenome ontology
        term.  Example: ``{"K562": "EFO:0002067", "HEK293": "EFO:0001182"}``.
    model : xgb.XGBClassifier
        Trained Model C.
    feature_cols : list[str]
        Feature columns used during training (e.g. ``MODEL_C_FEATURES``).
    ag_client : DnaClient
        Initialised AlphaGenome client.
    ag_cache_dir : Path
        Cache directory for API responses.
    window_bp : int
        Feature extraction half-window (default 500 bp).

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Long-format DataFrame with one row per (site, cell_type) pair.
        Contains all original candidate columns plus ``risk_score``,
        ``rank``, ``cell_type``, and ``site_id``.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If *cell_type_map* is empty.
    """
    if not cell_type_map:
        raise ValueError("cell_type_map must contain at least one entry.")

    logger.info(
        "transfer_across_cell_types: %d sites × %d cell types",
        len(candidates), len(cell_type_map),
    )

    parts: list[pd.DataFrame] = []
    for cell_type, ontology_term in cell_type_map.items():
        logger.info("--- Processing cell type: %s (%s) ---", cell_type, ontology_term)
        scored = score_candidates_for_cell_type(
            candidates,
            cell_type=cell_type,
            ag_ontology_term=ontology_term,
            model=model,
            feature_cols=feature_cols,
            ag_client=ag_client,
            ag_cache_dir=Path(ag_cache_dir),
            window_bp=window_bp,
        )
        parts.append(scored)

    transfer_df = pd.concat(parts, ignore_index=True)
    logger.info(
        "transfer_across_cell_types complete: %d total rows (%d sites × %d cell types)",
        len(transfer_df), len(candidates), len(cell_type_map),
    )
    return transfer_df


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Score matrix
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def build_score_matrix(
    transfer_df: pd.DataFrame,
    *,
    sort_by: Optional[str] = "cfd_score",
    ascending: bool = True,
    site_id_col: str = SITE_ID_COL,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Reshape a long-format transfer DataFrame into a wide score matrix.

    Parameters
    ----------
    transfer_df : pd.DataFrame
        Long-format output of :func:`transfer_across_cell_types`.  Must
        contain ``site_id``, ``cell_type``, and ``risk_score`` columns.
    sort_by : str | None
        Column in *transfer_df* to use for row sorting.  If ``None`` or the
        column is not found, rows are sorted by mean risk score descending.
        Typical choices: ``"cfd_score"`` (sequence-only score, ascending =
        lowest-sequence-score first, showing chromatin-driven re-ranking).
    ascending : bool
        Sort direction for *sort_by* (default ``True`` = ascending).
    site_id_col : str
        Name of the site identifier column (default ``"site_id"``).

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Wide matrix with:

        - Index: site identifiers (``site_id``).
        - Columns: cell-type names.
        - Values: risk scores in [0, 1].
    """
    for col in (site_id_col, "cell_type", "risk_score"):
        if col not in transfer_df.columns:
            raise ValueError(
                f"build_score_matrix: required column {col!r} not in transfer_df."
            )

    matrix = transfer_df.pivot_table(
        index=site_id_col,
        columns="cell_type",
        values="risk_score",
        aggfunc="first",
    )
    matrix.columns.name = None

    # Determine row order.
    if sort_by is not None and sort_by in transfer_df.columns:
        sort_series = (
            transfer_df.groupby(site_id_col)[sort_by].first()
            .reindex(matrix.index)
        )
        ordered_idx = sort_series.sort_values(ascending=ascending).index
        matrix = matrix.loc[ordered_idx]
    else:
        if sort_by is not None:
            logger.warning(
                "build_score_matrix: sort_by=%r not found in transfer_df; "
                "sorting by mean risk score descending",
                sort_by,
            )
        mean_scores = matrix.mean(axis=1)
        matrix = matrix.loc[mean_scores.sort_values(ascending=False).index]

    return matrix


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Rank correlation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def compute_rank_correlation_matrix(
    score_matrix: pd.DataFrame,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Compute Spearman rank correlation between all pairs of cell types.

    Uses :meth:`pandas.DataFrame.corr` with ``method="spearman"``.  NaN
    values are handled pairwise (sites with NaN in either cell type are
    excluded from that pair's computation).

    Parameters
    ----------
    score_matrix : pd.DataFrame
        Wide matrix of risk scores (rows = sites, columns = cell types), as
        returned by :func:`build_score_matrix`.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Symmetric correlation matrix with shape
        ``(n_cell_types, n_cell_types)``.  Values are in [−1, 1].
        Diagonal entries are 1.0.
    """
    if score_matrix.empty:
        raise ValueError("compute_rank_correlation_matrix: score_matrix is empty.")

    corr = score_matrix.corr(method="spearman")
    return corr


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Validation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def validate_transfer_rankings(
    transfer_df: pd.DataFrame,
    positives: pd.DataFrame,
    cell_type: str,
    *,
    match_window_bp: int = 5,
) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Evaluate ranked predictions against experimental positive labels.

    For the given *cell_type*, retrieves predicted risk scores from
    *transfer_df*, matches them to known positives in *positives*, and
    computes AUPRC and AUROC.

    Parameters
    ----------
    transfer_df : pd.DataFrame
        Long-format output of :func:`transfer_across_cell_types`.  Must
        contain ``cell_type``, ``risk_score``, ``guide_id`` (if available),
        ``chrom``, and ``start`` columns.
    positives : pd.DataFrame
        Experimentally verified positive off-target sites for *cell_type*.
        Must contain ``guide_id``, ``chrom``, ``start``.
    cell_type : str
        Cell type to validate.
    match_window_bp : int
        Tolerance (bp) for position matching between candidates and positives.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, float]
        Keys: ``auprc``, ``auroc``, ``precision_at_top``, ``recall_at_top``,
        ``enrichment_at_top``, ``n_sites``, ``n_positives``.
    """
    ct_df = transfer_df[transfer_df["cell_type"] == cell_type].copy()
    if len(ct_df) == 0:
        raise ValueError(
            f"validate_transfer_rankings: no sites found for cell_type={cell_type!r}. "
            f"Available cell types: {transfer_df['cell_type'].unique().tolist()}"
        )

    # Ensure guide_id exists (needed by label_candidates).
    if "guide_id" not in ct_df.columns:
        logger.warning(
            "validate_transfer_rankings: 'guide_id' missing — adding a "
            "placeholder. All sites will be treated as from the same guide."
        )
        ct_df = ct_df.copy()
        ct_df["guide_id"] = "unknown_guide"
        positives = positives.copy()
        if "guide_id" not in positives.columns:
            positives["guide_id"] = "unknown_guide"

    labeled = label_candidates(ct_df, positives, match_window_bp=match_window_bp)
    y_true = labeled["label"].to_numpy(dtype=np.int32)
    y_score = labeled["risk_score"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)

    metrics = compute_metrics(y_true, y_score)
    metrics["n_sites"] = float(len(labeled))
    metrics["n_positives"] = float(int(y_true.sum()))

    logger.info(
        "validate_transfer_rankings (%s): AUPRC=%.4f, AUROC=%.4f, "
        "n_sites=%d, n_positives=%d",
        cell_type,
        metrics.get("auprc", float("nan")),
        metrics.get("auroc", float("nan")),
        int(metrics["n_sites"]),
        int(metrics["n_positives"]),
    )
    return metrics


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Chromatin correlation (Validation 1)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def compute_chromatin_correlation(
    feature_matrix: pd.DataFrame,
    *,
    ag_feature: str = "ag_dnase_mean",
    exp_feature: str = "exp_dnase_mean",
    cell_type: Optional[str] = None,
) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Spearman correlation between AlphaGenome predictions and ENCODE signals.

    This is **Validation 1** from the project spec: we expect r > 0.5 for K562
    (preferably r > 0.7) to confirm that AlphaGenome's predictions are
    meaningful at CRISPR off-target loci.

    Per the project's critical rules, Spearman (not Pearson) is used because
    the two signals are on different scales.

    Parameters
    ----------
    feature_matrix : pd.DataFrame
        Feature matrix containing both AlphaGenome (``ag_*``) and ENCODE
        (``exp_*``) feature columns.
    ag_feature : str
        AlphaGenome feature column name (default ``"ag_dnase_mean"``).
    exp_feature : str
        ENCODE feature column name (default ``"exp_dnase_mean"``).
    cell_type : str | None
        If provided, filter to rows where ``cell_type == cell_type``.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, float]
        Keys: ``spearman_r``, ``p_value``, ``n``.  Any value is ``nan`` if
        fewer than 3 paired non-NaN observations are available.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If *ag_feature* or *exp_feature* are not present in *feature_matrix*.
    """
    for col in (ag_feature, exp_feature):
        if col not in feature_matrix.columns:
            raise ValueError(
                f"compute_chromatin_correlation: column {col!r} not in "
                f"feature_matrix. Available columns: "
                f"{[c for c in feature_matrix.columns if c.startswith(('ag_', 'exp_'))]}"
            )

    df = feature_matrix
    if cell_type is not None and "cell_type" in df.columns:
        df = df[df["cell_type"] == cell_type]
        if len(df) == 0:
            logger.warning(
                "compute_chromatin_correlation: no rows for cell_type=%r", cell_type
            )
            return {"spearman_r": float("nan"), "p_value": float("nan"), "n": 0}

    valid = df[[ag_feature, exp_feature]].dropna()
    n = len(valid)

    if n < 3:
        logger.warning(
            "compute_chromatin_correlation: only %d valid pairs; "
            "Spearman r cannot be reliably estimated",
            n,
        )
        return {"spearman_r": float("nan"), "p_value": float("nan"), "n": n}

    r, p = spearmanr(valid[ag_feature], valid[exp_feature])
    logger.info(
        "Spearman correlation (%s vs %s%s): r=%.4f, p=%.4g, n=%d",
        ag_feature, exp_feature,
        f", cell_type={cell_type!r}" if cell_type else "",
        r, p, n,
    )
    return {"spearman_r": float(r), "p_value": float(p), "n": n}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Visualisation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def plot_transfer_heatmap(
    score_matrix: pd.DataFrame,
    *,
    output_path: Optional[Path] = None,
    title: str = "CRISPR Off-Target Risk Scores Across Cell Types",
    top_n: Optional[int] = None,
    normalize_rows: bool = False,
    figsize: Optional[tuple[float, float]] = None,
    cmap: str = "RdBu_r",
):
    """Plot the site × cell-type risk score heatmap (Figure 3).

    Parameters
    ----------
    score_matrix : pd.DataFrame
        Wide risk-score matrix (rows = sites, columns = cell types), as
        returned by :func:`build_score_matrix`.
    output_path : Path | None
        Save destination.  Extension determines format (PNG, SVG, PDF).
    title : str
        Figure title.
    top_n : int | None
        If given, show only the top-n rows (by mean risk score).
    normalize_rows : bool
        If ``True``, z-score each row (highlights relative differences
        between cell types for the same site).  Useful for visualization
        when absolute risk levels differ substantially between sites.
    figsize : tuple | None
        Figure size ``(width, height)`` in inches.  Auto-sized if ``None``.
    cmap : str
        Matplotlib colormap.  ``"RdBu_r"`` (red=high risk, blue=low risk).

    Returns
    -------
    matplotlib.figure.Figure

    Raises
    ------
    ImportError
        If matplotlib or seaborn are not installed.
    """
    try:
        import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
        import seaborn as sns
    except ImportError:
        raise ImportError(
            "matplotlib and seaborn are required for plot_transfer_heatmap. "
            "Install with: pip install matplotlib>=3.7 seaborn>=0.12"
        )

    plot_matrix = score_matrix.copy()
    if top_n is not None:
        mean_scores = plot_matrix.mean(axis=1)
        plot_matrix = plot_matrix.loc[
            mean_scores.nlargest(top_n).index
        ]

    if normalize_rows:
        plot_matrix = plot_matrix.sub(plot_matrix.mean(axis=1), axis=0)
        std = plot_matrix.std(axis=1).replace(0.0, 1.0)
        plot_matrix = plot_matrix.div(std, axis=0)
        vmin, vmax, center = None, None, 0.0
        colorbar_label = "Row-normalised risk score (z-score)"
    else:
        vmin, vmax, center = 0.0, 1.0, 0.5
        colorbar_label = "Risk score (Model C probability)"

    n_sites, n_ct = plot_matrix.shape
    if figsize is None:
        figsize = (max(4.0, n_ct * 1.2 + 2.0), max(4.0, n_sites * 0.18 + 1.5))

    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=figsize)
    sns.heatmap(
        plot_matrix,
        ax=ax,
        cmap=cmap,
        vmin=vmin,
        vmax=vmax,
        center=center,
        xticklabels=True,
        yticklabels=False,   # too many sites to label individually
        cbar_kws={"label": colorbar_label, "shrink": 0.8},
    )
    ax.set_title(title, fontsize=13, pad=10)
    ax.set_xlabel("Cell type", fontsize=11)
    ax.set_ylabel(f"Off-target sites (n={n_sites})", fontsize=11)
    ax.tick_params(axis="x", labelsize=10, rotation=30)
    fig.tight_layout()

    if output_path is not None:
        output_path = Path(output_path)
        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        fig.savefig(output_path, dpi=150, bbox_inches="tight")
        logger.info("Transfer heatmap saved → %s", output_path)

    return fig


def plot_rank_correlation_heatmap(
    rank_corr: pd.DataFrame,
    *,
    output_path: Optional[Path] = None,
    title: str = "Spearman Rank Correlation of Off-Target Risk Across Cell Types",
    figsize: tuple[float, float] = (6.0, 5.5),
    cmap: str = "coolwarm",
    annot: bool = True,
):
    """Plot Spearman rank-correlation matrix between cell types.

    A high correlation between two cell types indicates that the off-target
    risk rankings are similar (e.g. both have open chromatin at the same
    loci).  Low correlation demonstrates cell-type-specific re-ranking.

    Parameters
    ----------
    rank_corr : pd.DataFrame
        Square Spearman correlation matrix from
        :func:`compute_rank_correlation_matrix`.
    output_path : Path | None
        Save destination.
    title : str
        Figure title.
    figsize : tuple[float, float]
        Figure size in inches.
    cmap : str
        Diverging colormap centred at zero.
    annot : bool
        Annotate each cell with its correlation value.

    Returns
    -------
    matplotlib.figure.Figure
    """
    try:
        import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
        import seaborn as sns
    except ImportError:
        raise ImportError(
            "matplotlib and seaborn are required. "
            "Install with: pip install matplotlib seaborn"
        )

    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=figsize)
    mask = np.zeros_like(rank_corr.values, dtype=bool)
    np.fill_diagonal(mask, True)  # hide diagonal (always 1.0)

    sns.heatmap(
        rank_corr,
        ax=ax,
        cmap=cmap,
        vmin=-1.0,
        vmax=1.0,
        center=0.0,
        annot=annot,
        fmt=".2f",
        mask=mask,
        square=True,
        cbar_kws={"label": "Spearman r", "shrink": 0.8},
        linewidths=0.5,
    )
    ax.set_title(title, fontsize=12, pad=10)
    ax.tick_params(axis="x", rotation=30, labelsize=10)
    ax.tick_params(axis="y", rotation=0, labelsize=10)
    fig.tight_layout()

    if output_path is not None:
        output_path = Path(output_path)
        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        fig.savefig(output_path, dpi=150, bbox_inches="tight")
        logger.info("Rank correlation heatmap saved → %s", output_path)

    return fig


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Main pipeline entry point
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run_cell_type_transfer(
    candidates: pd.DataFrame,
    cell_type_map: dict[str, str],
    model,
    feature_cols: list[str],
    *,
    ag_client,
    ag_cache_dir: Path,
    window_bp: int = 500,
    output_dir: Optional[Path] = None,
    top_n_heatmap: int = 50,
    normalize_heatmap_rows: bool = False,
    sort_heatmap_by: Optional[str] = "cfd_score",
    positives_by_cell_type: Optional[dict[str, pd.DataFrame]] = None,
    match_window_bp: int = 5,
) -> dict:
    """Run the complete cell-type transfer analysis pipeline.

    Scores candidates across all cell types in *cell_type_map*, builds the
    score matrix, computes rank correlations, optionally validates against
    experimental labels, and generates figures.

    Parameters
    ----------
    candidates : pd.DataFrame
        Off-target candidates (chrom + start required; seq features optional).
    cell_type_map : dict[str, str]
        ``{cell_type_name: ontology_term}`` mapping.
    model : xgb.XGBClassifier
        Trained Model C final model.
    feature_cols : list[str]
        Feature columns used during Model C training (e.g. ``MODEL_C_FEATURES``).
    ag_client : DnaClient
        Initialised AlphaGenome client.
    ag_cache_dir : Path
        Cache directory for API responses.
    window_bp : int
        Feature extraction half-window (default 500 bp).
    output_dir : Path | None
        Directory for saving figures and CSV results.
    top_n_heatmap : int
        Number of top-risk sites to show in the heatmap.
    normalize_heatmap_rows : bool
        If ``True``, z-score normalise heatmap rows.
    sort_heatmap_by : str | None
        Column for heatmap row ordering (default ``"cfd_score"``).
    positives_by_cell_type : dict[str, pd.DataFrame] | None
        Mapping from cell-type name to positives DataFrame for validation.
        Only cell types with GUIDE-seq or equivalent data need entries.
    match_window_bp : int
        Position tolerance for matching candidates to experimental positives.

    Returns
    -------
    dict with keys:
        - ``"transfer_df"`` : pd.DataFrame — long-format risk scores.
        - ``"score_matrix"`` : pd.DataFrame — wide site × cell-type matrix.
        - ``"rank_corr"`` : pd.DataFrame — Spearman rank correlation matrix.
        - ``"validation"`` : dict[str, dict] — per-cell-type AUPRC/AUROC
          (only for cell types in *positives_by_cell_type*).
    """
    logger.info(
        "=== Cell-Type Transfer Analysis ===\n"
        "  %d candidates × %d cell types",
        len(candidates), len(cell_type_map),
    )

    # --- Score across cell types ---
    transfer_df = transfer_across_cell_types(
        candidates,
        cell_type_map=cell_type_map,
        model=model,
        feature_cols=feature_cols,
        ag_client=ag_client,
        ag_cache_dir=Path(ag_cache_dir),
        window_bp=window_bp,
    )

    # --- Build score matrix ---
    score_matrix = build_score_matrix(
        transfer_df,
        sort_by=sort_heatmap_by,
        ascending=True,
    )

    # --- Rank correlation ---
    rank_corr = compute_rank_correlation_matrix(score_matrix)
    logger.info("Rank correlation matrix:\n%s", rank_corr.to_string())

    # --- Validation ---
    validation: dict[str, dict] = {}
    if positives_by_cell_type:
        for ct, positives in positives_by_cell_type.items():
            if ct not in cell_type_map:
                logger.warning(
                    "run_cell_type_transfer: validation cell type %r not in "
                    "cell_type_map — skipping",
                    ct,
                )
                continue
            try:
                val_metrics = validate_transfer_rankings(
                    transfer_df, positives, ct,
                    match_window_bp=match_window_bp,
                )
                validation[ct] = val_metrics
                logger.info(
                    "Validation (%s): AUPRC=%.4f, n_positives=%d",
                    ct, val_metrics.get("auprc", float("nan")),
                    int(val_metrics.get("n_positives", 0)),
                )
            except Exception as exc:
                logger.warning(
                    "Validation for %r failed: %s", ct, exc
                )

    # --- Save outputs ---
    if output_dir is not None:
        output_dir = Path(output_dir)
        output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

        # Long-format CSV
        transfer_csv = output_dir / "cell_type_transfer_scores.csv"
        transfer_df.to_csv(transfer_csv, index=False)
        logger.info("Transfer scores saved → %s", transfer_csv)

        # Score matrix CSV
        matrix_csv = output_dir / "cell_type_score_matrix.csv"
        score_matrix.to_csv(matrix_csv)
        logger.info("Score matrix saved → %s", matrix_csv)

        # Rank correlation CSV
        corr_csv = output_dir / "cell_type_rank_correlation.csv"
        rank_corr.to_csv(corr_csv)
        logger.info("Rank correlation saved → %s", corr_csv)

        # Heatmap figure
        try:
            plot_transfer_heatmap(
                score_matrix,
                output_path=output_dir / "fig3_cell_type_heatmap.png",
                top_n=top_n_heatmap,
                normalize_rows=normalize_heatmap_rows,
            )
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning("Transfer heatmap plotting failed: %s", exc)

        # Rank correlation figure
        try:
            plot_rank_correlation_heatmap(
                rank_corr,
                output_path=output_dir / "cell_type_rank_correlation_heatmap.png",
            )
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning("Rank correlation heatmap plotting failed: %s", exc)

        # Validation summary
        if validation:
            val_df = pd.DataFrame(
                [{"cell_type": ct, **v} for ct, v in validation.items()]
            )
            val_csv = output_dir / "transfer_validation.csv"
            val_df.to_csv(val_csv, index=False)
            logger.info("Validation summary saved → %s", val_csv)

    return {
        "transfer_df": transfer_df,
        "score_matrix": score_matrix,
        "rank_corr": rank_corr,
        "validation": validation,
    }


=== scripts/02_parse_datasets.py ===
"""
scripts/02_parse_datasets.py — Parse raw supplementary tables into the
standardised SafeGenome off-target table.

Usage
-----
    python scripts/02_parse_datasets.py [options]

    --guide-seq PATH        Tsai 2015 GUIDE-seq TSV
    --change-seq PATH       Lazzarotto 2020 CHANGE-seq XLSX
    --deepcrispr PATH       Chuai 2018 Additional File 7 (CSV/XLSX)
    --discover-seq-s1 PATH  Wienert 2019 Supplementary Table S1 XLSX
    --discover-seq-s3 PATH  Wienert 2019 Supplementary Table S3 XLSX
    --deepcrispr-genome-build {hg19,hg38}  (default: hg19)

Outputs
-------
    data/processed/guide_seq_tsai2015.parquet
    data/processed/change_seq_lazzarotto2020.parquet
    data/processed/deepcrispr_k562.parquet
    data/processed/discover_seq_k562.parquet

Output schema (all files)
--------------------------
  guide_id        str     guide name, e.g. "VEGFA_site1"
  chrom           str     normalised chromosome, e.g. "chr6"
  start           int64   0-based start (BED convention)
  end             int64   0-based half-open end  (= start + 23 for NGG sites)
  strand          str     "+" or "−"
  guide_seq       str     20-nt protospacer sequence (DNA, uppercase, no PAM)
  offtarget_seq   str     20-nt off-target spacer (DNA, uppercase, no PAM)
  pam_seq         str     3-nt PAM observed at the off-target site
                          ("NNN" when not available in source — must be
                          extracted from genome in feature_matrix.py)
  num_mismatches  int64   mismatches in spacer only (PAM mismatches not counted)
  read_count      float64 raw experimental read/score value; interpretation
                          depends on dataset:
                          - guide_seq / discover_seq: GUIDE-seq / MRE11 reads
                          - change_seq: CHANGE-seq reads
                          - deepcrispr: cleavage frequency or binary label
  cell_type       str     cell line, e.g. "HEK293", "U2OS", "K562", "T cells"
  dataset         str     dataset tag
  genome_build    str     always "hg38" (liftover applied where needed)

Design decisions
-----------------
* Truncated guides (tru_*) are excluded — their spacers are <20 nt and are
  incompatible with 20-nt CFD / MIT scoring.
* On-target sites (num_mismatches == 0) are removed per project protocol.
  The model must NOT learn "0 mismatches = positive".
* GUIDE-seq and DeepCRISPR coordinates are originally hg19; lifted to hg38.
* CHANGE-seq and DISCOVER-seq coordinates are already hg38.  No liftover.
* Every filtering step is logged with log_filter for the paper methods section.
* DeepCRISPR and DISCOVER-seq are POSITIVES-ONLY datasets.  Negatives must
  be generated separately via Cas-OFFinder.
* Column detection uses alias lists; if a required column is missing the
  parser raises ValueError listing the actual columns found for debugging.

Data sources
------------
GUIDE-seq:
  Tsai et al. (2015) Nat Biotechnol 33:187-197.  doi:10.1038/nbt.3117
  Source: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/maximilianh/crisporpaper/
          master/origData/Tsai2015/nbt.3117-S2.txt
  Save as: data/raw/tsai2015_guide_seq.tsv
  Genome build: hg19 → lifted to hg38 here.

CHANGE-seq:
  Lazzarotto et al. (2020) Nat Biotechnol 38:1317-1327.
  doi:10.1038/s41587-020-0555-7
  Source: Supplementary Tables XLSX from PMC7652380.
  Save as: data/raw/lazzarotto2020_change_seq_supp_tables.xlsx
  Instructions: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7652380/
                → Supplementary Material → SuppTables.xlsx
  Genome build: hg38 (no liftover needed).

DeepCRISPR K562:
  Chuai et al. (2018) Genome Biol 19:80.  doi:10.1186/s13059-018-1459-4
  Source: Additional File 7 (PMC6020378 supplementary).
  Instructions: https://genomebiology.biomedcentral.com/articles/
                10.1186/s13059-018-1459-4  → Additional files → File 7
  Save as: data/raw/chuai2018_deepcrispr_k562.xlsx  (or .csv)
  Genome build: assumed hg19 (override with --deepcrispr-genome-build hg38).
  Content: 12 sgRNAs, ~120 positive off-target sites in K562 (file also
           contains HEK293 rows; K562 rows are extracted by cell-type filter).

DISCOVER-seq K562:
  Wienert et al. (2019) Science 364:286-289.  doi:10.1126/science.aav9023
  Source: Supplementary Tables S1 and S3 from PMC6589096.
  Instructions: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6589096/
                → Supplementary Materials
                → Download Table_S1.xlsx  (guide info and on-target sites)
                → Download Table_S3.xlsx  (DISCOVER-seq off-target sites)
  Save as: data/raw/wienert2019_discover_seq_S1.xlsx
           data/raw/wienert2019_discover_seq_S3.xlsx
  Genome build: hg38 (no liftover needed).
  Content: 3 sgRNAs in K562 — VEGFA_site2 (57 OTs), RNF2 (0), HBB (2).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import logging
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import pandas as pd

# --- path setup so src imports work when run as a script ---
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]))

from src.utils import get_logger, log_filter, liftover_hg19_to_hg38, normalize_chrom

logger = get_logger("parse_datasets")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Paths
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
_RAW = _ROOT / "data" / "raw"
_PROCESSED = _ROOT / "data" / "processed"

_GUIDE_SEQ_RAW = _RAW / "tsai2015_guide_seq.tsv"
_CHANGE_SEQ_RAW = _RAW / "lazzarotto2020_change_seq_supp_tables.xlsx"
_DEEPCRISPR_RAW = _RAW / "chuai2018_deepcrispr_k562.xlsx"
_DISCOVER_SEQ_S1_RAW = _RAW / "wienert2019_discover_seq_S1.xlsx"
_DISCOVER_SEQ_S3_RAW = _RAW / "wienert2019_discover_seq_S3.xlsx"

_GUIDE_SEQ_OUT = _PROCESSED / "guide_seq_tsai2015.parquet"
_CHANGE_SEQ_OUT = _PROCESSED / "change_seq_lazzarotto2020.parquet"
_DEEPCRISPR_OUT = _PROCESSED / "deepcrispr_k562.parquet"
_DISCOVER_SEQ_OUT = _PROCESSED / "discover_seq_k562.parquet"

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Standard output schema
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_OUTPUT_COLUMNS: list[str] = [
    "guide_id",
    "chrom",
    "start",
    "end",
    "strand",
    "guide_seq",
    "offtarget_seq",
    "pam_seq",
    "num_mismatches",
    "read_count",
    "cell_type",
    "dataset",
    "genome_build",
]

_OUTPUT_DTYPES: dict[str, str] = {
    "start": "int64",
    "end": "int64",
    "num_mismatches": "int64",
    "read_count": "float64",
}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Shared helpers for flexible column detection
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def _pick_col(df: pd.DataFrame, aliases: list[str], label: str) -> str:
    """Return the first alias that exists in *df*; raise ``ValueError`` if none.

    Parameters
    ----------
    df : pd.DataFrame
    aliases : list[str]
        Column name candidates tried in order of preference.
    label : str
        Human-readable field name used in the error message.

    Returns
    -------
    str
        The matched column name.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        With a message that lists the actual columns found, to help users
        diagnose format mismatches without reading the source code.
    """
    for a in aliases:
        if a in df.columns:
            return a
    raise ValueError(
        f"Cannot find '{label}' column.\n"
        f"  Tried aliases : {aliases}\n"
        f"  Actual columns: {sorted(df.columns)}"
    )


def _load_tabular(path: Path) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Load a CSV, TSV, or Excel file into a DataFrame.

    Tries tab-separated first; falls back to comma-separated only when the
    tab-split produced exactly one column (i.e. no tabs found in the file).
    """
    sfx = path.suffix.lower()
    if sfx in {".xlsx", ".xls"}:
        return pd.read_excel(path, engine="openpyxl")
    df = pd.read_csv(path, sep="\t")
    if df.shape[1] == 1:
        df = pd.read_csv(path, sep=",")
    return df


def _apply_normalise_chrom(
    df: pd.DataFrame, col: str
) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, "pd.Series[bool]"]:
    """Normalise *col* via ``normalize_chrom()`` in-place.

    Returns the modified DataFrame and a boolean ``pd.Series`` keep-mask
    (``True`` = valid chrom).  Using a Series (rather than a plain Python
    list) ensures ``df[mask]`` is always interpreted as row-selection, even
    for empty DataFrames where ``df[[]]`` would be column-selection.
    """
    norm: list[str] = []
    mask: list[bool] = []
    for c in df[col].astype(str):
        try:
            norm.append(normalize_chrom(c))
            mask.append(True)
        except ValueError:
            norm.append("")
            mask.append(False)
    df = df.copy()
    df[col] = norm
    return df, pd.Series(mask, index=df.index, dtype=bool)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# GUIDE-seq parser  (Tsai 2015, hg19 → hg38)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def parse_guide_seq(path: Path = _GUIDE_SEQ_RAW) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Parse the Tsai 2015 GUIDE-seq supplementary table (crisporpaper TSV).

    Parameters
    ----------
    path : Path
        Path to tsai2015_guide_seq.tsv.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Standardised off-target table (see module docstring for schema).
    """
    logger.info("=== Parsing GUIDE-seq (Tsai 2015) from %s ===", path)

    if not path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(
            f"GUIDE-seq file not found: {path}\n"
            "Download from:\n"
            "  https://raw.githubusercontent.com/maximilianh/crisporpaper/"
            "master/origData/Tsai2015/nbt.3117-S2.txt\n"
            "Save as: data/raw/tsai2015_guide_seq.tsv"
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Load
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # The file has a '#' prefix on the header; skip it with comment='#'
    # and supply column names manually.
    col_names = [
        "Chromosome",
        "Start",
        "End",
        "Name",
        "GUIDE_Seq_Reads",
        "Strand",
        "Cells",
        "Targetsite",
        "Target_Sequence",
        "Offtarget_Sequence",
        "Mismatches_20bp",
        "Mismatches_3bp_PAM",
        "Mismatch_Total",
    ]
    df = pd.read_csv(path, sep="\t", comment="#", names=col_names)
    n_raw = len(df)
    logger.info("Loaded %d rows", n_raw)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Filter 1: remove truncated guides (tru_*)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # tru_* guides have spacers <20 nt — incompatible with 20-nt CFD scoring.
    mask_tru = df["Targetsite"].str.startswith("tru_")
    df = df[~mask_tru].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "truncated guides (tru_*)", n_raw, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Filter 2: require 23-nt sequences (guide + 3-nt PAM)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    mask_len = (df["Target_Sequence"].str.len() == 23) & (
        df["Offtarget_Sequence"].str.len() == 23
    )
    df = df[mask_len].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "non-23-nt sequences", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Extract guide/off-target sequences and PAM
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Target_Sequence is the on-target genomic site (guide + PAM), 23 nt.
    # The first 20 nt are the protospacer; the last 3 nt are the PAM.
    # For the off-target PAM we use Offtarget_Sequence[20:] (actual observed).
    df["guide_seq"] = df["Target_Sequence"].str[:20].str.upper()
    df["offtarget_seq"] = df["Offtarget_Sequence"].str[:20].str.upper()
    df["pam_seq"] = df["Offtarget_Sequence"].str[20:].str.upper()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Filter 3: remove on-target sites (Mismatch_Total == 0)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    df = df[df["Mismatch_Total"] > 0].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "on-target sites (Mismatch_Total == 0)", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Normalize cell-line names
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    df["cell_type"] = df["Cells"].replace({"293": "HEK293"})

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Coordinate normalization: hg19 BED (0-based) → hg38 (0-based)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    interval_len = (df["End"] - df["Start"]).values  # usually 23

    lifted_chroms: list[str] = []
    lifted_starts: list[int] = []
    keep_mask: list[bool] = []

    for _, row in df.iterrows():
        result = liftover_hg19_to_hg38(str(row["Chromosome"]), int(row["Start"]))
        if result is None:
            keep_mask.append(False)
            lifted_chroms.append("")
            lifted_starts.append(0)
        else:
            hg38_chrom, hg38_start = result
            keep_mask.append(True)
            lifted_chroms.append(hg38_chrom)
            lifted_starts.append(hg38_start)

    df["_keep"] = keep_mask
    df["_hg38_chrom"] = lifted_chroms
    df["_hg38_start"] = lifted_starts

    df = df[df["_keep"]].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "liftover failures (unmappable hg19 positions)", n_before, len(df))

    # Recompute end: hg38_start + original interval length (End - Start unchanged)
    df["start"] = df["_hg38_start"].astype("int64")
    df["end"] = (df["_hg38_start"] + (df["End"] - df["Start"])).astype("int64")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Normalize chromosome names
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    valid_chroms: list[bool] = []
    norm_chroms: list[str] = []
    for c in df["_hg38_chrom"]:
        try:
            norm_chroms.append(normalize_chrom(c))
            valid_chroms.append(True)
        except ValueError:
            norm_chroms.append("")
            valid_chroms.append(False)

    df["chrom"] = norm_chroms
    df = df[valid_chroms].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "non-standard contigs after liftover", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Assemble output
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    df["guide_id"] = df["Targetsite"]
    df["strand"] = df["Strand"]
    df["num_mismatches"] = df["Mismatches_20bp"].astype("int64")
    df["read_count"] = df["GUIDE_Seq_Reads"].astype("float64")
    df["dataset"] = "guide_seq_tsai2015"
    df["genome_build"] = "hg38"

    out = df[_OUTPUT_COLUMNS].copy()
    for col, dtype in _OUTPUT_DTYPES.items():
        out[col] = out[col].astype(dtype)

    logger.info(
        "GUIDE-seq final: %d rows, %d guides, cells: %s",
        len(out),
        out["guide_id"].nunique(),
        sorted(out["cell_type"].unique()),
    )
    return out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# CHANGE-seq parser  (Lazzarotto 2020, hg38)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def parse_change_seq(path: Path = _CHANGE_SEQ_RAW) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Parse the Lazzarotto 2020 CHANGE-seq supplementary tables Excel file.

    The full dataset is in Supplementary Table 3 (~202,000 rows, hg38).

    Parameters
    ----------
    path : Path
        Path to lazzarotto2020_change_seq_supp_tables.xlsx.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Standardised off-target table.

    Raises
    ------
    FileNotFoundError
        With download instructions if the file is missing.
    ValueError
        If the expected sheet / columns cannot be found in the workbook.
    """
    logger.info("=== Parsing CHANGE-seq (Lazzarotto 2020) from %s ===", path)

    if not path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(
            f"CHANGE-seq supplementary file not found: {path}\n\n"
            "Download instructions:\n"
            "  1. Open https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7652380/ in a browser.\n"
            "  2. Scroll to the bottom → 'Supplementary Material' section.\n"
            "  3. Click 'SuppTables.xlsx' (≈15 MB).\n"
            "  4. Save as:  data/raw/lazzarotto2020_change_seq_supp_tables.xlsx\n"
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Load the CHANGE-seq off-target sheet
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Supp Table 3 contains the reformatted BED-style off-target table.
    # Columns: chrom, chromStart, chromEnd, name, CHANGEseq_reads, strand,
    #          offtarget_sequence, (Unnamed:7), chromStart:chromEnd,
    #          distance, target
    # "name" = guide label (e.g. "AAVS1_site_1"), shared across all hits
    #   for that guide.
    # "target" = 23-nt guide+PAM sequence.
    # "distance" = mismatches in the 20-nt spacer only (PAM not counted).
    # All coordinates are hg38 BED (0-based, half-open).
    # Cell type: all experiments used primary human T-cell genomic DNA as
    # the naked-DNA substrate (no per-row cell annotation in the table).
    _SHEET = "CHANGE-seq_Supp_Table_3"
    _REQUIRED_COLS = {"chrom", "chromStart", "chromEnd", "name",
                      "CHANGEseq_reads", "strand", "offtarget_sequence",
                      "distance", "target"}

    logger.info("Reading Excel workbook sheet list …")
    xl = pd.ExcelFile(path, engine="openpyxl")

    if _SHEET in xl.sheet_names:
        sheet_name: str = _SHEET
    else:
        # Fallback: find any sheet with the required columns
        sheet_name = ""
        for sname in xl.sheet_names:
            try:
                peek = xl.parse(sname, nrows=3)
                if _REQUIRED_COLS.issubset(set(peek.columns)):
                    sheet_name = sname
                    break
            except Exception:
                continue
        if not sheet_name:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Could not find CHANGE-seq sheet in {path}.\n"
                f"Available sheets: {xl.sheet_names}\n"
                f"Expected sheet: {_SHEET!r} with columns: {_REQUIRED_COLS}"
            )

    logger.info("Found CHANGE-seq data on sheet %r", sheet_name)

    df = xl.parse(sheet_name, engine="openpyxl")
    n_raw = len(df)
    logger.info("Loaded %d rows", n_raw)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Filter 1: drop rows with missing offtarget_sequence
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    df = df[df["offtarget_sequence"].notna()].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "rows with missing offtarget_sequence", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Filter 2: remove on-target sites (distance == 0)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    df = df[df["distance"] > 0].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "on-target sites (distance == 0)", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Filter 3: require 23-nt sequences
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    mask_len = (df["offtarget_sequence"].astype(str).str.len() == 23) & (
        df["target"].astype(str).str.len() == 23
    )
    df = df[mask_len].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "non-23-nt sequences", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Extract guide/off-target sequences and PAM
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # offtarget_sequence: 23-nt (spacer + PAM observed at off-target site).
    # target:             23-nt guide+PAM (N in PAM position).
    df["guide_seq"] = df["target"].astype(str).str[:20].str.upper()
    df["offtarget_seq"] = df["offtarget_sequence"].astype(str).str[:20].str.upper()
    df["pam_seq"] = df["offtarget_sequence"].astype(str).str[20:].str.upper()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Normalize chromosome names (already hg38, no liftover needed)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    norm_chroms: list[str] = []
    valid_mask: list[bool] = []
    for c in df["chrom"].astype(str):
        try:
            norm_chroms.append(normalize_chrom(c))
            valid_mask.append(True)
        except ValueError:
            norm_chroms.append("")
            valid_mask.append(False)

    df["chrom"] = norm_chroms
    df = df[valid_mask].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "non-standard contigs", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Coordinates: already hg38 BED (0-based)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    df["start"] = pd.to_numeric(df["chromStart"], errors="coerce").astype("int64")
    df["end"] = pd.to_numeric(df["chromEnd"], errors="coerce").astype("int64")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Cell type: naked DNA from primary human T cells (no per-row annotation)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    df["cell_type"] = "T cells"

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Assemble output
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    df["guide_id"] = df["name"].astype(str)
    df["strand"] = df["strand"].astype(str)
    df["num_mismatches"] = df["distance"].astype("int64")
    df["read_count"] = pd.to_numeric(
        df["CHANGEseq_reads"], errors="coerce"
    ).astype("float64")
    df["dataset"] = "change_seq_lazzarotto2020"
    df["genome_build"] = "hg38"

    out = df[_OUTPUT_COLUMNS].copy()
    for col, dtype in _OUTPUT_DTYPES.items():
        out[col] = out[col].astype(dtype)

    logger.info(
        "CHANGE-seq final: %d rows, %d guides, cells: %s",
        len(out),
        out["guide_id"].nunique(),
        sorted(out["cell_type"].unique()),
    )
    return out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# DeepCRISPR K562 parser  (Chuai 2018, Additional File 7)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def _parse_deepcrispr_twosheets(
    xl: "pd.ExcelFile",
    path: Path,
    src_genome_build: str,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Parse the actual Chuai 2018 two-sheet Excel format.

    Reads 'target' sheet (guide sequences) and 'off-target' sheet, joins
    them on 'Target ID', then applies sequence normalisation, mismatch
    computation, on-target filtering, and hg19→hg38 liftover.

    Parameters
    ----------
    xl : pd.ExcelFile
        Already-opened ExcelFile handle.
    path : Path
        Used only for logging messages.
    src_genome_build : str
        ``"hg19"`` triggers liftover; ``"hg38"`` passes coordinates through.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Standardised off-target table (see module docstring for schema).
    """
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Read target sheet → build Target ID → 20-nt guide_seq map
    # 'Target sgRNA' is 23-nt (spacer + PAM); take first 20-nt.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    df_target = xl.parse("target", engine="openpyxl")
    logger.info(
        "target sheet: %d rows, columns: %s", len(df_target), list(df_target.columns)
    )
    guide_map: dict[str, str] = {
        str(tid): str(seq)[:20].upper()
        for tid, seq in zip(
            df_target["Target ID"].astype(str),
            df_target["Target sgRNA"].astype(str),
        )
    }

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Read off-target sheet
    # Columns: OT-ID, Target ID, Chromosome, Start, End, Strand, OT,
    #          Cleavage Frequency
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    df = xl.parse("off-target", engine="openpyxl")
    n_raw = len(df)
    logger.info(
        "off-target sheet: %d rows, columns: %s", n_raw, list(df.columns)
    )

    # Map guide sequence from target sheet
    df["_guide_str"] = df["Target ID"].astype(str).map(guide_map)

    # Drop rows whose Target ID is not in the target sheet
    n_before = n_raw
    df = df[df["_guide_str"].notna()].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "rows with unknown Target ID", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # OT sequence: 23-nt → spacer (20-nt) + PAM (3-nt or "NNN")
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    df["_ot_str"] = df["OT"].astype(str).str.strip().str.upper()
    n_before = len(df)
    mask_len = df["_ot_str"].str.len().between(20, 23)
    df = df[mask_len].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "OT sequences outside 20-23 nt range", n_before, len(df))

    df["offtarget_seq"] = df["_ot_str"].str[:20]
    df["guide_seq"] = df["_guide_str"].str[:20]
    df["pam_seq"] = df["_ot_str"].str[20:].where(
        df["_ot_str"].str.len() == 23, other="NNN"
    )

    pam_missing = (df["pam_seq"] == "NNN").sum()
    if pam_missing:
        logger.warning(
            "%d rows have no PAM in OT sequence (pam_seq='NNN'); "
            "feature_matrix.py must fill from genome.",
            pam_missing,
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Mismatch count (no column in source — compute from sequences)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    df["num_mismatches"] = [
        sum(g != o for g, o in zip(gs, os))
        for gs, os in zip(df["guide_seq"], df["offtarget_seq"])
    ]

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Filter: remove on-target sites (0 mismatches)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    df = df[df["num_mismatches"] > 0].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "on-target sites (num_mismatches == 0)", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Coordinates: liftover hg19 → hg38 or pass through
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if src_genome_build.lower() == "hg19":
        logger.info("Lifting over DeepCRISPR coordinates hg19 → hg38 …")
        n_before = len(df)
        lifted_chroms: list[str] = []
        lifted_starts: list[int] = []
        keep_mask: list[bool] = []

        for _, row in df.iterrows():
            result = liftover_hg19_to_hg38(str(row["Chromosome"]), int(row["Start"]))
            if result is None:
                keep_mask.append(False)
                lifted_chroms.append("")
                lifted_starts.append(0)
            else:
                keep_mask.append(True)
                lifted_chroms.append(result[0])
                lifted_starts.append(result[1])

        df["_keep"] = keep_mask
        df["_h38_chrom"] = lifted_chroms
        df["_h38_start"] = lifted_starts
        df = df[df["_keep"]].copy()
        log_filter(logger, "liftover failures", n_before, len(df))

        interval_len = (
            pd.to_numeric(df["End"], errors="coerce")
            - pd.to_numeric(df["Start"], errors="coerce")
        ).astype("int64")
        df["start"] = df["_h38_start"].astype("int64")
        df["end"] = (df["_h38_start"] + interval_len).astype("int64")
        df["chrom"] = df["_h38_chrom"]
    else:
        logger.info("Coordinates already hg38; skipping liftover.")
        df["start"] = pd.to_numeric(df["Start"], errors="coerce").astype("int64")
        df["end"] = pd.to_numeric(df["End"], errors="coerce").astype("int64")
        df["chrom"] = df["Chromosome"].astype(str)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Normalise chromosome names
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    df, valid_mask = _apply_normalise_chrom(df, "chrom")
    df = df[valid_mask].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "non-standard contigs", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Assemble output
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    df["guide_id"] = df["Target ID"].astype(str)
    df["strand"] = df["Strand"].astype(str)
    df["read_count"] = (
        pd.to_numeric(df["Cleavage Frequency"], errors="coerce").astype("float64")
    )
    df["cell_type"] = "K562"
    df["dataset"] = "deepcrispr_k562"
    df["genome_build"] = "hg38"

    out = df[_OUTPUT_COLUMNS].copy()
    for col, dtype in _OUTPUT_DTYPES.items():
        out[col] = out[col].astype(dtype)

    logger.info(
        "DeepCRISPR K562 final: %d rows, %d guides",
        len(out),
        out["guide_id"].nunique(),
    )
    return out


def parse_deepcrispr_k562(
    path: Path = _DEEPCRISPR_RAW,
    *,
    src_genome_build: str = "hg19",
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Parse Additional File 7 from Chuai et al. 2018 (DeepCRISPR, Genome Biology).

    The file aggregates experimentally verified off-target sites for ~30 sgRNAs
    (12 in K562, 18 in HEK293) detected by Digenome-seq, GUIDE-seq, BLESS,
    HTGTS, and IDLV.  This parser extracts **K562 rows only**.

    **POSITIVES ONLY.**  Negatives must be generated via Cas-OFFinder.

    Parameters
    ----------
    path : Path
        Path to the downloaded file.  Accepts ``.xlsx``, ``.xls``, ``.tsv``,
        or ``.csv``.  Default: ``data/raw/chuai2018_deepcrispr_k562.xlsx``.
    src_genome_build : str
        Coordinate system of the source file.  ``"hg19"`` triggers liftover to
        hg38; ``"hg38"`` skips liftover.  Default ``"hg19"`` (data originates
        from Kim 2015 Digenome-seq).  Override with ``"hg38"`` if the file was
        already lifted.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Standardised off-target table (see module docstring for schema).

    Raises
    ------
    FileNotFoundError
        With exact download instructions if the file is missing.
    ValueError
        If a required column cannot be found; message lists actual columns.
    """
    logger.info("=== Parsing DeepCRISPR K562 (Chuai 2018) from %s ===", path)

    if not path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(
            f"DeepCRISPR file not found: {path}\n\n"
            "Download instructions:\n"
            "  1. Open https://genomebiology.biomedcentral.com/articles/"
            "10.1186/s13059-018-1459-4\n"
            "  2. Scroll to 'Additional files' → click 'Additional file 7'.\n"
            "  3. Download the file (CSV or XLSX).\n"
            f"  4. Save as: {path}\n"
            "  Alternate PMC link: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/"
            "PMC6020378/ (Supplementary Material → Additional file 7)\n"
            "\n"
            f"  If coordinates are in hg38, re-run with --deepcrispr-genome-build hg38"
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Load — detect actual Chuai 2018 two-sheet Excel format
    # vs. flat-file / alternate format (backward-compatible fallback)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    sfx = path.suffix.lower()
    if sfx in {".xlsx", ".xls"}:
        _xl = pd.ExcelFile(path, engine="openpyxl")
        if "off-target" in _xl.sheet_names and "target" in _xl.sheet_names:
            logger.info(
                "Detected two-sheet Excel format ('target' + 'off-target'); "
                "using _parse_deepcrispr_twosheets()."
            )
            return _parse_deepcrispr_twosheets(_xl, path, src_genome_build)

    df = _load_tabular(path)
    n_raw = len(df)
    logger.info("Loaded %d rows. Columns: %s", n_raw, list(df.columns))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Filter 1: K562 only (if a cell-type column exists)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    _CT_ALIASES = [
        "Cell_type", "cell_type", "Cell_Type", "CellType",
        "cell_line", "Cell_Line", "Cell", "cell",
    ]
    ct_col = next((c for c in _CT_ALIASES if c in df.columns), None)
    if ct_col is not None:
        n_before = len(df)
        df = df[df[ct_col].astype(str).str.upper().str.contains("K562")].copy()
        log_filter(logger, "non-K562 rows", n_before, len(df))
    else:
        logger.info(
            "No cell-type column found; treating all %d rows as K562.", len(df)
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Column detection
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    col_guide_id = _pick_col(df, [
        "Target", "target", "Guide_Name", "guide_name", "sgRNA", "Name",
        "name", "Target_Name", "Guide", "guide", "ID",
    ], "guide_id")

    col_guide_seq = _pick_col(df, [
        "Target_Sequence", "target_sequence", "guide_seq", "Guide_Sequence",
        "GuideSeq", "protospacer", "sgRNA_seq",
    ], "guide_seq")

    col_ot_seq = _pick_col(df, [
        "Offtarget_Sequence", "Off_Target_Sequence", "offtarget_seq",
        "OT_Sequence", "OT_seq", "off_target_seq", "off_target",
        "OffTarget_Sequence",
    ], "offtarget_seq")

    col_chrom = _pick_col(df, [
        "Chromosome", "#Chromosome", "chrom", "chr", "Chr", "CHROM",
        "chromosome",
    ], "chrom")

    col_start = _pick_col(df, [
        "Start", "start", "chromStart", "POS", "pos",
    ], "start")

    col_end = _pick_col(df, ["End", "end", "chromEnd"], "end")

    col_strand = _pick_col(df, ["Strand", "strand", "STRAND"], "strand")

    col_freq = _pick_col(df, [
        "Cleavage_Frequency", "cleavage_frequency", "CleaveFreq",
        "cleavage_freq", "Frequency", "frequency", "Score", "score",
        "Label", "label", "Cleave_Freq", "cleavage",
    ], "cleavage_frequency / score")

    # Mismatch count is optional; computed from sequences if absent
    _MM_ALIASES = [
        "Mismatches", "mismatches", "num_mismatches", "n_mismatches",
        "Mismatch_count", "distance", "#Mismatches",
    ]
    col_mm = next((c for c in _MM_ALIASES if c in df.columns), None)
    if col_mm is None:
        logger.info(
            "No mismatch column found; will compute from guide/offtarget sequences."
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Filter 2: drop rows with missing off-target sequence
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    df = df[df[col_ot_seq].notna()].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "rows with missing offtarget_seq", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Sequence normalisation: 20-nt spacer + 3-nt PAM (or "NNN" placeholder)
    # Sequences may be 20-nt (spacer only) or 23-nt (spacer + PAM).
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    df["_ot_str"] = df[col_ot_seq].astype(str).str.strip().str.upper()
    df["_guide_str"] = df[col_guide_seq].astype(str).str.strip().str.upper()

    n_before = len(df)
    mask_len = (
        df["_ot_str"].str.len().between(20, 23)
        & df["_guide_str"].str.len().between(20, 23)
    )
    df = df[mask_len].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "sequences outside 20-23 nt range", n_before, len(df))

    df["offtarget_seq"] = df["_ot_str"].str[:20]
    df["guide_seq"] = df["_guide_str"].str[:20]
    # "NNN" when PAM not in source; feature_matrix.py must fill from genome
    df["pam_seq"] = df["_ot_str"].str[20:].where(
        df["_ot_str"].str.len() == 23, other="NNN"
    )

    pam_missing = (df["pam_seq"] == "NNN").sum()
    if pam_missing:
        logger.warning(
            "%d rows have no PAM in source sequence (pam_seq='NNN'); "
            "CFD scoring will require genome lookup in feature_matrix.py.",
            pam_missing,
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Mismatch count
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if col_mm is not None:
        df["num_mismatches"] = (
            pd.to_numeric(df[col_mm], errors="coerce").fillna(-1).astype("int64")
        )
    else:
        df["num_mismatches"] = [
            sum(g != o for g, o in zip(gs, os))
            for gs, os in zip(df["guide_seq"], df["offtarget_seq"])
        ]

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Filter 3: remove on-target sites (0 mismatches)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    df = df[df["num_mismatches"] > 0].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "on-target sites (num_mismatches == 0)", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Coordinates: liftover hg19 → hg38 or pass through hg38 as-is
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if src_genome_build.lower() == "hg19":
        logger.info("Lifting over DeepCRISPR coordinates hg19 → hg38 …")
        n_before = len(df)
        lifted_chroms: list[str] = []
        lifted_starts: list[int] = []
        keep_mask: list[bool] = []

        for _, row in df.iterrows():
            result = liftover_hg19_to_hg38(str(row[col_chrom]), int(row[col_start]))
            if result is None:
                keep_mask.append(False)
                lifted_chroms.append("")
                lifted_starts.append(0)
            else:
                keep_mask.append(True)
                lifted_chroms.append(result[0])
                lifted_starts.append(result[1])

        df["_keep"] = keep_mask
        df["_h38_chrom"] = lifted_chroms
        df["_h38_start"] = lifted_starts
        df = df[df["_keep"]].copy()
        log_filter(logger, "liftover failures", n_before, len(df))

        interval_len = (
            pd.to_numeric(df[col_end], errors="coerce")
            - pd.to_numeric(df[col_start], errors="coerce")
        ).astype("int64")
        df["start"] = df["_h38_start"].astype("int64")
        df["end"] = (df["_h38_start"] + interval_len).astype("int64")
        df["chrom"] = df["_h38_chrom"]
    else:
        logger.info("Coordinates already hg38; skipping liftover.")
        df["start"] = pd.to_numeric(df[col_start], errors="coerce").astype("int64")
        df["end"] = pd.to_numeric(df[col_end], errors="coerce").astype("int64")
        df["chrom"] = df[col_chrom].astype(str)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Normalise chromosome names
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    n_before = len(df)
    df, valid_mask = _apply_normalise_chrom(df, "chrom")
    df = df[valid_mask].copy()
    log_filter(logger, "non-standard contigs", n_before, len(df))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Assemble output
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    df["guide_id"] = df[col_guide_id].astype(str)
    df["strand"] = df[col_strand].astype(str)
    df["read_count"] = pd.to_numeric(df[col_freq], errors="coerce").astype("float64")
    df["cell_type"] = "K562"
    df["dataset"] = "deepcrispr_k562"
    df["genome_build"] = "hg38"

    out = df[_OUTPUT_COLUMNS].copy()
    for col, dtype in _OUTPUT_DTYPES.items():
        out[col] = out[col].astype(dtype)

    logger.info(
        "DeepCRISPR K562 final: %d rows, %d guides",
        len(out),
        out["guide_id"].nunique(),
    )
    return out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# DISCOVER-seq K562 parser  (Wienert 2019, hg38)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def _parse_coord_string(coord: str) -> tuple[str, int, int]:
    """Parse 'chr6:43770822-43770841' → ('chr6', 43770822, 43770841).

    Parameters
    ----------
    coord : str
        Coordinate string in the format ``<chrom>:<start>-<end>``.

    Returns
    -------
    tuple[str, int, int]
        (chrom, start, end) as parsed from the string.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If the string cannot be parsed.
    """
    try:
        chrom, rest = coord.split(":", 1)
        start_s, end_s = rest.split("-", 1)
        return chrom.strip(), int(start_s.strip()), int(end_s.strip())
    except Exception as exc:
        raise ValueError(f"Cannot parse coordinate string {coord!r}: {exc}") from exc


def _parse_discover_sheet(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    guide_id: str,
    coord_col: str,
    strand_col: str,
    pam_col: str,
    guide_seq_col: str,
    mismatches_col: str,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Extract off-target rows from a single DISCOVER-seq sheet.

    Common processing applied to VEGFA, RNF2, and HBB sheets after they
    are read with the correct ``header`` argument.  Handles:

    * NaN separator rows (filter by ``coord_col.notna()``)
    * Coordinate-string parsing (``'chrN:start-end'``)
    * On-target filtering (``mismatches > 0``)
    * Strand normalisation (sense/antisense → +/−)
    * read_count = sum of all numeric columns after the mismatch column

    Parameters
    ----------
    df : pd.DataFrame
        Raw sheet as read by ``pd.read_excel``.
    guide_id : str
        Constant guide identifier assigned to all rows (e.g. ``'VEGFA'``).
    coord_col, strand_col, pam_col, guide_seq_col, mismatches_col : str
        Column names in *df*.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Rows with the standardised output columns populated.
    """
    # Validate required columns
    for col in [coord_col, strand_col, pam_col, guide_seq_col, mismatches_col]:
        if col not in df.columns:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Required column '{col}' not found in DISCOVER-seq sheet "
                f"(guide_id='{guide_id}').\n"
                f"  Actual columns: {sorted(df.columns)}"
            )

    # Capture rep column names NOW before any new columns are added.
    # rep cols = all columns after the mismatches column in the original sheet.
    mm_idx = df.columns.tolist().index(mismatches_col)
    rep_cols = list(df.columns[mm_idx + 1:])

    # Drop NaN / separator rows (coord_col is NaN in separator rows)
    n_before = len(df)
    df = df[df[coord_col].notna()].copy()
    df = df[df[coord_col].astype(str).str.contains(":", na=False)].copy()
    log_filter(logger, f"{guide_id} separator / non-coordinate rows", n_before, len(df))

    if df.empty:
        # Return empty DataFrame with correct columns populated
        for col in ["chrom", "start", "end", "guide_seq", "num_mismatches",
                    "offtarget_seq", "pam_seq", "strand", "read_count",
                    "guide_id", "cell_type", "dataset", "genome_build"]:
            df[col] = pd.Series(dtype=object)
        return df

    # Parse coordinate string → chrom, start, end
    parsed = df[coord_col].astype(str).apply(
        lambda s: _parse_coord_string(s) if ":" in s else (None, None, None)
    )
    df["chrom"] = [t[0] for t in parsed]
    df["start"] = pd.to_numeric([t[1] for t in parsed], errors="coerce")
    df["end"] = pd.to_numeric([t[2] for t in parsed], errors="coerce")

    # Drop rows with unparseable coordinates
    n_before = len(df)
    df = df[df["chrom"].notna() & df["start"].notna() & df["end"].notna()].copy()
    log_filter(logger, f"{guide_id} unparseable coordinates", n_before, len(df))

    # Mismatch count (needed before guide_seq extraction below)
    df["num_mismatches"] = (
        pd.to_numeric(df[mismatches_col], errors="coerce").fillna(-1).astype("int64")
    )

    # Guide sequence: extract from the on-target row (mismatches == 0) BEFORE
    # filtering it.  The "Guide sequence" column in DISCOVER-seq S1 contains the
    # GENOMIC sequence at each locus (i.e. the off-target sequence with mismatches),
    # NOT the guide RNA itself.  The on-target row has 0 mismatches, so its
    # "Guide sequence" value IS the true 20-nt protospacer.
    on_target_rows = df[df["num_mismatches"] == 0]
    if len(on_target_rows) > 0:
        guide_seq_value: str = (
            on_target_rows[guide_seq_col]
            .astype(str).str.strip().str.upper().str[:20]
            .iloc[0]
        )
        logger.info(
            "%s: extracted guide_seq '%s' from on-target row (mismatches==0)",
            guide_id, guide_seq_value,
        )
    else:
        guide_seq_value = "N" * 20
        logger.warning(
            "%s: no on-target row found; guide_seq set to placeholder N×20", guide_id
        )

    # Off-target sequence: the "Guide sequence" column holds the genomic sequence
    # at each off-target locus (20 nt, same strand as the guide).
    df["offtarget_seq"] = (
        df[guide_seq_col].astype(str).str.strip().str.upper().str[:20]
    )

    # Filter on-target rows (mismatches == 0) — MUST come AFTER guide_seq extraction
    n_before = len(df)
